**Definition (learn):** A clean procedural history can leave persons born into unchosen disadvantage without a defensible share; fair procedure should be judged partly by whether inequalities benefit the least advantaged (difference principle) (✅ owner 03 §15; ⚠️ analytical synthesis; Gauba PDF pp.434-435, 443 as cross-reference).

**Breakdown:**
- Rawls is named in the owner as a critic of utilitarianism who accommodates substantive concerns within procedure (✅ owner 03 §15).
- Difference principle as the reply (⚠️ owner synthesis).
- Full architecture is in the justice file (owner 03 §15).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.434-435, 443 (owner-cited, cross-owner).

**Links:** PT-1079; PT-1090; PT-1093.

**Trap:** Treat the difference principle as the reply, not as Nozick's own position.

**Mains hook:** A procedurally clean history can still fail the least advantaged; that is the core of the welfare-liberal reply.

**Back:** *"RAWLS REPLIES: CLEAN HISTORY is NOT ENOUGH; check the LEAST ADVANTAGED."*

### Flashcard PT-1081 — The neoliberal knowledge argument

**Front:** What is the neoliberal knowledge argument?

**Definition (learn):** Governments cannot possess sufficient knowledge of dispersed individual needs and circumstances to plan an economy; needs are coordinated through unpredictable transactions in a free market (✅ owner 03 §13; Gauba PDF pp.42-43).

**Breakdown:**
- Informational core of the rollback case (owner 03 advanced Unit 3).
- Spontaneous order (PT-1083).
- Limit: a planning-scale problem does not automatically apply to every welfare instrument (owner 03 §13).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.42-43 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1077; PT-1083; PT-1082.

**Trap:** Treating the knowledge argument as proof against every state welfare action.

**Mains hook:** Dispersed knowledge is a strong objection to central planning and a weaker objection to targeted public services.

**Back:** *"KNOWLEDGE ARGUMENT: the STATE cannot KNOW what MARKETS discover."*

### Flashcard PT-1082 — The neoliberal coercion argument

**Front:** What is the neoliberal coercion argument against redistribution?

**Definition (learn):** Forced or compulsory redistribution is itself coercion that curtails freedom without reliably satisfying wants, even when intended to enlarge welfare (✅ owner 03 §13; Gauba PDF pp.42-43).

**Breakdown:**
- Coercion is located in state transfers.
- Owner notes the framing is value-laden: market-generated deprivation is treated as neutral (⚠️ owner 03 §13).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.42-43 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1081; PT-1078; PT-1090.

**Trap:** Calling redistribution coercion while treating market-generated deprivation as neutral is a contested framing (owner 03 §13).

**Mains hook:** Ask whether coercion is measured from a neutral baseline, which is the welfare liberal's challenge.

**Back:** *"COERCION ARGUMENT: TAX IS FORCE, MARKET DEPRIVATION is ASSUMED NEUTRAL."*

### Flashcard PT-1083 — Spontaneous order (concept)

**Front:** What is spontaneous order in neoliberal thought?

**Definition (learn):** Human needs and coordination emerge through unpredictable transactions in a free society and market, not through centralised state knowledge (✅ owner 03 advanced §4; Gauba PDF p.43).

**Breakdown:**
- Coordination is emergent, not designed.
- Linked to Hayek (PT-1077).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.43 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1077; PT-1081; PT-1082.

**Trap:** Spontaneous order is not anarchy; it is a claim about how coordination emerges.

**Mains hook:** The concept carries the market-democracy claim (Gauba reports neoliberals say markets maximise choice).

**Back:** *"SPONTANEOUS ORDER = COORDINATION that NOBODY DESIGNS."*

### Flashcard PT-1084 — Gauba's critique: bourgeois bias and unequal group voice

**Front:** What critique does Gauba make of liberalism, and what example does he give?

**Definition (learn):** Liberalism is tied to capitalism, often reproduces bourgeois power and underestimates unequal organisation of interests; representative institutions may not register all interests (✅ owner 03 §7, advanced §3; Gauba PDF pp.43-44).

**Breakdown:**
- Example: concentrated business interests versus diffuse consumers (✅ owner 03 §7).
- Structural illustration from the book's period, not a current Indian measurement (owner 03 Unit 5 limitation).
- Welfare concessions may stabilise the status quo (✅ owner 03 advanced §7).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.43-44 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1076; PT-1085; PT-1091.

**Trap:** Do not cite the business-versus-consumers example as a present-day Indian data point.

**Mains hook:** Formal representation does not guarantee equal voice.

**Back:** *"GAUBA'S CRITIQUE: BUSINESS SPEAKS LOUD, CONSUMERS are DIFFUSE."*

### Flashcard PT-1085 — Individualism and utilitarianism in early liberalism

**Front:** How do individualism and utilitarianism relate in early liberal thought?

**Definition (learn):** Early liberal thought develops both rights-bearing individualism and happiness-maximising utilitarianism, though Mill pulls utilitarianism toward liberty and welfare reform (✅ owner 03 §5; Gauba PDF p.42).

**Breakdown:**
- Individualism: rights-based.
- Utilitarianism: greatest happiness (PT-1073).
- Mill's pull toward liberty (PT-1074).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.42 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1073; PT-1074.

**Trap:** Do not present utilitarianism and individualism as identical.

**Mains hook:** Two roots of liberalism explain its internal divisions.

**Back:** *"TWO ROOTS: RIGHTS (individual) and HAPPINESS (utility)."*

### Flashcard PT-1086 — Classical, welfare and neoliberal: what makes liberty real?

**Front:** What single question separates the three liberal strands, and what does each answer fear most?

**Definition (learn):** What conditions make liberty real? Classical: state coercion. Welfare: unmet social need and deprivation. Neoliberal: the redistributive state itself (⚠️ owner 03 §7, §18 synthesis).

**Breakdown:**
- Classical: minimal state.
- Welfare: positive state.
- Neoliberal: rollback of the welfare state.
- Owner comparison table: state, property, equality, liberty, welfare (✅ owner 03 §8).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.41-43 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1066; PT-1067; PT-1069; PT-1064.

**Trap:** Equality rows in the owner's table are analytical (⚠️), not Gauba's text.

**Mains hook:** Use the one-question frame for any compare question.

**Back:** *"CLASSICAL FEARS THE STATE; WELFARE FEARS DEPRIVATION; NEOLIBERAL FEARS THE WELFARE STATE."*

### Flashcard PT-1087 — Trap: liberalism is always anti-state

**Front:** Why is "liberalism always means anti-state politics" wrong?

**Definition (learn):** Gauba distinguishes classical negative liberalism from welfare positive liberalism, and the chapter's point is that liberalism is internally divided on state action (✅ owner 03 §9, advanced §11).

**Breakdown:**
- Welfare liberalism wants positive state action (PT-1067).
- Liberalism is a family, not a single state theory.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.41-42 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1066; PT-1067.

**Trap:** Do not portray all liberalism as anti-state.

**Mains hook:** Start with the family thesis to avoid the error.

**Back:** *"NOT ALL LIBERALS FEAR THE STATE: welfare liberals WANT IT."*

### Flashcard PT-1088 — Trap: neoliberalism and liberalism are identical

**Front:** Why is "neoliberalism and liberalism are identical" wrong?

**Definition (learn):** Neoliberalism is a twentieth-century restoration of laissez-faire within the wider liberal family, responding to an already-existing welfare state (✅ owner 03 §9; Gauba PDF pp.42-43).

**Breakdown:**
- Restoration, not origin.
- Contrast with welfare liberalism (PT-1067).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.42-43 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1069; PT-1067.

**Trap:** Mistaking neoliberalism for "all of liberalism".

**Mains hook:** Neoliberalism is a rollback movement within liberalism.

**Back:** *"NEOLIBERAL = LIBERAL ROLLBACK, not LIBERALISM ITSELF."*

### Flashcard PT-1089 — Trap: Hayek, Friedman and Nozick as one doctrine

**Front:** Why are Hayek, Friedman and Nozick not one identical doctrine?

**Definition (learn):** Gauba groups them for rollback; their arguments remain distinct: Hayekian spontaneous order, Friedmanite market freedom, Nozickian entitlement and minimal state (✅ owner 03 §9, advanced §12).

**Breakdown:**
- Hayek: knowledge and spontaneous order (PT-1077).
- Friedman: market choice (PT-1078).
- Nozick: entitlement (PT-1079).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.42 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1077; PT-1078; PT-1079.

**Trap:** Grouping them without naming each argument.

**Mains hook:** Name each route to the limited-government conclusion.

**Back:** *"THREE NAMES, THREE ROUTES: order, choice, entitlement."*

### Flashcard PT-1090 — Trap: Nozick is indifferent to injustice

**Front:** Why is "Nozick's minimal state is indifferent to injustice" a trap?

**Definition (learn):** Nozick's framework requires rectification of unjust historical acquisitions and transfers; a Nozickian answer defends only distributions traceable to just acquisition and transfer (✅ owner 03 §14; ✅ SEP, fetched).

**Breakdown:**
- Rectification principle (PT-1079).
- Historical facts needed to trigger rectification are hard to establish (owner 03 §14).
- Clean procedure does not guarantee least-advantaged benefit (PT-1080).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** SEP entry (fetched); owner 03 §14.

**Links:** PT-1079; PT-1080.

**Trap:** Presenting Nozick as a defender of any existing property distribution.

**Mains hook:** Nozick's own framework is conditional, not an automatic defence of the status quo.

**Back:** *"NOZICK ≠ ANY-DISTRIBUTION: RECTIFY the UNJUST."*

### Flashcard PT-1091 — Indian mixed economy: a conceptual lens only

**Front:** How can the liberal strands be applied conceptually to India's post-Independence economy, and what must not be claimed?

**Definition (learn):** India's planned mixed economy can be read as broadly analogous to welfare liberalism; later market reform as a partial move toward rollback liberalism (⚠️ owner 03 §16 conceptual application).

**Breakdown:**
- Conceptual only: pair with Economy-file dates, policy names and outcomes.
- No reform year, GDP figure or scheme name is asserted in the owner.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted here (owner 03 §16 caution).

**Links:** PT-1067; PT-1084; PT-1068.

**Trap:** Citing a reform year or figure from this file.

**Mains hook:** Use the lens to ask whether a reform widened or narrowed the state's role in securing conditions of liberty, then add verified Indian facts.

**Back:** *"INDIA AS LENS: planning = welfare-liberal balance; reform = partial rollback."*

### Flashcard PT-1092 — T.H. Green versus Laski and Tawney (analytical split)

**Front:** Why might an answer distinguish Green and Hobhouse's "new liberalism" from Laski and Tawney's welfare state?

**Definition (learn):** Green's moral argument (liberty needs conditions) is distinct from the fuller institutional welfare state of Laski and Tawney (⚠️ owner 03 §12.2 analytical refinement).

**Breakdown:**
- Moral-philosophical strand: Green, Hobhouse.
- Institutional strand: Laski, Tawney.
- The "New Liberalism" label is not Gauba's own (⚠️ owner 03 §22).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.41 (✅ owner 03).

**Links:** PT-1075; PT-1076.

**Trap:** Attributing the label "New Liberalism" to Gauba's text.

**Mains hook:** Use the split to show chronology and argument in an answer.

**Back:** *"GREEN = MORAL CASE; LASKI/TAWNEY = INSTITUTIONS."*

### Flashcard PT-1093 — Answer architecture for liberalism (qualified thesis)

**Front:** What is the qualified thesis for a liberalism question?

**Definition (learn):** Liberalism is an internally divided family united by the priority of individual liberty and opposition to arbitrary authority; its strands disagree on which conditions make liberty real and which threat is most dangerous (✅ owner 03 §17; ⚠️ owner synthesis).

**Breakdown:**
- "Distinguish": classical, welfare, neoliberal.
- "Critically examine": knowledge and coercion arguments against Rawlsian reply.
- 10 marks: two strands and one unit each; 20 marks: four or five units, two chains, India lens as ⚠️ value-addition.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner directive table (✅ owner 03 §17, §21).

**Links:** PT-1086; PT-1080; PT-1070.

**Trap:** Treating liberalism as one undifferentiated economic doctrine.

**Mains hook:** Verdict: contested economic programme, durable constitutional core.

**Back:** *"LIBERALISM: ONE LIBERTY, THREE FEARS, a CONSTITUTIONAL CORE."*

### GROUP — 04 Marxism and Neo-Marxism

### Flashcard PT-1094 — Marxism (definition)

**Front:** How does Gauba define Marxism?

**Definition (learn):** A set of political and economic principles founded by Marx and Engels to give socialism a scientific basis through historical analysis of conflict between antagonistic classes (✅ owner 04 §3; Gauba PDF p.47).

**Breakdown:**
- Scientific socialism: a historical method, not a utopia (✅ owner 04 advanced §4).
- Gauba opens by calling Marxism a living tradition, not owned by Marx alone (PDF p.45, PT-1121 context).
- The term was unknown in Marx's lifetime; Engels reports "I am not a Marxist" (✅ owner 04 advanced §3).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.45, 47 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1095; PT-1096; PT-1104.

**Trap:** Marxism is not simply "Marx's personal doctrine"; the owner treats it as a tradition with further possibilities.

**Mains hook:** Define Marxism as scientific socialism rooted in the analysis of class conflict over the means of production.

**Back:** *"MARXISM = SCIENTIFIC SOCIALISM: class conflict read through history."*

### Flashcard PT-1095 — Classical Marxism versus neo-Marxism

**Front:** What separates classical Marxism from neo-Marxism?

**Definition (learn):** Classical Marxism centres on property, class division, contradiction and revolution; neo-Marxism widens the lens to ideology, culture, alienation and new forms of domination (✅ owner 04 §5, §8; Gauba PDF pp.47, 53-54).

**Breakdown:**
- Classical: economy-centred, base-determined explanation.
- Neo: interdependence of base and superstructure; alienation; Frankfurt School; Althusser; dependency (PT-1107 to PT-1111).
- Neo-Marxism is a development within Marxism (owner 04 §9).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.47, 53-54 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1094; PT-1107; PT-1109; PT-1110; PT-1111.

**Trap:** Treating neo-Marxism as abandoning Marxism (PT-1116).

**Mains hook:** The real contrast is between economism and a layered model of domination (PT-1118).

**Back:** *"CLASSICAL = ECONOMY and CLASS; NEO = IDEOLOGY, CULTURE, DOMINATION."*

### Flashcard PT-1096 — Dialectical materialism

**Front:** What is dialectical materialism?

**Definition (learn):** The view that matter, not idea, is fundamental, and that contradictions in material conditions drive development (✅ owner 04 §3; Gauba PDF p.48).

**Breakdown:**
- Philosophical basis of Marxism (PT-1097 for the historical application).
- Gauba attributes three laws of dialectical materialism to Engels (✅ owner 04 advanced §5).
- Matter is primary; contradiction drives change (✅ owner 04 advanced §4).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.48 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1097; PT-1100.

**Trap:** Do not present the thesis-antithesis-synthesis formula as Marx's own (PT-1114).

**Mains hook:** Dialectical materialism is the philosophy; historical materialism is its application to society.

**Back:** *"DIALECTICAL = MATTER FIRST, CONTRADICTION DRIVES CHANGE."*

### Flashcard PT-1097 — Historical materialism

**Front:** What does historical materialism claim, and what is the precise strength of its claim in the owner?

**Definition (learn):** Economic relations of production, distribution and exchange play an important role in shaping wider social, political, intellectual and ethical life (✅ owner 04 §3; Gauba PDF p.48).

**Breakdown:**
- Owner records the wording as "play important role in shaping", not "decisive role".
- Application of dialectical materialism to society (PT-1096).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.48 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1096; PT-1099; PT-1115.

**Trap:** Do not upgrade the claim to "the decisive role" (owner 04 §12).

**Mains hook:** Historical materialism explains a strong influence, not a mechanical determination.

**Back:** *"HISTORICAL MATERIALISM = ECONOMY SHAPES LIFE, not DECIDES EVERYTHING."*

### Flashcard PT-1098 — Surplus value

**Front:** What is surplus value, and how does Gauba illustrate it?

**Definition (learn):** The unpaid part of labour appropriated by the capitalist as profit, rent and interest (✅ owner 04 §3; Gauba PDF pp.52-53).

**Breakdown:**
- Economic proof of exploitation (✅ owner 04 advanced §3 step 6).
- Illustration: the wage relation (PT-1113).
- Not any change in sale price (PT-1117).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.52-53 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1113; PT-1117; PT-1094.

**Trap:** Surplus value is not equal to any profit or market price change (PT-1117).

**Mains hook:** Surplus value is the economic mechanism of exploitation; alienation is its human counterpart.

**Back:** *"SURPLUS VALUE = UNPAID LABOUR taken as PROFIT, RENT, INTEREST."*

### Flashcard PT-1099 — Base and superstructure

**Front:** What are the base and superstructure in historical materialism?

**Definition (learn):** The base is the mode of production; the superstructure includes law, politics, religion, morals, art and culture (✅ owner 04 §5; Gauba PDF p.48).

**Breakdown:**
- Base: mode of production.
- Superstructure: law, politics, religion, morals, social practices, literature, art and culture (✅ owner 04 advanced §4).
- Non-mechanical relation: interdependence (⚠️ owner 04 §12; Gauba PDF p.53).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.48, 53 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1097; PT-1118; PT-1110.

**Trap:** Base does not determine superstructure mechanically (PT-1118).

**Mains hook:** Relative autonomy of culture and ideology is the neo-Marxist refinement.

**Back:** *"BASE = PRODUCTION; SUPERSTRUCTURE = LAW, MORALS, CULTURE; INTERDEPENDENT."*

### Flashcard PT-1100 — Forces and relations of production

**Front:** What are the forces and relations of production, and how do they form the base?

**Definition (learn):** Forces of production are means of production plus labour power and technique; relations of production are ownership patterns and class relations; together they form the economic base (✅ owner 04 §5, §12; Gauba PDF pp.48-49).

**Breakdown:**
- Forces: means of production plus labour power.
- Relations: ownership and class.
- Mode of production = both combined.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.48-49 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1101; PT-1096; PT-1099.

**Trap:** Do not treat relations as merely legal titles; the owner stresses class relations too.

**Mains hook:** The tension between forces and relations is the engine of historical change.

**Back:** *"FORCES = MEANS + LABOUR; RELATIONS = OWNERSHIP + CLASS."*

### Flashcard PT-1101 — Fetter and rupture (the revolutionary mechanism)

**Front:** What is the fetter and rupture mechanism in historical materialism?

**Definition (learn):** When forces of production outgrow existing relations of production, the relations become a fetter, and revolution breaks the fetter, replacing one social formation with another (✅ owner 04 §12; Gauba PDF p.49).

**Breakdown:**
- Sequence: slave-owning, feudal, capitalist, socialist/communist (✅ owner 04 §12).
- Owner's mechanism is an analytical synthesis of Gauba's account (⚠️).
- Limit: timing and agency of real revolutions have not matched the schema neatly (owner 04 Unit 2).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.49 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1100; PT-1112; PT-1119.

**Trap:** Presenting the fetter mechanism as a tested law of history.

**Mains hook:** The mechanism is a structural account; the critics ask why contingent politics matter more than the schema predicts.

**Back:** *"FETTER → RUPTURE: relations HOLD BACK forces, REVOLUTION breaks."*

### Flashcard PT-1102 — Private property and antagonistic classes

**Front:** How does private property in the means of production create classes in Marxism?

**Definition (learn):** Private property in the means of production divides society into dominant (owning) and dependent classes with antagonistic interests (✅ owner 04 §13; Gauba PDF p.47).

**Breakdown:**
- Source of class antagonism.
- Drives conflict across historical stages toward a classless resolution (✅ owner 04 §13).
- The state is not taught here; see owner 13 (⚠️ owner 04 §13).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.47, 49-52 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1094; PT-1101; PT-1106.

**Trap:** Do not treat class as only income level; the owner ties class to property ownership.

**Mains hook:** Property is the root of the antagonism that the rest of the theory explains.

**Back:** *"PROPERTY DIVIDES: OWNERS vs DEPENDENTS."*

### Flashcard PT-1103 — The four tenets of classical Marxism

**Front:** What are the four tenets Gauba uses to organise classical Marxism?

**Definition (learn):** Dialectical materialism, historical materialism, class conflict and surplus value (✅ owner 04 §1; Gauba PDF pp.48, 52).

**Breakdown:**
- Philosophy: dialectical materialism (PT-1096).
- Theory of history: historical materialism (PT-1097).
- Class conflict (PT-1102).
- Economic proof: surplus value (PT-1098).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.48, 52 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1096; PT-1097; PT-1102; PT-1098.

**Trap:** Dropping any tenet when answering a classical Marxism question.

**Mains hook:** Map the four tenets as the spine of a classical answer.

**Back:** *"FOUR TENETS: DIALECTICS, HISTORY, CLASS, SURPLUS."*

### Flashcard PT-1104 — Marx and Engels

**Front:** What roles do Marx and Engels play in the owner's account?

**Definition (learn):** Founders of Marxism and scientific socialism who treat history as struggle rooted in the mode of production (✅ owner 04 §6; Gauba PDF p.47).

**Breakdown:**
- Engels supplies the three laws of dialectical materialism and helps edit later volumes of Capital (✅ owner 04 advanced §5).
- Engels's 1893 letter gives "false consciousness" (PT-1040, fetched).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.47-48 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1094; PT-1096; PT-1040.

**Trap:** Attributing Engels's phrase to Marx.

**Mains hook:** Name both founders precisely and attribute each claim.

**Back:** *"MARX and ENGELS: the FOUNDERS of SCIENTIFIC SOCIALISM."*

### Flashcard PT-1105 — Lenin, Luxemburg and Mao (classical exponents)

**Front:** Who are the named exponents of classical Marxism beyond Marx and Engels?

**Definition (learn):** Lenin, Rosa Luxemburg and Mao Zedong (✅ owner 04 §6, advanced §5; Gauba PDF p.47).

**Breakdown:**
- Named as chief exponents of classical Marxism.
- Lenin's ideology view: PT-1041.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.47 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1104; PT-1041.

**Trap:** Treating all three as identical on ideology.

**Mains hook:** Use the three names to show the classical tradition's plurality.

**Back:** *"LENIN, LUXEMBURG, MAO: CLASSICAL EXPONENTS."*

### Flashcard PT-1106 — Young Marx and alienation

**Front:** What is the "Young Marx" or humanist strain, and what does it contribute?

**Definition (learn):** The early humanist Marx on alienation, freedom and capitalism's dehumanising effects (✅ owner 04 §6; Gauba PDF pp.47, 54).

**Breakdown:**
- Gauba names the *Economic and Philosophic Manuscripts of 1844* without enumerating alienation's forms (✅ owner 04 §13).
- Marx's "Estranged Labour" manuscript distinguishes estrangement from product, activity, species-being and other persons (⚠️ owner 04 §13 cites marxists.org; not fetched by this agent).
- Full doctrine in owner Philosophy file (owner 04 §13).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1844 manuscripts (✅ owner 04 cited Gauba).

**Links:** PT-1102; PT-1108; PT-1109.

**Trap:** Do not present the fourfold alienation as Gauba's own enumeration.

**Mains hook:** Alienation shows exploitation's human cost; surplus value its economic mechanism.

**Back:** *"YOUNG MARX: ALIENATION = CAPITALISM DEHUMANISES WORKERS."*

### Flashcard PT-1107 — Frankfurt School (critical theory)

**Front:** Who are the Frankfurt School figures Gauba names, and what do they extend?

**Definition (learn):** Adorno, Horkheimer, Marcuse and Habermas extend Marxism through critical theory and ideology critique (✅ owner 04 §6; Gauba PDF p.53).

**Breakdown:**
- Habermas generation caveat: Stanford Encyclopedia places Habermas in the second generation (⚠️ owner 04 §14.2; SEP not fetched by this agent).
- Adorno, Horkheimer and Marcuse as first-generation figures (⚠️ same source).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.53 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1037 (critical theory); PT-1108; PT-1095.

**Trap:** Gauba does not give a generational sequence; the caveat is external.

**Mains hook:** The Frankfurt School moves the critique from factory to culture and communication.

**Back:** *"FRANKFURT: ADORNO, HORKHEIMER, MARCUSE, HABERMAS: CRITIQUE beyond the FACTORY."*

### Flashcard PT-1108 — Herbert Marcuse: one-dimensional humanity

**Front:** What does Marcuse argue about advanced bourgeois society?

**Definition (learn):** Advanced bourgeois society reduces human beings to one-dimensionality by anaesthetising discontent through manipulated desires (✅ owner 04 §6; Gauba PDF p.54).

**Breakdown:**
- Extends domination from factory into culture and consumption (✅ owner 04 §18 Unit 4).
- Limitation: can understate direct economic exploitation (owner 04 Unit 4).
- Marcuse's measurement warning is in PT-1020.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.54 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1107; PT-1020; PT-1110.

**Trap:** One-dimensionality is not the claim that workers are uninformed; it is about manipulated desire.

**Mains hook:** Culture and consumption can reproduce domination without coercion.

**Back:** *"MARCUSE: ONE-DIMENSIONAL, DESIRE MANAGED, DISCONTENT ANAESTHETISED."*

### Flashcard PT-1109 — Louis Althusser and the scientific strain

**Front:** What does Althusser contribute to neo-Marxism?

**Definition (learn):** He represents the scientific strain, resisting purely humanist Marxism and analysing deep social structures, especially modes of production (✅ owner 04 §6, §14.3; Gauba PDF p.54).

**Breakdown:**
- Scientific versus humanist: a direction contrast (✅ owner 04 advanced §4).
- Balances Marcuse's humanism (PT-1108).
- Limitation: can drain agency and consciousness if pushed to structure alone (owner 04 Unit 5).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.54 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1108; PT-1095; PT-1099.

**Trap:** Althusser's structuralism is not the same as Marcuse's humanism.

**Mains hook:** Structure without agency and agency without structure are the two failure modes.

**Back:** *"ALTHUSSER: STRUCTURES over CONSCIOUSNESS, the SCIENTIFIC strain."*

### Flashcard PT-1110 — Antonio Gramsci and hegemony

**Front:** What does Gramsci add to the Marxist account of capitalist rule?

**Definition (learn):** Hegemony, civil society and structures of legitimation explain why capitalist rule survives through consent as well as coercion (✅ owner 04 §14.1; Gauba PDF pp.306, 309-311, cross-referenced from the power chapter).

**Breakdown:**
- Relative autonomy of culture and civil society (owner 04 §14.1).
- Cross-owner citation: pages come from Gauba's power chapter, not from the Marxism chapter (owner 04 §21).
- Owner: this is a genuine neo-Marxist departure.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.306, 309-311 (cross-owner, ✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1099; PT-1108; PT-1140 (Gramsci on fascism); PT-1109.

**Trap:** "Hegemony explains everything" loses analytical precision (owner 04 Unit 3).

**Mains hook:** Consent explains stability that coercion alone cannot.

**Back:** *"GRAMSCI: CONSENT, not just COERCION, keeps CAPITALISM standing."*

### Flashcard PT-1111 — Dependency theory versus world-systems (classification caution)

**Front:** What does Gauba say about dependency theorists, and why is "dependency" not a synonym for "world-system"?

**Definition (learn):** Gauba names dependency theorists as extending the axis of struggle to developing nations confronting colonialism and neo-colonialism (✅ owner 04 §14.4; Gauba PDF p.54). Dependency theory and world-systems theory are related but distinct (⚠️ owner 04 analytical caution).

**Breakdown:**
- Dependency: structural dependence of periphery on core.
- World-systems: one integrated global hierarchy of core, periphery and semi-periphery across long time (⚠️ owner 04 §14.4).
- Gauba uses only the "dependency theorists" label.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.54 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1094; PT-1095; PT-1118.

**Trap:** Attributing a world-systems framework or named theorist to Gauba's text.

**Mains hook:** Use dependency to extend Marxist logic to international inequality, citing verified Indian trade facts separately.

**Back:** *"DEPENDENCY: PERIPHERY DEPENDS on the CORE; not the same as WORLD-SYSTEMS."*

### Flashcard PT-1112 — The stage chart: slave, feudal, capitalist, socialist

**Front:** What does Gauba's stage chart show about modes of production?

**Definition (learn):** Different modes of production generate different social formations: slave-owning, feudal, capitalist, socialist and communist (✅ owner 04 §7; Gauba PDF pp.49-50).

**Breakdown:**
- Stage sequence applies the fetter mechanism (PT-1101).
- Class struggle continues from slave society to feudalism to capitalism; socialism ends the cycle in a classless society (✅ owner 04 §4).
- Owner notes the stage chart is an illustration.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.49-52 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1101; PT-1102; PT-1119.

**Trap:** Presenting the stage sequence as an empirically tested law.

**Mains hook:** The stage chart is the historical scaffold; critics test it against actual revolutions.

**Back:** *"SLAVE → FEUDAL → CAPITALIST → SOCIALIST → COMMUNIST: each stage breaks the last."*

### Flashcard PT-1113 — The wage relation as an example of surplus value

**Front:** How does the wage relation illustrate surplus value?

**Definition (learn):** The worker produces more value than the subsistence wage returns; the unpaid residue becomes capitalist profit (✅ owner 04 §7; Gauba PDF pp.52-53).

**Breakdown:**
- Labour produces more than the wage pays for.
- The residue is profit, rent and interest.
- Safe answer contrast: direct workplace exploitation versus ideological consent through culture and media (⚠️ owner 04 §7).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.52-53 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1098; PT-1108; PT-1110.

**Trap:** Confusing wage with value produced.

**Mains hook:** The wage relation is the simplest route to exploitation; consent is the layer that keeps it stable.

**Back:** *"WAGE < VALUE PRODUCED; THE GAP IS SURPLUS."*

### Flashcard PT-1114 — Trap: Marx's thesis-antithesis-synthesis

**Front:** Why should you avoid presenting thesis-antithesis-synthesis as Marx's own formula?

**Definition (learn):** The triad is a teaching shorthand from Gauba's discussion of Hegel's dialectic, not Marx's settled formula (⚠️ owner 04 §9, §12; Gauba PDF p.48).

**Breakdown:**
- Correct source: Hegelian dialectic as the background.
- Use only as pedagogic shorthand.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.48 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1096; PT-1097.

**Trap:** Presenting the triad as Marx's canonical formula.

**Mains hook:** Attribute the dialectic to its source before using it.

**Back:** *"TRIAD = HEGEL'S SHORTHAND, not MARX'S FORMULA."*

### Flashcard PT-1115 — Trap: historical materialism means economics alone

**Front:** Why is "economics alone explains everything" wrong about historical materialism?

**Definition (learn):** Gauba's neo-Marxist section records growing recognition of interdependence between base and superstructure (✅ owner 04 §9; Gauba PDF p.53).

**Breakdown:**
- Law, religion and culture are not mere decoration (owner 04 §15 Chain 1).
- Gramsci's hegemony gives culture causal work (PT-1110).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.53 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1097; PT-1099; PT-1110.

**Trap:** Ignoring the "play an important role" wording.

**Mains hook:** Economy shapes but does not solely decide.

**Back:** *"ECONOMY SHAPES, not SOLE-DECIDES."*

### Flashcard PT-1116 — Trap: neo-Marxism abandons Marxism

**Front:** Why is "neo-Marxism abandons Marxism" wrong?

**Definition (learn):** Gauba presents neo-Marxism as a development within Marxism, especially around alienation, ideology and emancipation (✅ owner 04 §9; Gauba PDF pp.47, 53-54).

**Breakdown:**
- Continuity: alienation and critique.
- Extension, not refutation (owner 04 §20).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.47, 53-54 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1095; PT-1107; PT-1118.

**Trap:** Treating non-factory domination as outside Marxism.

**Mains hook:** Extension is the owner's verdict.

**Back:** *"NEO-MARXISM = EXTENSION, not DESERTION."*

### Flashcard PT-1117 — Trap: surplus value equals any profit

**Front:** Why is "any profit is surplus value" wrong?

**Definition (learn):** Surplus value is the unpaid part of labour, not any change in sale price (✅ owner 04 §9; Gauba PDF pp.52-53).

**Breakdown:**
- Labour origin is the test.
- Market price movement is separate.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.52-53 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1098; PT-1113.

**Trap:** Equating profit with surplus value.

**Mains hook:** Define surplus value through unpaid labour first.

**Back:** *"SURPLUS = UNPAID LABOUR, not MARKET PRICE."*

### Flashcard PT-1118 — Mechanical determinism versus layered domination (economism)

**Front:** What is economism, and what does the layered model of domination add?

**Definition (learn):** Economism explains domination through economic relations alone; the layered model adds ideology, culture and international structure as sites of domination with their own causal weight (⚠️ owner 04 §6-7, §16; Gauba PDF pp.53-54).

**Breakdown:**
- Mechanical base-determines-superstructure is the crude reading Gauba rejects (PT-1099).
- Gramsci and Althusser give the superstructure real causal weight (owner 04 §15 Chain 2).
- Layers: direct coercion, economic extraction and cultural consent (owner 04 advanced §9).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.53-54 (✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1099; PT-1110; PT-1111; PT-1121.

**Trap:** Presenting classical Marxism as either purely deterministic or purely refuted.

**Mains hook:** The real contrast is between economism and a layered model of domination.

**Back:** *"ECONOMISM = ONE LAYER; LAYERED DOMINATION = COERCION, EXTRACTION, CONSENT."*

### Flashcard PT-1119 — The state-withering problem

**Front:** What challenge does the state-withering problem pose to Marxism?

**Definition (learn):** Actual socialist regimes showed not the withering away of the state but collapse and a return toward liberalisation; this is a historical challenge internal to the tradition (✅ owner 04 advanced §7; Gauba PDF p.54).

**Breakdown:**
- Gauba's comments on the former Soviet Union, Eastern Europe and Chinese liberalisation are book-period observations, not current descriptions (owner 04 advanced §12).
- Challenge: the expected withering of state power did not occur.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.54 (2009 edition context, ✅ owner 04).

**Links:** PT-1101; PT-1112; PT-1118.

**Trap:** Using the book's regime remarks as present-day analysis.

**Mains hook:** The state-withering failure is a reason to revise the mechanism, not to drop the critique.

**Back:** *"STATE DID NOT WITHER: a MARXIST PUZZLE, a HISTORICAL CHALLENGE."*

### Flashcard PT-1120 — Marxism: an Indian conceptual lens only

**Front:** How can Marxist concepts be applied to Indian material, and what must not be claimed?

**Definition (learn):** Marxist concepts (non-mechanical base-superstructure, hegemony, dependency) can frame whether a neutral legal or administrative arrangement protects an existing economic arrangement, but the owner asserts no Indian data (⚠️ owner 04 §19).

**Breakdown:**
- Pair the lens with verified Polity or Economy facts.
- Dependency lens applies to India's trade and investment position only with verified data.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted (owner 04 §19).

**Links:** PT-1110; PT-1111; PT-1118.

**Trap:** Quoting an Indian figure from this file.

**Mains hook:** Use the lens to ask whose interest a neutral-looking arrangement serves.

**Back:** *"INDIA: LENS ONLY; FACTS FROM VERIFIED FILES."*

### Flashcard PT-1121 — Answer architecture for Marxism (qualified thesis)

**Front:** What is the qualified thesis for a Marxism question?

**Definition (learn):** Marxism explains social life through the materialist mechanism of forces and relations of production and class conflict; classical base-superstructure is not mechanical, and neo-Marxist departures extend rather than abandon the mechanism (✅ owner 04 §16; ⚠️ owner synthesis).

**Breakdown:**
- "Explain": premises, mechanism, conclusion, fetter detail.
- "Distinguish": classical versus each neo-Marxist departure.
- 10 marks: fetter sentence, one unit each for classical and neo; 20 marks: four or five units, two chains, India lens.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner directive table (✅ owner 04 §16, §20).

**Links:** PT-1101; PT-1118; PT-1110; PT-1115.

**Trap:** Presenting classical Marxism as purely deterministic or purely refuted.

**Mains hook:** Verdict: extension, not refutation; layered domination, not economism.

**Back:** *"MARXISM: MECHANISM + LAYERS = EXTENSION, not REFUTATION."*

### GROUP — 05 Socialism, Fascism, Anarchism and Gandhism (with the conservatism module in §22)

### Flashcard PT-1122 — Socialism (definition)

**Front:** How does Gauba define socialism?

**Definition (learn):** Ownership or control of the means of production by the community or public authority in the interest of all (✅ owner 05 §3; Gauba PDF p.55).

**Breakdown:**
- Aim: equality through public control of major means of production (✅ owner 05 §2).
- Socialism is a wider family than Marxism (PT-1165).
- Two routes: evolutionary (PT-1123) and revolutionary.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.55-56 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1123; PT-1124; PT-1165.

**Trap:** Do not equate socialism with Marxism.

**Mains hook:** Socialism asks how equality is secured through public control, a question Marxism answers by revolution and social democracy by reform.

**Back:** *"SOCIALISM = PUBLIC CONTROL of PRODUCTION for ALL."*

### Flashcard PT-1123 — Evolutionary versus revolutionary socialism

**Front:** What separates evolutionary (democratic) socialism from revolutionary socialism?

**Definition (learn):** Evolutionary socialism is pursued gradually, constitutionally and democratically; revolutionary socialism seeks total transformation through overthrow of capitalism (✅ owner 05 §3, §5; Gauba PDF p.56).

**Breakdown:**
- Evolutionary: parliament, reform, planning, unions (✅ owner 05 §8).
- Revolutionary: class struggle and overthrow (✅ owner 04 §6; owner 05 advanced §4).
- Owner: democratic socialism reforms power; revolutionary socialism replaces it (owner 05 advanced §6).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.56 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1122; PT-1124; PT-1127; PT-1094.

**Trap:** Treating evolutionary socialism as liberal capitalism with a welfare state; the owner treats it as a real socialist route.

**Mains hook:** Compare method, class theory and relation to liberal democracy.

**Back:** *"EVOLUTION = VOTE and REFORM; REVOLUTION = RUPTURE and OVERTHROW."*

### Flashcard PT-1124 — Fabian socialism (gradual socialisation)

**Front:** What is the Fabian method of socialisation?

**Definition (learn):** Gradual socialisation through legislation, administration, education and middle-class permeation rather than insurrection (✅ owner 05 §6; Gauba PDF pp.57-59).

**Breakdown:**
- Existing democratic state used as an instrument of reform (✅ owner 05 §7).
- Democratic state as tool of planned reform (PT-1125).
- Owner notes the criticism of weak working-class roots (PT-1129).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.57-59 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1123; PT-1125; PT-1129; PT-1123.

**Trap:** Do not treat Fabianism as revolutionary socialism with delays.

**Mains hook:** Fabianism builds socialism inside the constitutional state; its critics ask whether the state will be captured.

**Back:** *"FABIAN = LEGISLATE, ADMINISTER, EDUCATE, PERMEATE."*

### Flashcard PT-1125 — Sidney Webb (name, Fabian democracy)

**Front:** Who is Sidney Webb, and what does he add to Fabianism?

**Definition (learn):** Webb makes the democratic state an instrument of planned social reform and redefines democracy to include control over wealth production, not merely political participation (✅ owner 05 §12.1; Gauba PDF pp.58-59).

**Breakdown:**
- Name control: Gauba prints "Sydney Webb"; the standard name is Sidney Webb (✅ owner 05 §12.1, §21.1; ✅ Wikipedia "Sidney Webb", fetched 2026-11-01; Britannica returned HTTP 403).
- Dates: born 13 July 1859, died 13 October 1947 (✅ Wikipedia, fetched).
- Joined the Fabian Society in 1884; helped to co-found the London School of Economics (✅ Wikipedia, fetched). The LSE founding date (1895) is given on that page; it is not in the owner.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1859-1947; Fabian membership 1884; LSE 1895 (✅ Wikipedia, fetched).

**Links:** PT-1124; PT-1123; PT-1138.

**Trap:** Spelling: use Sidney, not Sydney, for Webb; report Gauba's spelling only in a source-variance note.

**Mains hook:** Webb's redefinition of democracy as control over production is the reformist route to equality.

**Back:** *"SIDNEY WEBB (1859-1947): DEMOCRACY must CONTROL WEALTH PRODUCTION."*

### Flashcard PT-1126 — Ferdinand Lassalle (democratic route)

**Front:** What is Lassalle's position in the owner's account of democratic socialism?

**Definition (learn):** He accepts the historical movement toward workers' power but prefers universal suffrage and peaceful constitutional transition to a workers' state (✅ owner 05 §6; Gauba PDF p.60).

**Breakdown:**
- Democratic route within socialism.
- Owner gives no Lassalle book title or date (⚠️ do not add).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.60 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1123; PT-1127.

**Trap:** Lassalle's preference for suffrage is not Bernstein's revisionism (PT-1127), though both are democratic routes.

**Mains hook:** Suffrage and constitutional transition are the democratic socialist's instruments.

**Back:** *"LASSALLE: WORKERS' POWER through the BALLOT, not the BARRICADE."*

### Flashcard PT-1127 — Eduard Bernstein (revisionism)

**Front:** How does Bernstein revise Marx?

**Definition (learn):** He downgrades the inevitability of class struggle and treats socialism as a democratic movement rather than an apocalyptic end-state (✅ owner 05 §5; Gauba PDF pp.60-62).

**Breakdown:**
- Revision: inevitability of class struggle.
- Socialism as democratic movement.
- Owner gives no book title or date (⚠️ do not add).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.60-62 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1123; PT-1126; PT-1094.

**Trap:** Bernstein's revision is not Marx's own position; it is a downgrade of inevitability.

**Mains hook:** Revisionism is the democratic-socialist answer to Marx's predicted rupture.

**Back:** *"BERNSTEIN: REVISES INEVITABILITY; SOCIALISM becomes a DEMOCRATIC MOVEMENT."*

### Flashcard PT-1128 — Georges Sorel (adjacent to anarchism)

**Front:** What does Sorel argue, and why is he not simply an anarchist?

**Definition (learn):** Trade unions and the revolutionary general strike are the chief instruments of transformative struggle; Gauba notes he is not a full-fledged anarchist (✅ owner 05 §12.2; Gauba PDF pp.76-77).

**Breakdown:**
- Radical change through unions and the general strike.
- Stops short of full statelessness.
- Popular grouping as "anarcho-syndicalism" is a classification caution (✅ owner 05 §12.2).
- Owner: "Britannica (Georges Sorel) control" for name (owner 05 §21.1; not fetched).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.76-77 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1148; PT-1151; PT-1153; PT-1123.

**Trap:** Presenting Sorel as a straightforward anarchist misclassifies him.

**Mains hook:** Sorel shows that radical change can run through organised labour without the total rejection of the state.

**Back:** *"SOREL: UNIONS and the GENERAL STRIKE, NEARLY but NOT FULLY ANARCHIST."*

### Flashcard PT-1129 — Criticisms of democratic socialism

**Front:** What criticisms does Gauba record of evolutionary and Fabian socialism?

**Definition (learn):** Lack of a coherent unified doctrine; drift toward liberal compromise; weak roots in the working class; the possibility of legitimising the bourgeois state (✅ owner 05 advanced §7; Gauba PDF pp.62-63).

**Breakdown:**
- Doctrinal incoherence (PDF p.62).
- Liberal drift (PDF p.62).
- Working-class weakness and legitimation of the bourgeois state (PDF pp.62-63).
- Owner still calls it a real, functioning socialist route (owner 05 advanced §8).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.62-63 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1124; PT-1123; PT-1094.

**Trap:** Treating democratic socialism as pure liberalism is too crude (owner 05 advanced §12).

**Mains hook:** Democratic socialism's strength (institutions) is its risk (co-option).

**Back:** *"DEMOCRATIC SOCIALISM: INCOHERENT, DRIFTING, WEAKLY ROOTED, CO-OPTABLE."*

### Flashcard PT-1130 — Fascism (definition and origin)

**Front:** How does Gauba define fascism and where does it originate?

**Definition (learn):** A doctrine or movement founded by Mussolini in Italy in 1919, built from an incoherent bundle of principles rather than a systematic philosophy, standing for totalitarian single-party dictatorship that is intensely nationalist, racist, militarist and imperialist (✅ owner 05 §3; Gauba PDF pp.64-65).

**Breakdown:**
- Origin: the *fasci* militant groups (✅ owner 05 advanced §3).
- Interwar context (1919-39) (✅ owner 05 advanced §3).
- Owner: Gauba frames fascism as "political pathology", not a coherent philosophy (✅ owner 05 §2).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1919 (Italy); Gauba PDF pp.64-65 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1131; PT-1132; PT-1133; PT-1145.

**Trap:** Calling fascism a coherent philosophy (PT-1145).

**Mains hook:** Fascism is a historically specific totalitarian movement, studied for its pathology rather than its theory.

**Back:** *"FASCISM = 1919 ITALY, INCOHERENT BUNDLE, SINGLE-PARTY TOTALITARIAN."*

### Flashcard PT-1131 — Benito Mussolini (founder and theorist-in-practice)

**Front:** What role does Mussolini play in the owner's account?

**Definition (learn):** Founder of Italian fascism (1919); defined its myth of the nation and rejected the economic conception of history (✅ owner 05 §6; Gauba PDF pp.64, 68-69).

**Breakdown:**
- Dismantled parliamentary parties into a single party (✅ owner 05 §7).
- Converted premiership into "head of the government" (✅ owner 05 §7).
- Blackshirt militia under unconditional command; king and senate reduced to a facade (✅ owner 05 advanced §3).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1919 founding; Gauba PDF pp.64-69 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1130; PT-1134; PT-1137; PT-1146.

**Trap:** Mussolini's and Hitler's systems are not identical (PT-1146).

**Mains hook:** Mussolini is the template case for the leader principle.

**Back:** *"MUSSOLINI: the NATION as MYTH, the LEADER as LAW."*

### Flashcard PT-1132 — Adolf Hitler and Nazism

**Front:** How does Gauba treat Hitler and Nazism relative to fascism?

**Definition (learn):** Nazism is fascism's chief variant; it added a racial doctrine ("myth of race") to Mussolini's nationalist myth (✅ owner 05 §6; Gauba PDF pp.64, 69).

**Breakdown:**
- Shared: leader principle, single party, anti-liberal and anti-Marxist stance.
- Distinct: racist doctrine; programme against "non-Aryan" elements (✅ owner 05 advanced §5).
- Mein Kampf used for propaganda arguments (✅ owner 05 advanced §5).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.64, 69 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1131; PT-1142; PT-1146.

**Trap:** Treating Nazism as identical to Italian fascism (PT-1146).

**Mains hook:** Nazism shows how a racial myth can extend the fascist template.

**Back:** *"HITLER: MUSSOLINI's MYTH + RACE = NAZISM."*

### Flashcard PT-1133 — The leader principle

**Front:** What is the leader principle?

**Definition (learn):** Concentration of unconditional authority in a supreme leader or dictator whose will is treated as law, with the individual holding no rights against the state (✅ owner 05 §3; Gauba PDF pp.66-67).

**Breakdown:**
- Replaces equality with hierarchy culminating in the leader (✅ owner 05 §8).
- Practical sign: dismantling parliamentary pluralism (PT-1134).
- Owner-level verdict: fascism's mechanism of rule.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.66-67 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1130; PT-1131; PT-1134; PT-1136.

**Trap:** The leader principle is not the same as any elected office.

**Mains hook:** Leader principle + single party + totalitarian state is the fascist triad.

**Back:** *"LEADER PRINCIPLE: the LEADER'S WILL IS LAW; INDIVIDUAL HAS NO RIGHTS."*

### Flashcard PT-1134 — Single party and totalitarian state (mechanics)

**Front:** What practical mechanisms does Gauba attribute to the single-party fascist state?

**Definition (learn):** Parliamentary parties dismantled into one party; press, radio, publishing and cinema under government control; judiciary subordinated to fascist ideals (✅ owner 05 §7; Gauba PDF pp.67-68).

**Breakdown:**
- Propaganda and censorship (✅ owner 05 advanced §3).
- Suppression of rival parties and unions (✅ owner 05 §8).
- Internal repression of dissent (✅ owner 05 advanced §8).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.67-68 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1133; PT-1131; PT-1135; PT-1044 (ideology-totalitarian).

**Trap:** Do not present these as ordinary authoritarian features; they are the totalitarian reach (PT-1046).

**Mains hook:** Ask which institutions remain independent to test whether a state is totalitarian.

**Back:** *"ONE PARTY, ONE PRESS, ONE JUDGE'S ALLEGIANCE: totalitarian REACH."*

### Flashcard PT-1135 — Fascism as counter-revolution

**Front:** Why does Gauba call fascism a counter-revolutionary theory?

**Definition (learn):** It stripped capitalism of democratic and welfare-oriented restraints, left workers without rights, unions or free criticism, and secured elite economic control (✅ owner 05 §4; Gauba PDF pp.65, 67-70).

**Breakdown:**
- Shields concentrated capital (✅ owner 05 advanced §3).
- Owner notes: Gauba separately calls it militarist and imperialist; linking these into one economic causal claim needs more argument (owner 05 §4).
- Laski's "rescue of capitalism" (PT-1139).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.65, 67-70 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1139; PT-1136; PT-1138.

**Trap:** Do not claim fascism's militarism is proved by one economic cause.

**Mains hook:** Fascism is counter-revolution for capital, not a rival egalitarian path.

**Back:** *"FASCISM = COUNTER-REVOLUTION: CAPITAL SAVED, WORKERS SILENCED."*

### Flashcard PT-1136 — Fascism's negation: hierarchy, not equality

**Front:** How do fascism and socialism differ on equality and hierarchy?

**Definition (learn):** Fascism rejects equality and substitutes hierarchy culminating in the leader; socialism pursues equality through public control (✅ owner 05 §8, §13; Gauba PDF pp.55-56, 66).

**Breakdown:**
- Fascism: state as end, individual as means (✅ owner 05 §5).
- Socialism: equality via public control (PT-1122).
- Both criticise unregulated capitalism, but for opposite social values (owner 05 §9).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.55-56, 66 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1122; PT-1133; PT-1147; PT-1164.

**Trap:** Grouping fascism with socialism as "collectivist" (PT-1147).

**Mains hook:** The fault line is equality-seeking critics versus hierarchy-seeking fascism.

**Back:** *"FASCISM: HIERARCHY for EQUALITY's OPPOSITE; SOCIALISM: EQUALITY via PUBLIC CONTROL."*

### Flashcard PT-1137 — Fascism versus Marxism: myth versus class conflict

**Front:** How does fascism's account of society differ from Marxism's?

**Definition (learn):** Fascism denies the economic conception of history and class conflict, replacing it with the myth of an indivisible national interest; Marxism makes class conflict central (✅ owner 05 §5; Gauba PDF pp.68-69).

**Breakdown:**
- Mussolini's own rejection of the economic conception of history (✅ owner 05 advanced §3).
- Hitler's racial extension of the myth (PT-1132).
- Gauba's Marxist critique: fascism suppresses class-conflict discourse (PT-1143).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.68-69 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1094; PT-1131; PT-1143.

**Trap:** Treating fascism as a version of socialism because both attack capitalism (PT-1147).

**Mains hook:** Myth replaces analysis.

**Back:** *"FASCISM: NATIONAL MYTH replaces CLASS ANALYSIS."*

### Flashcard PT-1138 — Socio-economic basis of fascism (the debate)

**Front:** What positions does Gauba survey on fascism's social base?

**Definition (learn):** Whether fascism was chiefly a lower-middle-class movement (McGovern, MacIver) or, for Laski, an instrument serving concentrated capital; Ebenstein stresses weak democracy plus depression; Trotsky locates the mass base in a desperate middle class (✅ owner 05 advanced §3; Gauba PDF pp.65-72).

**Breakdown:**
- McGovern and MacIver: lower-middle-class and disoriented small-bourgeois groups (owner 05 advanced §5 for MacIver; PT-1130).
- Laski: capitalist interest (PT-1139).
- Ebenstein: weak democracy and depression (PT-1141).
- Trotsky: desperate middle class (PT-1140).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.65-72 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1139; PT-1141; PT-1140; PT-1135.

**Trap:** Do not treat one theory as the complete answer; the owner presents them as competing claims.

**Mains hook:** Use the debate to show that fascism's emergence is contested.

**Back:** *"WHO BACKED FASCISM? MIDDLE CLASS, CAPITAL, or WEAK DEMOCRACY in DEPRESSION."*

### Flashcard PT-1139 — Harold Laski (fascism rescues capitalism)

**Front:** What does Laski argue about fascism and capitalism?

**Definition (learn):** Fascism rescues capitalism from the tension between capitalism and democracy by abolishing democratic rights, unions and free criticism (✅ owner 05 §6; Gauba PDF pp.68, 70).

**Breakdown:**
- Entrusts unlimited power to those who own and control production (✅ owner 05 advanced §5).
- Gauba records this as one side of the socio-economic debate (PT-1138).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.68, 70 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1135; PT-1138; PT-1141.

**Trap:** Laski's thesis is an argument, not a settled fact (owner 05 advanced §7 debate).

**Mains hook:** Laski ties fascism to the crisis of capitalism-democracy.

**Back:** *"LASKI: FASCISM = CAPITAL'S RESCUE by ABOLISHING DEMOCRACY."*

### Flashcard PT-1140 — Antonio Gramsci and Trotsky on fascism

**Front:** How do Gramsci and Trotsky explain fascism?

**Definition (learn):** Gramsci: fascist ideology sustains capitalist hegemony and structures of domination to secure mass acceptance. Trotsky: fascism's mass base is a desperate, rootless middle class exploiting crisis fear (✅ owner 05 §6, advanced §5; Gauba PDF p.72).

**Breakdown:**
- Gramsci: tortured under Mussolini's regime (✅ owner 05 §6).
- Trotsky: fear and uncertainty of crisis (✅ owner 05 advanced §5).
- Gramsci's general hegemony theory: PT-1110.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.72 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1110; PT-1138; PT-1143.

**Trap:** Gramsci explains why fascism was accepted, not that fascism is coherent (owner 05 Unit 5).

**Mains hook:** Consent and crisis-mass psychology are the Marxist explanations.

**Back:** *"GRAMSCI: CONSENT for ELITE RULE. TROTSKY: DESPERATE MIDDLE CLASS."*

### Flashcard PT-1141 — William Ebenstein (fascism and weak democracy)

**Front:** What does Ebenstein argue about fascism's origins?

**Definition (learn):** Fascism arises less from capitalism itself than from weak democracy plus fear and frustration in economic depression (✅ owner 05 §6; Gauba PDF pp.70-71).

**Breakdown:**
- Ebenstein's definition: totalitarian, nationalist, racist, militarist and imperialist single-party rule (✅ owner 05 advanced §5).
- Fascism thrives where democracy is weak and depression breeds frustration.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.65, 70-71 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1130; PT-1138; PT-1139.

**Trap:** Ebenstein's explanation is not fascism's own stated diagnosis (owner 05 advanced §8).

**Mains hook:** Weak democracy plus depression is a political-institutional explanation, distinct from the economic one.

**Back:** *"EBENSTEIN: WEAK DEMOCRACY + DEPRESSION = FASCISM'S SOIL."*

### Flashcard PT-1142 — Spread of fascism: Germany, Spain and Japan

**Front:** Which countries does Gauba name as partial adopters or parallels of fascism?

**Definition (learn):** Partially adopted by Hitler's Nazism in Germany and Franco's Falangism in Spain; weaker parallels in Japan (✅ owner 05 advanced §3; Gauba PDF pp.64-65).

**Breakdown:**
- Germany: Nazism, a variant with racial doctrine (PT-1132).
- Spain: Franco's Falangism (owner 05 advanced §3).
- Japan: weaker parallels (owner 05 §3 advanced).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.64-65 (book-period illustrations, ✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1132; PT-1130.

**Trap:** These are historical illustrations, not present-day scorecards (owner 05 advanced §12).

**Mains hook:** The spread shows a template travelling under local conditions; the parallels are weaker outside Italy and Germany.

**Back:** *"ITALY 1919; GERMANY NAZI; SPAIN FALANGIST; JAPAN WEAKER PARALLEL."*

### Flashcard PT-1143 — Liberal and Marxist critiques of fascism

**Front:** What critiques of fascism does Gauba record, from liberals and from Marxists?

**Definition (learn):** Liberal: destruction of individual liberty, irrationalism, denial of natural equality, anti-pluralism, demolition of constitutional government. Marxist: capitalism-in-decay device suppressing class conflict and thwarting international communism (✅ owner 05 advanced §7; Gauba PDF pp.71-73).

**Breakdown:**
- Liberal list (owner 05 advanced §3, §7).
- Marxist list: Gramsci and Trotsky (PT-1140).
- Gauba's own verdict follows (PT-1144).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.71-73 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1140; PT-1144; PT-1137.

**Trap:** Listing critiques without Gauba's verdict loses the appraisal marks.

**Mains hook:** Two critiques from opposite sides converge on fascism's illegitimacy.

**Back:** *"LIBERALS: LIBERTY and REASON; MARXISTS: CAPITAL'S DEVICE."*

### Flashcard PT-1144 — Gauba's verdict: "sick mental and political attitude"

**Front:** What is Gauba's own appraisal of fascism, and how does he distinguish it from communist totalitarianism?

**Definition (learn):** Fascism is a "sick mental and political attitude" and a lunatic-fringe philosophy that curbs liberty and equality and distorts justice, not a genuine political theory (✅ owner 05 §4; Gauba PDF pp.71-73). It is distinct from communist totalitarianism because communism retains a genuine, if coercive, distributive aim (✅ owner 05 advanced §7; Almond and Powell cited by Gauba, PDF p.73).

**Breakdown:**
- Verdict: retrograde, anti-progressive (owner 05 advanced §7).
- Distributive capability: communism yes; fascism lacks it (owner 05 advanced §12).
- Owner: "not a genuine alternative political theory" (owner 05 §4).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.71-73 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1143; PT-1145; PT-1046.

**Trap:** Treating fascism and Soviet-style communism as identical totalitarianism is one Gauba explicitly criticises (PT-1147; owner 05 advanced §12).

**Mains hook:** The distributive difference matters for the appraisal.

**Back:** *"GAUBA: FASCISM = SICK PATHOLOGY; COMMUNISM = COERCIVE but DISTRIBUTIVE."*

### Flashcard PT-1145 — Trap: fascism is a coherent political philosophy

**Front:** Why is "fascism is a coherent political philosophy like liberalism or Marxism" wrong?

**Definition (learn):** Gauba states fascism wove unrelated principles into an incoherent whole to meet political needs and never developed a consistent theory (✅ owner 05 §9; Gauba PDF pp.64-65).

**Breakdown:**
- Bundle of principles, not a system.
- Belongs to political pathology (PT-1144).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.64-65 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1130; PT-1144.

**Trap:** The error is in the category, not in the political danger.

**Mains hook:** Coherence is a test of doctrine; fascism fails it as a doctrine.

**Back:** *"FASCISM: a BUNDLE, not a BLUEPRINT."*

### Flashcard PT-1146 — Trap: Mussolini's Italy and Hitler's Germany are identical

**Front:** Why is it wrong to treat Italian fascism and German Nazism as identical?

**Definition (learn):** Gauba treats Nazism as the chief variant that shares the leader principle, single party and anti-liberal and anti-Marxist stance, but adds a racist doctrine that Italian fascism did not centre in the same way (✅ owner 05 §9; Gauba PDF pp.64, 69).

**Breakdown:**
- Shared features (PT-1133, PT-1134).
- Distinct racial doctrine (PT-1132).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.64, 69 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1131; PT-1132.

**Trap:** Collapsing two variants into one "fascism" loses the distinction.

**Mains hook:** Distinguish variants by their added doctrine.

**Back:** *"ITALY: MYTH OF NATION; GERMANY: MYTH + RACE."*

### Flashcard PT-1147 — Trap: fascism and socialism are both collectivist

**Front:** Why are fascism and socialism not on the same side just because both attack capitalism?

**Definition (learn):** Fascism rejects equality and seeks hierarchy; socialism seeks equality through public control. They share a critique of unregulated capitalism but stand for opposite social values (✅ owner 05 §9, advanced §12).

**Breakdown:**
- Equality row (PT-1136).
- Liberty row: fascism subordinates the individual (PT-1136).
- Chain 3 reply (owner 05 §17).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.55-56, 66 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1136; PT-1122; PT-1164.

**Trap:** Shared critique does not imply shared values.

**Mains hook:** Use the synthesis table to decide the family question.

**Back:** *"SHARED CRITIQUE, OPPOSITE VALUES: fascism vs socialism."*

### Flashcard PT-1148 — Anarchism (definition)

**Front:** How does Gauba define anarchism?

**Definition (learn):** The doctrine that society can and should be organised without the coercive authority of the state (✅ owner 05 §3; Gauba PDF p.73).

**Breakdown:**
- Target: coercive state authority, not all coordination or moral obligation (✅ owner 05 §4).
- Abolition of coercive state, not of all norms (✅ owner 05 advanced §4).
- Most anarchists accept expert and democratic moral authority (PT-1155).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.73-74 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1149; PT-1150; PT-1155.

**Trap:** Anarchism means chaos (PT-1155).

**Mains hook:** Anarchism's target is coercion; its method is voluntary association.

**Back:** *"ANARCHISM = NO COERCIVE STATE, NOT NO ORDER."*

### Flashcard PT-1149 — Philosophical anarchism

**Front:** What is philosophical anarchism in the owner's account?

**Definition (learn):** The denial of any legitimate command-right of one individual over another (✅ owner 05 advanced §4; Gauba PDF p.74).

**Breakdown:**
- Narrower than political anarchism: about the legitimacy of command.
- Owner's advanced file classifies it separately.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.74 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1148; PT-1150.

**Trap:** Denial of command-right is not denial of all expertise (PT-1155).

**Mains hook:** Use the concept to separate the moral from the institutional claim against the state.

**Back:** *"PHILOSOPHICAL ANARCHISM: NO ONE HAS A RIGHT TO COMMAND ANOTHER."*

### Flashcard PT-1150 — Schools of anarchism

**Front:** Which schools of anarchism does Gauba distinguish?

**Definition (learn):** Individualist through socialist and revolutionary variants: mutualist, communist, collectivist, syndicalist, pacifist and libertarian (✅ owner 05 advanced §1, §4; Gauba PDF pp.73-78).

**Breakdown:**
- Mutualist: Proudhon (PT-1151).
- Communist: Kropotkin (PT-1152).
- Collectivist and insurrectionary: Bakunin (PT-1153).
- Syndicalist: Sorel adjacent (PT-1128).
- Pacific: Tolstoy (PT-1154).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.73-78 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1151; PT-1152; PT-1153; PT-1154; PT-1148.

**Trap:** Treating all anarchism as violent insurrection or all as pacifist.

**Mains hook:** Schools differ on method, not on the anti-coercive core.

**Back:** *"ANARCHISM SCHOOLS: MUTUAL, COMMUNAL, COLLECTIVE, SYNDICAL, PACIFIC."*

### Flashcard PT-1151 — Pierre-Joseph Proudhon (mutualism)

**Front:** What is Proudhon's mutualist anarchism?

**Definition (learn):** Autonomous associations exchanging on equivalent terms with mutual aid and cheap credit; socialist anarchism with federal association and peaceful direct action (✅ owner 05 §3, §6; Gauba PDF pp.74-75).

**Breakdown:**
- Producers' associations linked by a federal principle (✅ owner 05 §7).
- Supported by a mutual credit bank (✅ owner 05 §7).
- Opposes both state power and capitalist power (✅ owner 05 advanced §5).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.74-75 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1150; PT-1152; PT-1153.

**Trap:** Mutualism is not state socialism; it is voluntary exchange among associations.

**Mains hook:** Mutualism shows an anarchist economics of federation and credit.

**Back:** *"PROUDHON: MUTUAL CREDIT, FEDERATED ASSOCIATIONS, no STATE."*

### Flashcard PT-1152 — Peter Kropotkin (mutual aid)

**Front:** What does Kropotkin contribute to anarchism?

**Definition (learn):** Communist anarchism built on mutual aid, sociability and distribution according to needs (✅ owner 05 §6; Gauba PDF p.75).

**Breakdown:**
- Mutual aid as the natural basis of cooperation (owner 05 Unit 6).
- Gives anarchism a constructive account, not only a negative one (owner 05 §16).
- Critics: over-optimism about large-scale cooperation (owner 05 Unit 6; Gauba PDF p.78).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.75, 78 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1150; PT-1151; PT-1153.

**Trap:** Kropotkin's communism is needs-based distribution, not state command.

**Mains hook:** Kropotkin's cooperation is a claim about human nature, which critics test at scale.

**Back:** *"KROPOTKIN: MUTUAL AID, SOCIABILITY, DISTRIBUTION BY NEED."*

### Flashcard PT-1153 — Mikhail Bakunin (collectivist insurrection)

**Front:** What does Bakunin advocate?

**Definition (learn):** Revolutionary, collectivist, insurrectionary and anti-authoritarian socialism organised from below upward (✅ owner 05 §6, advanced §5; Gauba PDF pp.75-76).

**Breakdown:**
- Collectivisation and insurrection (owner 05 §6).
- Anti-authoritarian socialism.
- Contrast with Proudhon's peaceful method (PT-1151).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.75-76 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1150; PT-1151; PT-1152; PT-1154.

**Trap:** Bakunin's insurrectionism is not the same as Tolstoy's pacifism.

**Mains hook:** Bakunin shows anarchism can be violent; Tolstoy shows it can be pacifist.

**Back:** *"BAKUNIN: COLLECTIVE, INSURRECTIONARY, FROM BELOW."*

### Flashcard PT-1154 — Leo Tolstoy (pacifist-Christian anarchism)

**Front:** What is Tolstoy's anarchism, and what is its link to Gandhi?

**Definition (learn):** A moral-pacifist anarchism grounded in love, non-obedience to immoral commands and critique of organised violence (✅ owner 05 §12.3; Gauba PDF p.77).

**Breakdown:**
- Gauba states Gandhi was inspired by these ideas in developing non-violence (✅ owner 05 §12.3; Gauba PDF p.77).
- Owner forbids attaching any letter, date or publication to this influence claim (owner 05 §12.3).
- Limitation: as individual moral position, weak institutional guidance (owner 05 Unit 3).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.77 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1158; PT-1160; PT-1150; PT-1153.

**Trap:** Tolstoy and Gandhi are not identical: Tolstoy is an individual moral witness, Gandhi builds collective method (PT-1162).

**Mains hook:** Tolstoy is an influence on Gandhian non-violence, not an equivalent.

**Back:** *"TOLSTOY: LOVE and NON-OBEDIENCE; INFLUENCE on GANDHI's NON-VIOLENCE."*

### Flashcard PT-1155 — Trap: anarchism means chaos

**Front:** Why is "anarchism means chaos" wrong?

**Definition (learn):** Most anarchists accept rational expert authority and the moral authority of democratic collective decisions; the target is coercive state authority (✅ owner 05 §9; Gauba PDF p.74).

**Breakdown:**
- Expert authority can remain (PT-1136; owner 05 §8).
- Order through voluntary association (PT-1148).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.74 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1148; PT-1149.

**Trap:** Anarchy as disorder is a misreading of the doctrine.

**Mains hook:** Explain why anarchism is anti-coercion rather than anti-order.

**Back:** *"ANARCHY ≠ CHAOS: no COERCIVE RULER, but ORDER by CONSENT."*

### Flashcard PT-1156 — Gandhism: ethics in politics

**Front:** What is the core of Gandhism as Gauba reconstructs it?

**Definition (learn):** Gandhi's dispersed but coherent worldview grounded in ethics, truth, non-violence and social regeneration; politics is inseparable from ethics and means from ends (✅ owner 05 §3, §4; Gauba PDF pp.78-81).

**Breakdown:**
- Means and ends are inseparable (owner 05 §4).
- Swaraj by non-violence and social reconstruction (owner 05 §4).
- Self-regulated order: classless ideal via moral discipline (owner 05 §4).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.78-83 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1157; PT-1158; PT-1159; PT-1162.

**Trap:** Gandhism is not a rejection of all politics; it moralises politics.

**Mains hook:** Gandhism's distinctive move is to insist on moral means.

**Back:** *"GANDHISM = ETHICS INSIDE POLITICS: MEANS = ENDS."*

### Flashcard PT-1157 — Satyagraha, ahimsa and swaraj

**Front:** How are satyagraha, ahimsa and swaraj related in Gandhi's method?

**Definition (learn):** Ahimsa (non-violence) is non-negotiable; satyagraha is non-violent resistance grounded in truth; swaraj is self-rule (✅ owner 05 §8; owner 05 advanced §4; Gauba PDF pp.79-81).

**Breakdown:**
- Ahimsa: non-violence as a principle (owner 05 §8).
- Satyagraha: method of moral persuasion (owner 05 §8).
- Swaraj: independence from foreign rule and moral self-rule (⚠️ owner 05 §5 wider reading).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.79-81 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1156; PT-1158; PT-1162.

**Trap:** Swaraj as independence alone omits the moral self-discipline reading.

**Mains hook:** Freedom without moral restraint is not genuine liberty (owner 05 §13).

**Back:** *"AHIMSA = NON-VIOLENCE; SATYAGRAHA = TRUTH-FORCE; SWARAJ = SELF-RULE."*

### Flashcard PT-1158 — Trusteeship

**Front:** What is trusteeship in Gandhi's economic ethics?

**Definition (learn):** Owners treat possessions as a trust for humanity rather than absolute private entitlement; moral transformation of owners into trustees, not expropriation by force (✅ owner 05 §3, advanced §4; Gauba PDF p.81).

**Breakdown:**
- Equality via trusteeship and dignity of labour, not coercive levelling (✅ owner 05 §8).
- Standard objection: the rich unlikely to undergo the required change of heart (✅ owner 05 §16 Unit 4; Gauba PDF p.81).
- Sarvodaya belongs to Common-Good file (owner 05 §12.4).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.81 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1156; PT-1159; PT-1154.

**Trap:** Trusteeship is not nationalisation or expropriation (owner 05 §8).

**Mains hook:** Trusteeship tests whether moral persuasion can reform property.

**Back:** *"TRUSTEESHIP: OWNERS as CUSTODIANS, not EXPROPRIATED."*

### Flashcard PT-1159 — Bread labour

**Front:** What is bread labour and what does it aim to achieve?

**Definition (learn):** Compulsory physical labour by everyone to restore dignity of labour and social equality (✅ owner 05 §4 advanced §4; Gauba PDF p.82).

**Breakdown:**
- Every person performs physical labour (owner 05 §7).
- Weakens class hierarchy and restores dignity of labour (owner 05 §7).
- Preference for simple technology (PT-1161).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.82 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1158; PT-1161; PT-1156.

**Trap:** Bread labour is a moral economic programme, not only manual work for its own sake.

**Mains hook:** Bread labour converts non-violence into a labour programme.

**Back:** *"BREAD LABOUR: EVERYONE WORKS with HANDS, DIGNITY over HIERARCHY."*

### Flashcard PT-1160 — Gandhi and Marx compared

**Front:** On which dimensions does Gauba compare Gandhi and Marx?

**Definition (learn):** Religion, technology, class conflict, the state and method of change (✅ owner 05 §9, §12; Gauba PDF pp.82-83).

**Breakdown:**
- Shared aim of a classless society, different means (owner 05 §9).
- Gandhi: non-violence and moral transformation; Marx: class conflict and revolution (owner 05 §4).
- Owner gives topic headings only; no further detail is asserted in this file.

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.82-83 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1156; PT-1162; PT-1094.

**Trap:** Treating both as the same because both mention a classless society (PT-1162).

**Mains hook:** Use the five dimensions as the comparison frame.

**Back:** *"GANDHI vs MARX: SAME CLASSLESS GOAL, OPPOSITE ROUTES."*

### Flashcard PT-1161 — Gandhi on machinery and simple technology

**Front:** What does Gandhi prefer in technology, and what is the Indian application?

**Definition (learn):** Simple technology and production by the masses over mass production (✅ owner 05 §8; Gauba PDF p.81).

**Breakdown:**
- Critique of heavy machinery, unemployment and consumerism (⚠️ owner 05 advanced §9 application).
- Links to the bread-labour dignity claim (PT-1159).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF p.81 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1159; PT-1160.

**Trap:** Gandhi's technology preference is a moral critique, not a claim that all machinery is useless.

**Mains hook:** Gandhi's industrial critique asks what work does to dignity and employment.

**Back:** *"GANDHI: SIMPLE TOOLS, MASS HANDS, DIGNITY OF LABOUR."*

### Flashcard PT-1162 — Trap: Gandhian statelessness means normlessness

**Front:** Why is "Gandhi wanted no social norms at all" wrong?

**Definition (learn):** The stateless ideal depends on truth, non-violence, self-discipline and moral responsibility (✅ owner 05 §5, §9; Gauba PDF pp.79-83).

**Breakdown:**
- Self-regulated order is moral, not normless (owner 05 §5).
- Coercive state becomes redundant (owner 05 §8).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.79-83 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1156; PT-1157.

**Trap:** Statelessness is not the absence of duty.

**Mains hook:** Self-regulation requires strong norms.

**Back:** *"STATELESS ≠ NORMLESS: SELF-DISCIPLINE is the ORDER."*

### Flashcard PT-1163 — Trap: Gandhi appealed to tradition, so he is conservative

**Front:** Why is "Gandhi appealed to village tradition, therefore Gandhi is a conservative" a trap?

**Definition (learn):** Gandhi attacks entrenched social hierarchy and subordinates property to trusteeship; appealing to indigenous practice against imported models is not granting inherited hierarchy a presumption of legitimacy (✅ owner 05 advanced §12; ⚠️ owner 05 §22.9 trap; Gauba PDF pp.81-82).

**Breakdown:**
- Trusteeship (PT-1158).
- Conservatism's presumption is not the same as Gandhian moral critique (PT-1178).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.81-82 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1158; PT-1180; PT-1178.

**Trap:** Surface appeal to tradition is not conservatism.

**Mains hook:** Distinguish appeal to heritage from acceptance of hierarchy.

**Back:** *"APPEAL TO VILLAGE ≠ DEFENCE OF HIERARCHY."*

### Flashcard PT-1164 — Four-doctrine synthesis: liberty, equality, state and property

**Front:** How do socialism, fascism, anarchism and Gandhism compare on liberty, equality, state and property?

**Definition (learn):** Socialism: liberty through social conditions; equality through public control; state as reform instrument; community property. Fascism: liberty subordinated; hierarchy; state as end; capital concentrated. Anarchism: freedom from coercive state; equality via abolition of hierarchy; state abolished; varied property. Gandhism: swaraj with self-discipline; equality via trusteeship; state redundant; trusteeship property (✅ owner 05 §13; Gauba PDF pp.55-83).

**Breakdown:**
- Four axes in one table (owner 05 §13).
- Verdict: fascism alone answers equality with hierarchy (⚠️ owner 05 §13).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.55-83 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1136; PT-1147; PT-1122; PT-1156.

**Trap:** Treating all four as one anti-capitalist bloc (PT-1165).

**Mains hook:** Use the four axes for any "distinguish" or "compare".

**Back:** *"FOUR AXES: LIBERTY, EQUALITY, STATE, PROPERTY: FASCISM OUTLIERS."*

### Flashcard PT-1165 — Trap: socialism is just Marxism

**Front:** Why is "socialism is just another word for Marxism" wrong?

**Definition (learn):** Socialism is a wider family including evolutionary, Fabian, revisionist and syndicalist variants (✅ owner 05 §9; Gauba PDF pp.55-63).

**Breakdown:**
- Fabians and Bernstein (PT-1124, PT-1127).
- Revolutionary Marxism is one variant (PT-1123).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba PDF pp.55-63 (✅ owner 05).

**Links:** PT-1122; PT-1123.

**Trap:** Collapsing the family into one variant.

**Mains hook:** Socialism is a family; Marxism is one branch.

**Back:** *"SOCIALISM = FAMILY; MARXISM = ONE BRANCH."*

### Flashcard PT-1166 — Conservatism: working definition and genus-differentia

**Front:** How should conservatism be defined, and what is its genus and differentia?

**Definition (learn):** Conservatism treats inherited institutions, practices and settled expectations as a store of practical experience, placing the burden of proof on those proposing redesign and favouring gradual, corrective, reversible reform (⚠️ owner 05 §22.2; named-scholar reconstruction, not Gauba).

**Breakdown:**
- Genus: doctrine about the warrant for political change (⚠️ owner 05 §22.2).
- Differentia: warrant lies in inherited practice and demonstrated workability, not a designed end-state (⚠️ owner 05 §22.2).
- Not a PDF pp.55-83 claim: no Gauba page cited (owner 05 §22 provenance note).

**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Named-scholar sources only (owner 05 §22).

**Links:** PT-1167; PT-1168; PT-1177.

**Trap:** Conservatism is a doctrine about change, not a doctrine against change.

**Mains hook:** Define conservatism by its warrant for change, then contrast with reaction.

**Back:** *"CONSERVATISM: CHANGE by PROOF, not by DESIGN."*

### Flashcard PT-1167 — Trap: conservatism opposes all change

**Front:** Why is "conservatism means opposing all change" the commonest error?
