- Property is suspect when it is both concentrated (power over others) and functionless (no justifying service) (⚠️ owner 18 §13).
- Test applied to the income, not merely to the size of the holding (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.420–426 (✅ owner 18).
**Links:** PT-4023, PT-4019.
**Trap:** Arguing all large holdings are automatically illegitimate ❌ (✅ owner 18 §13).
**Mains hook:** Use Tawney's test to ask what service justifies a given income stream.
**Back:** *"Tawney asks: what service does this income buy?"*

### Flashcard PT-4025 — Marx and Engels on private property
**Front:** Why do Marx and Engels want private property in the means of production abolished?
**Definition (learn):** Freedom requires mastery over social production, the end of alienation and emancipation from class domination. Private ownership of major means of production is the basis of class domination (✅ owner 18, PDF pp.384–388, 426–430).
**Breakdown:**
- Surplus value: labour creates value beyond its wage, which ownership appropriates (✅ owner basic 20 §7, PDF pp.453–455; see PT-4093).
- Gauba's advanced verdict: communism is attractive, but new domination can reappear through bureaucratic or political power (✅ owner advanced 20 §7, PDF p.459).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.384–388, 426–430 (✅ owner 18).
**Links:** PT-4020, PT-4026, PT-4027, PT-4019, PT-4093.
**Trap:** "Marxism abolishes all property" ❌.
**Mains hook:** State the structural case, then the realisation weakness.
**Back:** *"Marx: surplus is unpaid labour; abolish the means, not the toothbrush."*

### Flashcard PT-4026 — Herbert Marcuse on false needs
**Front:** What is Marcuse's critique of consumer capitalism?
**Definition (learn):** Consumer capitalism manufactures wants, dulls critical consciousness and produces one-dimensional people, thereby suppressing the urge for genuine freedom (✅ owner 18, PDF pp.388–389; advanced §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Freedom requires awakening people to their own alienation (✅ owner 18 advanced §5).
- Not the same as Macpherson's developmental-power argument (PT-4027).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.388–389 (✅ owner 18).
**Links:** PT-4025, PT-4027.
**Trap:** Confusing Marcuse with Macpherson ❌ (✅ owner 18 §11 ledger).
**Mains hook:** Freedom as consciousness of false needs, not only of external constraint.
**Back:** *"Marcuse: wants manufactured, consciousness one-dimensional."*

### Flashcard PT-4027 — C.B. Macpherson: developmental versus extractive power
**Front:** What is Macpherson's distinction between developmental and extractive power?
**Definition (learn):** Freedom is creative or developmental power. Capitalist market society erodes this among the poor while expanding extractive power among owners (✅ owner 18, PDF pp.389, 418–419).
**Breakdown:**
- Market valuation can stunt human capacities and needs welfare correction (✅ owner 21 §6, PDF p.480).
- Used against market-centred proceduralism in justice (✅ owner 19 §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.389, 418–419 (✅ owner 18).
**Links:** PT-4026, PT-4025, PT-4134.
**Trap:** Treating Macpherson as Marcuse ❌.
**Mains hook:** Developmental freedom as the yardstick for judging market outcomes.
**Back:** *"Macpherson: developmental power grows; extractive power grabs."*

### Flashcard PT-4028 — Nozick's entitlement theory
**Front:** What are Nozick's three principles of justice in holdings?
**Definition (learn):** A holding is just if it came about through: (i) justice in acquisition, constrained by a modified Lockean proviso that appropriation must not worsen others' position; (ii) justice in transfer, through voluntary exchange, gift or bequest from a just holding; (iii) justice in rectification, where earlier acquisition or transfer was unjust (✅ owner 18 §14; ✅ Wikipedia: *Anarchy, State, and Utopia*).
**Breakdown:**
- Historical, not end-state: how a holding arose matters, not its pattern.
- Minimal state limited to protection against force, theft, fraud and contract enforcement (✅ Wikipedia).
- Wilt Chamberlain example (PT-4091).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** First published 1974, Basic Books (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-4029, PT-4021, PT-4011, PT-4034.
**Trap:** Forgetting the third principle, rectification ❌.
**Mains hook:** Historical entitlement versus patterned distribution; the rectification clause is the internal limit.
**Back:** *"Acquire justly, transfer justly, rectify injustice. No patterns."*

### Flashcard PT-4029 — Nozick and the rectification trap
**Front:** Does Nozick bless any present distribution merely because later transfers were voluntary?
**Definition (learn):** No. Where the historical chain contains unjust acquisition or transfer, the holding is unjust regardless of later voluntary transfers, and rectification is required (✅ owner 18 §14; ⚠️ owner's reading of Nozick's rectification).
**Breakdown:**
- The scale, counterfactual baseline and institutional form of rectification are seriously under-specified (✅ owner 18 §14).
- Establishing historical facts for rectification is practically very difficult (✅ owner 19 §17 reply).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only; Wikipedia summary confirms the three-principle structure.
**Links:** PT-4028, PT-4021, PT-4065.
**Trap:** "Nozick opposes all redistribution" ❌; he opposes patterned redistribution of holdings that are already just (✅ owner 18 §14).
**Mains hook:** A libertarian defence of property has an internal limit, which the answer can use to qualify unconditional property protection.
**Back:** *"Nozick: voluntary now, but was the past clean?"*

### Flashcard PT-4030 — MacCallum's triadic formula
**Front:** What is MacCallum's point against the negative/positive dichotomy?
**Definition (learn):** Every freedom claim takes the form "X is free from Y to do or be Z": an agent, a constraint and a purpose. Negative and positive theories emphasise different variables (⚠️ owner 18 §12; not verified externally).
**Breakdown:**
- Shows the dichotomy is not a deep conceptual split.
- Does not settle which constraints or purposes are justified; that substantive weighing remains (✅ owner 18 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4007, PT-4031, PT-4032.
**Trap:** Thinking MacCallum resolves the liberty debate ❌.
**Mains hook:** Use the formula to qualify, not reject, the negative/positive framing.
**Back:** *"Free from Y to do Z: same formula, different variables."*

### Flashcard PT-4031 — Taylor's exercise concept versus opportunity concept
**Front:** What does Charles Taylor add to the liberty debate?
**Definition (learn):** Pure non-interference is a "space left open". Internal obstacles, such as fear, false consciousness, adaptive preferences or manipulated desire, can block a person from identifying and pursuing significant purposes. Freedom needs an *exercise* concept, not only an *opportunity* concept (⚠️ owner 18 §12; not verified externally).
**Breakdown:**
- Strengthens the case that some socially produced obstacles are legitimate targets.
- Taylor's concept can be stretched into intrusive correction of a person's "real" purposes; re-apply Berlin's caution (✅ owner 18 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4006, PT-4007, PT-4030.
**Trap:** Using Taylor without Berlin's check ❌.
**Mains hook:** Pair Taylor's exercise concept with Berlin's caution in every answer that invokes it.
**Back:** *"Taylor: an open space is not enough; purposes must be exercised, not imposed."*

### Flashcard PT-4032 — Republican liberty as non-domination (third family)
**Front:** What is the republican or non-domination family of liberty?
**Definition (learn):** Freedom is the absence of subjection to another's arbitrary power, whether or not that power is exercised. Domination requires capacity, arbitrariness and impunity (⚠️ owner 18 §22).
**Breakdown:**
- Philip Pettit: normative theory of freedom as non-domination, *Republicanism: A Theory of Freedom and Government* (1997) (✅ Wikipedia bibliography; definition not on the fetched page).
- Quentin Skinner: historical recovery of neo-Roman liberty, *Liberty before Liberalism* (1998) (⚠️ owner; not fetched).
- Not Gauba's own category; keep roles of Pettit and Skinner separate (⚠️ owner 18 §22.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Pettit 1997 (✅ Wikipedia); Skinner 1998 (⚠️ owner).
**Links:** PT-4004, PT-4007, PT-4030, PT-4033, PT-4034.
**Trap:** Presenting non-domination as Gauba's third liberty ❌; attributing Pettit's technical apparatus to Skinner ❌.
**Mains hook:** A three-family comparison, showing that reducing domination partly dissolves the liberty-equality trade-off.
**Back:** *"Third family: not interfered with, yet unfree while someone holds the leash."*

### Flashcard PT-4033 — The benevolent-master case
**Front:** What does the slave-with-a-kindly-master case show?
**Definition (learn):** A master who is kind and never interferes still leaves the slave unfree on the republican view. The negative view can record non-interference only on specified options and should not call the slave free simpliciter; the positive view says the slave lacks self-mastery or enabling conditions (⚠️ owner 18 §22.3).
**Breakdown:**
- Negative: narrow non-interference; standing threats constrain action.
- Positive: unfree by lack of self-mastery.
- Republican: unfree as a matter of status (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Illustration only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4032, PT-4004, PT-4006.
**Trap:** Making claims about the slave's subjective feelings ❌ (⚠️ owner warning).
**Mains hook:** The fastest illustration that the negative/positive dichotomy is not exhaustive.
**Back:** *"Kindness is not freedom while the leash remains."*

### Flashcard PT-4034 — Synthesis: equal liberty, substantive equality, function-bound property
**Front:** How do liberty, equality and property check one another?
**Definition (learn):** Liberty becomes universal only as equal liberty. Equality becomes real only when substantive, while allowing differential treatment for relevant need or function. Property is defensible only when it cannot convert into unaccountable power over others (✅ owner 18 §15; synthesis ⚠️ owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Unregulated property can destroy substantive equality.
- Equality without rights, due process or responsibility can become arbitrary.
- Tawney's function test and Nozick's entitlement test remain rival standards (⚠️ owner 18 §15).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.369–371, 392–398, 413–430 (✅ owner 18).
**Links:** PT-4001, PT-4013, PT-4019, PT-4028, PT-4032, PT-4036.
**Trap:** Presenting this as a canonical doctrine ❌ (owner says "proposed synthesis").
**Mains hook:** One-line spine: liberty is just only as equal liberty, equality is real only when substantive, property is defensible only when subordinated to welfare.
**Back:** *"Equal liberty, substantive equality, function-bound property."*

### Flashcard PT-4035 — Does positive liberty mean unlimited state command?
**Front:** Is positive liberty a licence for unlimited state authority?
**Definition (learn):** No. Positive liberty removes socially alterable obstacles to self-development; it does not authorise unlimited authority (✅ owner 18, PDF pp.379–381).
**Breakdown:**
- Gauba: not unlimited tutelage (✅ owner 18 advanced §12).
- Berlin's caution forbids claiming authority over a person's "true" purposes (✅ owner 18 §17 Objection 2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.379–381 (✅ owner 18).
**Links:** PT-4005, PT-4007, PT-4034.
**Trap:** "Positive liberty = more government" ❌.
**Mains hook:** Enabling conditions yes, dictated purposes no; use this as the answer's boundary sentence.
**Back:** *"Positive liberty removes barriers; it does not dictate the destination."*

### GROUP — Liberty, Equality and Property (advanced/18)

### Flashcard PT-4036 — Cumulative logic of the liberty-equality-property chapters
**Front:** How do the three advanced chapters on liberty, equality and property fit together?
**Definition (learn):** Gauba widens freedom beyond legal non-interference, widens equality beyond form, and then asks whether ownership makes either ideal real or hollow (✅ owner advanced 18 §1, PDF pp.375–381, 404–407, 413–430).
**Breakdown:**
- Liberty chapter: civil to economic liberty, then rival theories.
- Equality chapter: equal worth, formal to substantive, opportunity to outcome.
- Property chapter: labour, personhood, function, class power.
- Final advanced take: liberty without equality becomes privilege; equality without judgment becomes flattening; property without social control becomes power (✅ owner advanced 18 §1 map, ⚠️ phrasing).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba 5th ed. 2009 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4034, PT-4001, PT-4013, PT-4019.
**Trap:** Reading the three chapters as three separate slogans ❌ (✅ owner advanced 18 §2: must be read together).
**Mains hook:** Advanced answers treat the three ideals relationally, not in isolation.
**Back:** *"Liberty needs equality; equality needs judgment; property needs social control."*

### Flashcard PT-4037 — Hayek: political freedom neither necessary nor sufficient
**Front:** What precisely does Hayek claim about political freedom and individual freedom?
**Definition (learn):** Political freedom is neither necessary nor sufficient for individual freedom: a democracy may coerce, and a non-democracy may leave some private latitude (✅ owner advanced 18 §7, PDF p.381).
**Breakdown:**
- This is a conceptual claim, not an endorsement of non-democratic rule (✅ owner advanced 18 §7).
- Gauba's response: wealth structures access and the use of formal liberty (✅ owner advanced 18 §7, PDF pp.381–383, 409–412).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.381 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4010, PT-4011.
**Trap:** Treating the claim as anti-democratic ❌.
**Mains hook:** Separate Hayek's conceptual point from his policy conclusions.
**Back:** *"Hayek: democracy can coerce; autocracy can leave room. So freedom is not one thing."*

### Flashcard PT-4038 — Gauba's critique of Berlin in the material sphere
**Front:** Where does Gauba accept Berlin and where does he reject him?
**Definition (learn):** Gauba accepts self-mastery as a genuine issue in the moral sphere, but says poverty and exclusion in the material sphere can be socially produced and politically alterable, so they cannot be dismissed as private incapacity (✅ owner advanced 18 §7, PDF pp.378–380).
**Breakdown:**
- Accept: moral self-mastery is real.
- Reject: Berlin's narrow treatment of socially produced material deprivation.
- Berlin is not simply a Hayek-Nozick welfare critic (✅ owner advanced 18 §5, PDF pp.408–409).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.378–380 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4007, PT-4006, PT-4005.
**Trap:** Presenting Gauba as rejecting Berlin entirely ❌.
**Mains hook:** A precise two-part reply: accept the moral sense, reject the material narrowing.
**Back:** *"Gauba: Berlin right about the soul, too narrow about the stomach."*

### Flashcard PT-4039 — Dworkin: equality of resources
**Front:** What is Dworkin's equality of resources?
**Definition (learn):** Equality tested through hypothetical auction and insurance, which separates brute luck (neutralised) from option luck (responsibility retained). Equal concern is not identical welfare or outcome (⚠️ owner basic 18 §6 and advanced 18 §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Envy-test and hypothetical insurance as devices.
- Distinguishes brute and option luck.
- Responsibility-sensitive metric is hard to translate into policy (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️ Dworkin's book dates not in owner).
**Links:** PT-4015, PT-4087.
**Trap:** Equating equality of resources with equal welfare ❌.
**Mains hook:** Use the brute-option luck distinction to qualify outcome-based egalitarianism.
**Back:** *"Dworkin: neutralise brute luck; keep responsibility for option luck."*

### Flashcard PT-4040 — Trap: liberty and equality are natural enemies
**Front:** Are liberty and equality natural enemies?
**Definition (learn):** No. Gauba treats them as complementary once liberty means equal liberty and equality means reasonable, not literal, equalisation (✅ owner basic 18 §9; advanced §12).
**Breakdown:**
- Equal liberty: one person's freedom may not destroy another's.
- Reasonable equality: differential treatment by need and useful excellence.
- The opposition is strongest only when each is defined in its extreme form (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.392–393, 407–410 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4001, PT-4013, PT-4043.
**Trap:** Assuming a zero-sum opposition ❌.
**Mains hook:** Start the answer by defining both ideals in their reasonable form.
**Back:** *"Liberty and equality are partners when each is reasonable."*

### Flashcard PT-4041 — The formally voluntary but substantively unequal contract
**Front:** Why can a labour contract be formally voluntary yet substantively unequal?
**Definition (learn):** A contract signed under desperation is formally consensual, but unequal bargaining power can hollow out the liberty it appears to express (⚠️ owner basic 18 §7; advanced §9; Hobhouse's point against "freedom of contract" without approximate bargaining equality, ✅ owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Test: is the issue contractual freedom or unequal power?
- Links to formal versus substantive equality (PT-4014).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Illustration (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4003, PT-4014, PT-4011.
**Trap:** "A signed contract proves free choice" ❌.
**Mains hook:** Use desperation and bargaining power to show liberty as power, not only as absence of interference.
**Back:** *"A signed contract under desperation is consent with no bargaining."*

### Flashcard PT-4042 — Natural-right property and its instability
**Front:** Why does property defended as a natural right become unstable?
**Definition (learn):** Property is defended as a right recognised rather than created by the state; Gauba shows this becomes unstable once inheritance, money and large-scale inequality enter (✅ owner advanced 18 §4, PDF pp.417–419).
**Breakdown:**
- Locke's labour title is the origin.
- Money and inheritance let holdings outrun the original intuition (✅ owner advanced §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.417–419 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4021, PT-4017, PT-4019.
**Trap:** Treating natural-right defence as proof that present inequality is just ❌.
**Mains hook:** Shows an internal problem in liberal property theory.
**Back:** *"Natural right in theory; inheritance and money in practice."*

### Flashcard PT-4043 — PYQ route: liberty and equality questions
**Front:** Which verified PYQs anchor the liberty and equality chapters, and how should they be answered?
**Definition (learn):** Verified local PYQs in the owner set: 2025 (salient features of equality according to J.S. Mill; why liberty and equality remain inadequate without justice); 2024 (critically evaluate liberty and equality as political ideals); 2022 (complete liberty, inequality and the loss of freedom through order or restriction) (✅ owner basic 18 §9; advanced 18 §10).
**Breakdown:**
- Framework: define → separate negative liberty, self-mastery and enabling freedom → distinguish formal from substantive equality → apply the alterability test → reconcile through equal liberty and justice (⚠️ owner).
- Mill's equality: equal civic standing, not levelling (PT-4009).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQs 2022, 2024, 2025 (✅ owner local PYQ file).
**Links:** PT-4009, PT-4034, PT-4040, PT-4001.
**Trap:** Answering "liberty and equality" as two separate essays ❌.
**Mains hook:** Use the framework to reach a qualified conclusion through equal liberty.
**Back:** *"Define, separate, distinguish, test, reconcile."*

### GROUP — Justice: Concepts and Dimensions (basic/19)

### Flashcard PT-4044 — Justice as allocation under scarcity
**Front:** How does Gauba frame justice in the basic chapter?
**Definition (learn):** Justice is the fair allocation of benefits and burdens, including goods, services, opportunities, power, honours and obligations, where valued goods are scarce and criteria are contestable (✅ owner basic 19 §3, PDF pp.432–433). This is his chapter frame, not an exhaustive definition of every justice claim.
**Breakdown:**
- Works where arrangements can be publicly criticised and revised (open society) (✅ owner, PDF p.433).
- Corrective or penal justice need not take this allocative form (✅ owner basic 19 §4(1)).
- Justice then unfolds into legal, political and socio-economic dimensions (✅ owner, PDF pp.439–444).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.432–445 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** PT-4045, PT-4050, PT-4051, PT-4052, PT-4076.
**Trap:** Treating the allocation frame as the only definition of justice ❌.
**Mains hook:** Open with allocation under scarcity, then show why contestability requires an open society.
**Back:** *"Justice shares what is scarce, fairly, in an open society."*

### Flashcard PT-4045 — Justice as rightness, not usefulness
**Front:** Why does Gauba align justice with right and reasonable rather than useful or pleasant?
**Definition (learn):** Good/bad is a continuum of advantage; right/wrong is a dichotomy. Justice belongs to the field of rightness (✅ owner basic 19 §4(2), PDF pp.433–435).
**Breakdown:**
- Gauba's contrast is with utility-maximising reasoning that sacrifices rightness.
- It is not a universal refutation of utilitarian accounts of justice (✅ owner, Mill ⚠️ PT-4081).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.433–435 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** PT-4044, PT-4057, PT-4081.
**Trap:** "Gauba says utilitarianism cannot recognise justice" ❌ overstates the owner text.
**Mains hook:** Justice as rightness protects minorities from aggregate-advantage reasoning.
**Back:** *"Good is a scale; right is a line."*

### Flashcard PT-4046 — Justice is dynamic
**Front:** Why does Gauba say justice changes over time?
**Definition (learn):** Social consciousness changes, so arrangements once accepted as just can be condemned later. Examples in owner: slavery, serfdom, caste-based degradation (✅ owner basic 19 §4(3), PDF pp.435–436).
**Breakdown:**
- Reason works through historically changing social awareness, not timeless dogma (✅ owner advanced 19 §4).
- Attributed to D.D. Raphael (✅ owner basic 19 §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.435–436 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** PT-4047, PT-4079.
**Trap:** Reading "dynamic" as relativism ❌ (owner: "not relativism without reason").
**Mains hook:** Use historical reversals to argue that justice is judged by reasoned standards that grow with awareness.
**Back:** *"Yesterday's order can be tomorrow's injustice."*

### Flashcard PT-4047 — Ernest Barker: justice orders the three values
**Front:** What is Barker's integrating claim about justice?
**Definition (learn):** Justice orders liberty, equality and fraternity into one coherent arrangement rather than leaving them as separate slogans (✅ owner basic 19 §4(4), PDF pp.436–438).
**Breakdown:**
- Liberty: equal and regulated (✅ owner §8).
- Equality: must go beyond formal non-discrimination where structural deprivation persists.
- Fraternity: protection of weaker sections as a right, not charity (✅ owner §8).
- Law gains force when authority (validity) joins justice (value) (✅ owner advanced §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.436–440 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** PT-4074, PT-4048, PT-4034.
**Trap:** Listing the three values in sequence without ordering them ❌.
**Mains hook:** The answer should state the ordering principle, not just recite the triad.
**Back:** *"Barker: justice is the thread through the three values."*

### Flashcard PT-4048 — Justice according to law versus law according to justice
**Front:** What is the difference between justice according to law and law according to justice?
**Definition (learn):** Justice according to law asks whether decisions follow existing law by general, impartial and efficient application (Alf Ross). Law according to justice asks whether the law itself has moral value (Ernest Barker) (✅ owner basic 19 §5, PDF pp.439–440).
**Breakdown:**
- Ross: fair administration under existing law.
- Barker: law must be valid and valuable; authority plus justice.
- Gauba prefers Barker's view (✅ owner advanced 19 §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.439–440 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4049, PT-4047, PT-4058, PT-4078.
**Trap:** Treating a valid law as automatically just ❌.
**Mains hook:** Use the distinction to evaluate whether a lawful but oppressive rule is just.
**Back:** *"Ross asks 'is it applied fairly?'; Barker asks 'is it fair to apply?'"*

### Flashcard PT-4049 — Alf Ross and justice as impartial administration
**Front:** What does Alf Ross represent in the justice debate?
**Definition (learn):** Justice means conformity with existing law through general rules correctly and impartially applied, avoiding appeal to extra-legal moral absolutes (✅ owner basic 19 §6; advanced 19 §5, PDF pp.439–440).
**Breakdown:**
- Strength: protects against arbitrariness (✅ owner advanced 19 §7).
- Weakness: impeccably administered oppressive law can still block progress (✅ owner advanced 19 §7, PDF p.440).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.439–440 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4048, PT-4078.
**Trap:** Presenting Ross as endorsing unjust law ❌.
**Mains hook:** Formalism protects equal treatment but cannot judge the content of law.
**Back:** *"Ross: rules fairly run. Gauba: rules can still be wrong."*

### Flashcard PT-4050 — Legal justice
**Front:** What does legal justice cover?
**Definition (learn):** Fair administration according to existing law and/or testing the law itself against standards of justice (✅ owner basic 19 §3, PDF pp.439–440).
**Breakdown:**
- Without it, authority becomes arbitrary or morally empty (✅ owner §8).
- Court access matters: a law impartially applied can still bar the poor (⚠️ owner §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.439–440 (✅ owner 19).
**Links:** PT-4048, PT-4051, PT-4052.
**Trap:** Treating legal regularity as the whole of legal justice ❌.
**Mains hook:** Legal justice has two senses: administration and content.
**Back:** *"Legal justice: applied fairly, and fair to apply."*

### Flashcard PT-4051 — Political justice
**Front:** What does political justice require?
**Definition (learn):** Transforming institutions, rights and procedures so political power reflects the people rather than privileged groups (✅ owner basic 19 §3, PDF pp.440–441).
**Breakdown:**
- Democratic institutions, rule of law, constitutional government, free criticism, freedom of association (✅ owner §8, advanced §3).
- Money power can make elections formal but not meaningful (⚠️ owner advanced 19 §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.440–441 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4050, PT-4052.
**Trap:** Equating regular elections with political justice ❌.
**Mains hook:** Political justice is substantive when ordinary citizens can compete meaningfully.
**Back:** *"Political justice: power answers to the many, not the privileged few."*

### Flashcard PT-4052 — Socio-economic justice
**Front:** What does socio-economic justice require?
**Definition (learn):** Reordering social and economic relations so exploitation is checked and the material and moral gains of social life reach weaker sections (✅ owner basic 19 §3, PDF pp.441–442).
**Breakdown:**
- Property subordinated to the common good (✅ owner advanced 19 §5).
- Economic disparity can hollow out legal and political justice (✅ owner basic 19 §8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.441–442 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4050, PT-4051, PT-4019.
**Trap:** Reducing socio-economic justice to charity ❌ (PT-4073).
**Mains hook:** Economic injustice erodes the other dimensions from beneath.
**Back:** *"Socio-economic justice: dignity and material share for the weaker."*

### Flashcard PT-4053 — Procedural justice
**Front:** What is procedural justice?
**Definition (learn):** Justice secured by fair rules, impartial application and absence of force or fraud, often modelled on market or competition rules (✅ owner basic 19 §3, PDF pp.442–444).
**Breakdown:**
- Image: a fair race where cheating is disallowed (✅ owner §5).
- Gauba does not reject due process; he rejects procedure as sufficient (✅ owner §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.442–444 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4054, PT-4058, PT-4059, PT-4063.
**Trap:** A procedurally regular result is automatically just ❌.
**Mains hook:** Procedure is necessary but not sufficient; show how background inequality makes a fair race a facade.
**Back:** *"Procedural: fair rules, no cheating. Necessary, not sufficient."*

### Flashcard PT-4054 — Substantive and distributive justice
**Front:** What does substantive or distributive justice test?
**Definition (learn):** Whether actual arrangements improve the condition and life-chances of the poor and underprivileged, not procedure alone (✅ owner basic 19 §3, PDF pp.442–445). Terms are not universally synonymous across theory (✅ owner).
**Breakdown:**
- May require altering background structures, not only policing rules (✅ owner §5).
- Minimum needs first (PT-4060).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.442–445 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4053, PT-4060, PT-4061.
**Trap:** Treating the two terms as identical in all theories ❌ (owner caution).
**Mains hook:** Substantive justice asks whether the rules deliver for the weak.
**Back:** *"Substantive asks: did the weak gain a real chance?"*

### Flashcard PT-4055 — Social justice in Gauba's usage
**Front:** How does Gauba define social justice?
**Definition (learn):** A progressive demand to transform oppressive social conditions and secure what is due to the underprivileged from organised social life (✅ owner basic 19 §3, PDF pp.438–442).
**Breakdown:**
- Shift from the virtuous individual in a fixed order to the just society that transforms conditions (✅ owner basic 19 §4(5), advanced §1).
- Traditional justice (Plato) vs social justice (PT-4056, PT-4077).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.438–442 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4077, PT-4073, PT-4052.
**Trap:** "Social justice = welfare charity" ❌ (PT-4073).
**Mains hook:** Social justice is structural and rights-based.
**Back:** *"Social justice changes the order, not just the alms."*

### Flashcard PT-4056 — Plato: justice as functional harmony
**Front:** What is Plato's justice in Gauba's account?
**Definition (learn):** Each part of the soul and each class in the state performs its proper function under the rule of reason; a classic conservative or functional view (✅ owner basic 19 §6, PDF pp.438–439).
**Breakdown:**
- Begins from an ordered division of roles (✅ owner advanced 19 §7).
- Not closed hereditary caste: limited movement by aptitude is conceptually possible (✅ owner advanced 19 §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.438–439 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4055, PT-4077, PT-4078.
**Trap:** Equating Plato's order with modern social justice ❌ (owner advanced §12).
**Mains hook:** Plato defines justice as right performance in hierarchy; modern justice asks whether the hierarchy itself is just.
**Back:** *"Plato: each does their function under reason. Modern: is the function structure fair?"*

### Flashcard PT-4057 — Bentham and the utilitarian contrast
**Front:** How does Gauba use Bentham against justice-as-utility?
**Definition (learn):** Utility balances pleasure and pain through felicific calculation; Gauba treats it as insufficient for justice as rightness (✅ owner basic 19 §6, PDF pp.434–435).
**Breakdown:**
- Utility can answer benefit and harm but not whether a result is right (✅ owner advanced 19 §5).
- Utility vs justice matrix (✅ owner advanced 19 §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.434–435 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4045, PT-4081.
**Trap:** Stating that Bentham has no theory of rights ❌ (not in owner; avoid).
**Mains hook:** Justice cannot be reduced to aggregate advantage.
**Back:** *"Bentham counts happiness; Gauba asks whether it is right."*

### Flashcard PT-4058 — Trap: valid law is just
**Front:** Is justice simply whatever the law says?
**Definition (learn):** No. Gauba distinguishes legal validity from moral value and prefers a view where law must also answer to justice (✅ owner basic 19 §9, PDF p.440).
**Breakdown:**
- Validity: authority to issue law.
- Value: moral content (✅ owner advanced 19 §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.440 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4048, PT-4047.
**Trap:** The trap itself: "Valid law therefore just" ❌.
**Mains hook:** A law can be authoritative and still unjust; ask about its content.
**Back:** *"Valid is not the same as just."*

### Flashcard PT-4059 — Trap: procedural fairness settles everything
**Front:** Does a fair-looking procedure guarantee a just outcome?
**Definition (learn):** No. Procedure can operate inside severe inequality and leave outcomes unjust (✅ owner basic 19 §9, PDF pp.442–445).
**Breakdown:**
- Substantive justice does not abolish procedure (✅ owner §9).
- Practical synthesis: "procedure in the service of a substantively defensible order" (⚠️ owner advanced 19 §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.442–445 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4053, PT-4054, PT-4060, PT-4083.
**Trap:** Using "procedure only" or "outcome only" without an ordering ❌.
**Mains hook:** Show how procedure and substance check each other.
**Back:** *"Fair rules, fair purpose; neither alone."*

### Flashcard PT-4060 — Minimum needs before desert
**Front:** What is Gauba's closing balance between need and desert?
**Definition (learn):** Minimum needs such as subsistence, health care, education and work opportunities must be secured before desert-based differences are defended (✅ owner basic 19 §7, PDF pp.444–445).
**Breakdown:**
- Need-first position (✅ owner basic 19 §14).
- Incentive objection is contested, not established (⚠️ owner).
- Progressive taxation can finance a need floor while leaving market returns above it (⚠️ owner §18).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.444–445 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4066, PT-4067, PT-4068, PT-4054.
**Trap:** Desert alone as the test of reward ❌.
**Mains hook:** Floors first, differential rewards second.
**Back:** *"Feed, heal, teach, then reward."*

### Flashcard PT-4061 — Aristotle: distributive justice
**Front:** What is Aristotelian distributive justice?
**Definition (learn):** Allocates goods, honours and offices in geometric proportion to a relevant standard of worth, so equals get equal shares and unequals proportionally unequal shares (⚠️ owner basic 19 §12; standard reading, not externally verified).
**Breakdown:**
- Different constitutions choose different standards of worth (⚠️ owner).
- Root of Gauba's "fair allocation" frame (⚠️ owner).
- Need-based allocation is not one of Aristotle's own coordinate criteria (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4062, PT-4066, PT-4044.
**Trap:** Presenting Aristotle as endorsing need-based allocation ❌.
**Mains hook:** Distributive justice sets the starting allocation; name the standard of worth.
**Back:** *"Aristotle: proportion to worth, by the standard the polity chooses."*

### Flashcard PT-4062 — Aristotle: corrective justice
**Front:** What is corrective or rectificatory justice?
**Definition (learn):** Restores arithmetic equality after a wrong in a bilateral transaction, whether voluntary (contract) or involuntary (theft, injury, fraud), regardless of the parties' merit or status (⚠️ owner basic 19 §12).
**Breakdown:**
- Root of restitution and compensation remedies (⚠️ owner).
- Does not settle whether the prior distribution was fair (⚠️ owner).
- Not a synonym for retributive punishment or restorative justice (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4061, PT-4065, PT-4075.
**Trap:** Conflating corrective with distributive justice ❌.
**Mains hook:** Distributive sets the starting point; corrective maintains transactions.
**Back:** *"Corrective fixes the wrong; distributive sets the shares."*

### Flashcard PT-4063 — Hayek: justice as conduct within rules
**Front:** What is Hayek's procedural objection to patterned justice?
**Definition (learn):** Justice or injustice properly describes individual conduct within known rules, not the unplanned pattern produced by a spontaneous market order. Calling the pattern just or unjust is a category mistake on his view (⚠️ owner basic 19 §13).
**Breakdown:**
- Hayek still wants general, impartial rules (⚠️ owner).
- Gauba groups him with procedural positions more closely than his doctrine warrants (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4010, PT-4053, PT-4064, PT-4065.
**Trap:** Saying Hayek is indifferent to rules ❌.
**Mains hook:** Hayek's rule-centred objection to distributive assessment.
**Back:** *"Hayek: judge the conduct, not the unplanned pattern."*

### Flashcard PT-4064 — Friedman: voluntary exchange as fair procedure
**Front:** What is Friedman's procedural test?
**Definition (learn):** Voluntary market exchange under freedom of contract is itself the fair procedure. If an exchange is uncoerced, its outcome is legitimate regardless of inequality (⚠️ owner basic 19 §13).
**Breakdown:**
- Gauba's Macpherson objection: background inequality can make "voluntary" exchange substantively coercive (✅ owner basic 19 §13, PDF pp.442–445).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4011, PT-4063, PT-4053.
**Trap:** Treating uncoerced as fair under any background ❌.
**Mains hook:** Test whether the exchange is free in substance, not only in form.
**Back:** *"Friedman: fair if uncoerced. Gauba: uncoerced by whom, from what start?"*

### Flashcard PT-4065 — Nozick as procedural entitlement theorist
**Front:** Why does Nozick belong in the procedural-justice debate?
**Definition (learn):** Nozick supplies a historical entitlement test: a distribution is just only if its history satisfies acquisition, transfer and rectification (✅ owner basic 19 §13; ✅ Wikipedia: *Anarchy, State, and Utopia*, 1974).
**Breakdown:**
- Unlike Hayek's spontaneous order or Friedman's exchange emphasis, Nozick is historical (✅ owner §13).
- Rectification answers the charge of locking in inequality (✅ owner §17 Objection 2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1974, Basic Books (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-4028, PT-4029, PT-4063.
**Trap:** "Nozick blesses any current distribution" ❌ (PT-4029).
**Mains hook:** Compare Nozick's historical test with Rawls's ex-ante principles.
**Back:** *"Nozick: a holding is clean only if its whole history is."*

### Flashcard PT-4066 — Merit as a distributive criterion
**Front:** What does merit reward, and what is its weakness?
**Definition (learn):** Demonstrated ability, achievement or performance, such as examination rank or productivity (⚠️ owner basic 19 §14).
**Breakdown:**
- Aristotle's proportional logic and liberal procedural theories lean on it (⚠️ owner).
- Weakness: unequal starting conditions shape who can demonstrate merit (⚠️ owner; substantive-justice worry).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4061, PT-4060, PT-4067, PT-4068.
**Trap:** Treating examination rank as a pure measure of desert ❌.
**Mains hook:** Ask whether the merit test is itself fair to the starting position.
**Back:** *"Merit rewards demonstrated ability, not the starting line."*

### Flashcard PT-4067 — Need as a distributive criterion
**Front:** What does need reward, and what objection is raised?
**Definition (learn):** Basic requirements for a decent life, such as subsistence, health and education, regardless of contribution (⚠️ owner basic 19 §14).
**Breakdown:**
- Gauba's conclusion that minimum needs come first is a need-first position (✅ owner, PDF pp.444–445).
- The incentive objection to pure need allocation is contested (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.444–445 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4060, PT-4066, PT-4068.
**Trap:** Assuming need-based schemes provably reduce effort ❌.
**Mains hook:** Need sets the floor; the incentive question is empirical and contested.
**Back:** *"Need sets the floor; incentives are the argument."*

### Flashcard PT-4068 — Desert as a distributive criterion
**Front:** What is desert, and why is it hard to verify?
**Definition (learn):** Moral entitlement earned through effort, sacrifice or voluntary contribution, distinct from ability (merit) or requirement (need) (⚠️ owner basic 19 §14).
**Breakdown:**
- Underlies popular resistance to unearned reward (⚠️ owner).
- Hard to verify because effort is shaped by unchosen circumstance (⚠️ owner; Rawls treats desert cautiously).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4060, PT-4066, PT-4067, PT-4069.
**Trap:** Treating effort as purely self-made ❌.
**Mains hook:** Desert claims need to be separated from luck.
**Back:** *"Desert is earned, but how much of earning is luck?"*

### Flashcard PT-4069 — Rawls bridge: procedure to substance
**Front:** What is the procedural-substantive bridge in Rawls, as flagged in the basic owner?
**Definition (learn):** Rawls uses a fair procedure (original position behind a veil of ignorance) to derive two principles. The first secures equal basic liberties with lexical priority. The second contains fair equality of opportunity, which precedes the difference principle (✅ owner basic 19 §15; ✅ Wikipedia: *A Theory of Justice*).
**Breakdown:**
- The framework has distributive consequences but is not simply "substantively redistributive" (✅ owner §15).
- Full architecture: PT-4086 to PT-4089 (basic/20 cards).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1971 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-4053, PT-4054, PT-4068, PT-4087, PT-4089.
**Trap:** Forcing a strict procedural-or-substantive either-or ❌.
**Mains hook:** Rawls is the bridge figure between procedure and substance.
**Back:** *"Rawls: fair procedure, substantive principles."*

### Flashcard PT-4070 — Feminist link: unpaid care and merit
**Front:** Why does the justice debate need a feminist lens?
**Definition (learn):** Distributive frameworks built on individual holdings can overlook unpaid domestic and care labour and the gendered division of household work that shapes who can compete for merit-based rewards (⚠️ owner basic 19 §16; full treatment in the Feminism file).
**Breakdown:**
- Links merit to unequal starting positions (PT-4066).
- Full development belongs to the Feminism owner (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4066, PT-4054, PT-4096.
**Trap:** Presenting feminist critique as fully resolved here ❌.
**Mains hook:** Gendered care labour changes who can meet merit tests.
**Back:** *"Merit counts the paid; care work stays off the ledger."*

### Flashcard PT-4071 — Sen's capability as informational space
**Front:** How does Sen's capability approach extend the justice debate?
**Definition (learn):** Capability is the informational space for judging the real freedoms people have. Sen's method is comparative and realisation-focused: how remediable injustice in actual lives can be reduced through public reasoning (⚠️ owner basic 19 §16). Sen's *The Idea of Justice* (2009, Allen Lane / Harvard) (✅ Wikipedia), which contrasts transcendental institutionalism with comparison of remediable injustice (✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Nussbaum's capability approach is related but not identical (✅ owner).
- Supplementary extension, not Gauba's content (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2009 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-4069, PT-4072, PT-4063.
**Trap:** Treating Sen and Nussbaum as the same ❌.
**Mains hook:** Sen refines the procedural-substantive distinction by asking about real freedoms.
**Back:** *"Sen: judge real freedoms, compare remediable injustice."*

### Flashcard PT-4072 — Fraser: redistribution and recognition
**Front:** What does Nancy Fraser add to the justice debate?
**Definition (learn):** Fraser analyses injustice through redistribution and recognition under the aim of parity of participation. Her mature framework also adds representation (⚠️ owner basic 19 §16; not externally verified).
**Breakdown:**
- Not the originator of every recognition theory (✅ owner).
- Used as boundary marker where distribution needs status and representation analysis (✅ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4071, PT-4069, PT-4084.
**Trap:** Treating recognition as Gauba's own term ❌ (advanced 19/20 warnings).
**Mains hook:** Parity of participation: can persons interact as peers across distribution, status and representation.
**Back:** *"Fraser: parity needs goods, status and voice."*

### Flashcard PT-4073 — Trap: social justice is welfare charity
**Front:** Does social justice mean welfare charity?
**Definition (learn):** No. Gauba treats it as a structural and rights-based transformation of social conditions for the oppressed and underprivileged (✅ owner basic 19 §9, PDF pp.438–442).
**Breakdown:**
- Charity leaves structures intact; social justice changes them.
- Fraternity is a right of the weaker party (✅ owner §8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.438–442 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4055, PT-4052, PT-4047.
**Trap:** The trap itself ❌.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish charity from entitlement to structural change.
**Back:** *"Social justice is structure, not alms."*

### Flashcard PT-4074 — Trap: liberty, equality and fraternity as slogans
**Front:** Are liberty, equality and fraternity separate slogans to be listed?
**Definition (learn):** No. Barker's central point is that justice orders and reconciles them into one scheme (✅ owner basic 19 §9, PDF pp.436–438).
**Breakdown:**
- Answer shows the ordering, not the list (✅ owner §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.436–438 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4047, PT-4034.
**Trap:** The trap itself ❌.
**Mains hook:** State the ordering principle at the start.
**Back:** *"Three values, one order."*

### Flashcard PT-4075 — Indian applied structure: reservation and remedies (cautious)
**Front:** How can the justice lenses apply to Indian illustrations without Polity content?
**Definition (learn):** Reservation can be read through substantive equality, representation, anti-subordination and historical exclusion rather than need alone; judicial compensation and restitution illustrate corrective justice (⚠️ owner basic 19 §18).
**Breakdown:**
- Progressive taxation can fund a need floor while the tax itself does not reward merit or desert (⚠️ owner).
- Article detail and case law stay with Polity (⚠️ owner boundary).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None asserted (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4062, PT-4067, PT-4014, PT-4018.
**Trap:** Reproducing constitutional article detail in a justice answer ❌.
**Mains hook:** A cautious Indian illustration as the 20-mark closing move.
**Back:** *"Reservation as substance, remedy as corrective; articles belong to Polity."*

### Flashcard PT-4076 — Qualified-thesis templates for justice answers
**Front:** How should a justice answer be structured by mark weight?
**Definition (learn):** Qualified thesis plus named limit plus counter-qualification. 10 marks: one thesis, one limit (Macpherson or Section 4 point 7). 15 marks: add a distributive criterion and one objection-reply. 20 marks: add Aristotle's distributive/corrective pair, the Rawls bridge, and an Indian illustration (✅ owner basic 19 §19, §21; ⚠️ word limits are PSIR scaffolds).
**Breakdown:**
- Directives: examine (dimensions and criteria), discuss (definition → theories → critique → position), evaluate (objection-reply, qualified judgement), elucidate (illustration), distinguish (comparison table) (✅ owner §19).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Word ranges ⚠️ (~150 / ~200 / ~250).
**Links:** PT-4044, PT-4053, PT-4069, PT-4075.
**Trap:** Ending on a flat claim ❌.
**Mains hook:** Always end with a graded verdict.
**Back:** *"Thesis, limit, counter, verdict."*

### GROUP — Justice: Concepts and Dimensions (advanced/19)

### Flashcard PT-4077 — From traditional to social justice
**Front:** How does Gauba move from traditional justice to social justice?
**Definition (learn):** Traditional justice centres on the just man performing role-specific duty in a given order (Plato). Social justice centres on transforming the unjust order itself (✅ owner advanced 19 §1, §6, PDF pp.438–442).
**Breakdown:**
- Gauba clearly prefers the modern/social orientation (✅ owner advanced 19 §6).
- Plato's hierarchy is conservative because it begins from ordered roles, not transformation (✅ owner advanced §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.438–442 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4055, PT-4056.
**Trap:** Treating Plato and social justice as the same because both seek order ❌ (✅ owner advanced §12).
**Mains hook:** Order versus transformation is the axis of the answer.
**Back:** *"Plato asks 'do your job'; social justice asks 'change the job's rules'."*

### Flashcard PT-4078 — Ross: strength and weakness of formalism
**Front:** What is the strength and weakness of Alf Ross's formalism?
**Definition (learn):** Strength: general rules and impartial application protect against arbitrariness. Weakness: oppressive or stagnant law can be impeccably administered while still blocking social progress (✅ owner advanced 19 §7, PDF pp.439–440).
**Breakdown:**
- Procedure-based legitimacy does not judge the content of law.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.440 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4049, PT-4048.
**Trap:** Dismissing Ross as irrelevant ❌.
**Mains hook:** Use Ross to show that formal correctness is necessary and insufficient.
**Back:** *"Ross protects against whim; he cannot judge the rule itself."*

### Flashcard PT-4079 — Barker and identifying justice
**Front:** How does Gauba answer the question of how justice is identified, on Barker's view?
**Definition (learn):** Barker's view restores value to law but raises the question of how justice is to be identified. Gauba answers through reason informed by modern social consciousness rather than timeless dogma (✅ owner advanced 19 §7, PDF pp.435–440).
**Breakdown:**
- Authority gives law validity; justice gives it value (✅ owner advanced 19 §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.435–440 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4047, PT-4046.
**Trap:** Equating "modern consciousness" with majority opinion ❌ (owner says reason is the test).
**Mains hook:** Justice is reasoned judgement, not popularity.
**Back:** *"Barker: authority makes law, justice makes it worth obeying."*

### Flashcard PT-4080 — Herbert Spencer and the harsh limit of procedure
**Front:** Why does Gauba cite Herbert Spencer?
**Definition (learn):** Spencer pushes procedural justice to a harsh limit by resisting aid to the weak and importing survival-of-the-fittest logic into civil society (✅ owner advanced 19 §5, PDF pp.443–444).
**Breakdown:**
- Shows where pure procedure leads when substantive concern is absent.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.443–444 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4053, PT-4059.
**Trap:** Presenting Spencer as a mainstream procedural liberal ❌.
**Mains hook:** Procedure without any substantive floor can license neglect of the weak.
**Back:** *"Spencer: fair rules, and let the weak fall."*

### Flashcard PT-4081 — Mill and utilitarian justice
**Front:** Does utilitarianism make justice impossible, on Gauba's account?
**Definition (learn):** No. Mill develops a utilitarian account of justice and gives liberty special protection within it, so Gauba's criticism targets reduction of justice to aggregate advantage, not every utilitarian treatment of rights (⚠️ owner basic 19 §6, advanced 19 §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Mill's harm principle (PT-4009) is a rights-protective element.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-only (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4045, PT-4057, PT-4009.
**Trap:** Overstating Gauba's critique of utilitarianism ❌.
**Mains hook:** Narrow the critique to aggregate reduction.
**Back:** *"Mill's utility protects rights; Gauba's target is aggregate reduction."*

### Flashcard PT-4082 — Macpherson against market proceduralism
**Front:** How does Macpherson challenge market-centred procedural justice?
**Definition (learn):** Capitalist conditions can destroy ordinary people's creative freedom, thus the background fairness of competition (✅ owner advanced 19 §5, PDF pp.443–445).
**Breakdown:**
- Procedural fairness inside destroyed capacities is hollow.
- Links to developmental power (PT-4027).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.443–445 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4027, PT-4053, PT-4064.
**Trap:** Treating Macpherson as only a welfare critic ❌.
**Mains hook:** The fair race is unfair if runners' capacities are eroded.
**Back:** *"Macpherson: the race is rigged where capacities are eroded."*

### Flashcard PT-4083 — Procedure in the service of a substantively defensible order
**Front:** What is the practical synthesis Gauba's justice chapters point towards?
**Definition (learn):** Not "procedure or substance", but procedure in the service of a substantively defensible social order (⚠️ owner advanced 19 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Necessary-but-insufficient logic for procedure (✅ owner advanced §8).
- Minimum needs secured before desert (PT-4060).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Synthesis (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4059, PT-4060, PT-4053.
**Trap:** Presenting the synthesis as settled ❌.
**Mains hook:** Final sentence: procedure is the means; justice is the end.
**Back:** *"Procedure serves justice; it does not replace it."*

### Flashcard PT-4084 — Justice orders liberty, equality and fraternity
**Front:** How should an answer treat the claim "justice is not liberty, equality and fraternity separately, but their proper ordering"?
**Definition (learn):** Treat it as a prompt to show the ordering: liberty qualified by equality, and equality further qualified by fraternity (✅ owner advanced 19 §1 map, §10 prompt 3 ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Liberty must be equal and regulated.
- Equality must be substantive, especially in the economic sphere.
- Fraternity protects the weaker party (✅ owner advanced §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Synthesis (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4047, PT-4074, PT-4034.
**Trap:** Reciting the triad ❌.
**Mains hook:** State the ordering principle first, then test it on one case.
**Back:** *"Liberty qualified by equality; equality qualified by fraternity."*

### GROUP — Diverse Perspectives on Justice (basic/20)

### Flashcard PT-4085 — Rawls: primary goods
**Front:** What are "primary goods" in Rawls's distributive scheme?
**Definition (learn):** Rights, liberties, powers, opportunities, income, wealth and the social bases of self-respect (✅ owner basic 20 §3, PDF p.447).
**Breakdown:**
- These are the objects principles of justice distribute.
- Walzer objects to lumping diverse goods into this single category (✅ Wikipedia: *Spheres of Justice*).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.447 (✅ owner 20).
**Links:** PT-4086, PT-4099, PT-4088.
**Trap:** Treating primary goods as only money ❌.
**Mains hook:** Primary goods let Rawls compare persons without judging their conceptions of the good.
**Back:** *"Primary goods: rights, opportunities, income, self-respect."*

### Flashcard PT-4086 — Original position and veil of ignorance
**Front:** What do the original position and the veil of ignorance do in Rawls?
**Definition (learn):** The original position is a hypothetical starting point where rational persons choose principles without knowing their social place. The veil of ignorance removes knowledge of wants, talents, class and discriminatory markers (✅ owner basic 20 §3, PDF p.447).
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: choose fair principles, not reflect on actual selves (✅ owner basic 20 §14 reply to Sandel).
- Under uncertainty, risk-averse choice protects the worst-off position (maximin reasoning) (⚠️ owner basic 20 §13).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.447 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4085, PT-4087, PT-4100.
**Trap:** Treating the veil as a metaphysical claim about persons ❌ (owner reply to Sandel).
**Mains hook:** Define both devices before listing the principles, as the PYQ demands.
**Back:** *"Behind the veil, nobody knows their rank, so all choose fairly."*

### Flashcard PT-4087 — Rawls: two principles and lexical priority
**Front:** What are Rawls's two principles and in what order are they applied?
**Definition (learn):** First principle: each person has an equal claim to the most extensive compatible system of equal basic liberties. Second principle: social and economic inequalities must be to the greatest benefit of the least advantaged and attached to offices open under fair equality of opportunity (✅ Wikipedia: *A Theory of Justice*; ✅ owner basic 20 §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Lexical order: first principle, then fair equality of opportunity, then the difference principle (✅ Wikipedia).
- Gauba presents the second principle's parts as separate steps for teaching (✅ owner basic 20 §5, PDF pp.448–450).
- Basic liberties cannot be traded for economic gain (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** First edition 1971, Belknap Press (Harvard University Press) (✅ Wikipedia); revised edition 1999 as cited by the source (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4088, PT-4089, PT-4015, PT-4101.
**Trap:** Presenting the difference principle as the first principle ❌.
**Mains hook:** Priority is what separates Rawls from utilitarian trade-offs.
**Back:** *"Liberty first, fair chances second, difference third."*

### Flashcard PT-4088 — The difference principle
**Front:** What does the difference principle permit?
**Definition (learn):** Inequality is just only if it brings the greatest benefit to the least advantaged, and only after equal basic liberties and fair equality of opportunity are satisfied (✅ owner basic 20 §3, §5; ✅ Wikipedia "to the greatest benefit of the least advantaged").
**Breakdown:**
- Permits inequality; does not require identical outcomes (✅ owner §9).
- Benefit to the least advantaged alone does not justify inequality if liberties or opportunity are violated (⚠️ owner advanced 20 §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.448 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4087, PT-4101, PT-4112.
**Trap:** The difference principle alone justifies any inequality ❌.
**Mains hook:** Show the lexical constraints before invoking the difference principle.
**Back:** *"Inequality only if it lifts the bottom, after liberty and fair chances."*

### Flashcard PT-4089 — Rawls: justice as fairness
**Front:** What is "justice as fairness", and what does a PYQ answer need?
**Definition (learn):** Rawls's theory that principles of justice are those chosen in the original position: equal liberties, then fair opportunity and the difference principle, in lexical order (✅ owner basic 20 §6, §11).
**Breakdown:**
- Verified PYQ 2023, 10 marks: "What is meant by justice as fairness? Explain Rawls' theory of justice." Must include original position, veil, two principles and priority; add objections (✅ owner basic 20 §11, advanced 20 §10).
- Rawls's anti-utilitarian stance: no sacrifice of some for aggregate happiness (✅ owner basic 20 §7; PDF p.447).
- Also PYQ 2019 (Plato and Rawls) (✅ owner basic 20 §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQ 2023 10 marks (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4087, PT-4086, PT-4109.
**Trap:** Writing only the difference principle ❌ (owner PYQ trap).
**Mains hook:** Definition, devices, principles, priority, critique.
**Back:** *"Justice as fairness: fair choice, two principles, strict order."*

### Flashcard PT-4090 — Nozick: self-ownership and the minimal state
**Front:** What are the core elements of Nozick's libertarian justice?
**Definition (learn):** Self-ownership grounds persons' rights as side-constraints on how others, including the state, may treat them. Justice in holdings is historical: acquisition, transfer and rectification. Legitimate state is minimal (✅ owner basic 20 §6, §13; ✅ Wikipedia: *Anarchy, State, and Utopia*, 1974).
**Breakdown:**
- Taxing legitimately acquired income for redistribution is a rights violation on Nozick's view (✅ owner basic 20 §13).
- Nozick does propose a full theory of justice (✅ owner basic 20 §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1974, Basic Books (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-4028, PT-4091, PT-4092, PT-4102, PT-4103.
**Trap:** Nozick as anti-justice ❌.
**Mains hook:** Entitlement over end-state; rectification as the internal limit.
**Back:** *"Nozick: you own yourself; history, not pattern, decides holdings."*

### Flashcard PT-4091 — Wilt Chamberlain example
**Front:** What does the Wilt Chamberlain example show?
**Definition (learn):** Starting from a pattern that suits a preferred principle, repeated voluntary payments of 25 cents by a million people to watch Chamberlain produce a new, unpatterned distribution. Nozick argues the result is just, so patterned principles need continual interference with exchange (✅ owner basic 20 §7, PDF p.451; ✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Repeated voluntary transfers disrupt a preferred pattern (✅ owner §7).
- Shows that voluntary exchange can generate inequality without, on Nozick's view, injustice.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nozick 1974 (✅ Wikipedia); PDF p.451 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4090, PT-4092, PT-4088.
**Trap:** Assuming the example proves voluntary exchange always generates justice ❌ (it requires a just starting point).
**Mains hook:** Use the example to test any patterned principle, including the difference principle.
**Back:** *"Chamberlain's quarters: voluntary pennies, unpatterned outcome."*

### Flashcard PT-4092 — Water-source example and the acquisition proviso
**Front:** What does the water-source example teach about initial acquisition?
**Definition (learn):** Control over an indispensable natural resource cannot rightfully make others worse off, so initial acquisition is not morally unlimited (✅ owner basic 20 §7, PDF p.451).
**Breakdown:**
- Ties to Nozick's modified Lockean proviso (PT-4028).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.451 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4090, PT-4091, PT-4021.
**Trap:** Reading Nozick's acquisition as unlimited ❌.
**Mains hook:** The proviso prevents appropriation that worsens others' position.
**Back:** *"Water: no one may take the only well and leave others worse off."*

### Flashcard PT-4093 — Marx: surplus value and exploitation
**Front:** How does Marxian justice locate injustice?
**Definition (learn):** Injustice lies in exploitation: labour creates more value than the worker receives back as wages, and private ownership of major means of production allows appropriation of the remainder (✅ owner basic 20 §3, §7, PDF pp.453–455).
**Breakdown:**
- Marxist justice is not slightly better wages inside capitalism; it targets the structure (✅ owner §5).
- Communist distribution is by need; the classless order points beyond fair shares (✅ owner §8).
- Gauba: calling Marxism anti-justice is too strong (✅ owner §9, PDF p.453).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.453–459 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4025, PT-4094, PT-4104, PT-4120.
**Trap:** Marxism has nothing to do with justice ❌.
**Mains hook:** Exploitation, not inequality alone, is the wrong Marx names.
**Back:** *"Surplus value: labour's value kept by the owner."*

### Flashcard PT-4094 — Democratic socialism: Laski and Durbin
**Front:** What does democratic socialism add to Marxism?
**Definition (learn):** Social justice through democratic method, partial socialisation, welfare and public services, combined with civil liberties and party competition. Essential sectors may be publicly owned without wholesale abolition of property (✅ owner basic 20 §6, §8, PDF pp.459–461).
**Breakdown:**
- Refuses dictatorship; insists on freedom of thought and opposition rights (✅ owner §9, PDF pp.460–461).
- Institutionally fragile: needs public opinion, resources and incentive design (✅ owner advanced 20 §7, PDF p.461).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.459–461 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4093, PT-4105, PT-4116.
**Trap:** Democratic socialism = Marxism without elections ❌.
**Mains hook:** Middle path between revolution and laissez-faire.
**Back:** *"Democratic socialism: social ownership with free votes."*

### Flashcard PT-4095 — The anarchist family and justice
**Front:** Who are the anarchist thinkers in Gauba's chapter, and what do they share?
**Definition (learn):** Godwin, Proudhon, Bakunin, Kropotkin, Tolstoy, Gandhi and Bookchin. They distrust coercive authority and seek justice through decentralised, voluntary or morally regenerated association (✅ owner basic 20 §6, PDF pp.461–464).
**Breakdown:**
- The state is a coercive evil to be abolished or progressively diluted (✅ owner §8).
- Property often seen as domination, though strands differ (✅ owner §8).
- Criticised as impracticable and weak on regulation (✅ owner advanced 20 §7, PDF p.464).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.461–464 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4117, PT-4107, PT-4108.
**Trap:** Anarchism means chaos with no justice ❌ (owner describes an egalitarian voluntary order).
**Mains hook:** Use anarchism to show what the coercion critique can and cannot deliver.
**Back:** *"Anarchists trust association over authority."*

### Flashcard PT-4096 — Feminist corrective equality
**Front:** What do feminist theories add to justice?
**Definition (learn):** Equal rights plus corrective support against gendered disadvantage: education, representation, reservations, maternity benefits and protective measures. Early claims from Wollstonecraft and Mill (✅ owner basic 20 §6, §8, PDF pp.465–468).
**Breakdown:**
- Equality includes effective status, empowerment and participation (✅ owner §8).
- Historic exclusion from property and vote is part of the injustice (✅ owner §8).
- Gender indicators show health, education and power matter, not only formal rights (✅ owner §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.465–468 (✅ owner); development indicators are book-period (✅ owner advanced 20 §12).
**Links:** PT-4106, PT-4119, PT-4070.
**Trap:** Feminism demands identical treatment only ❌.
**Mains hook:** Formal equality is insufficient where structures require compensation.
**Back:** *"Feminist justice: equal rights, plus support where structures bite."*

### Flashcard PT-4097 — Subaltern justice and Gramsci's hegemony
**Front:** What does subaltern justice demand, and what is Gramsci's role?
**Definition (learn):** Justice requires restoring equitable shares, rights, dignity and protection to structurally subordinated groups, beyond class alone (caste, gender, region, religion, culture). Gramsci's hegemony explains how domination looks like consent (✅ owner basic 20 §6, PDF pp.467–469; ⚠️ extension to caste, owner basic 20 §5).
**Breakdown:**
- Elite theory naturalises rule; hegemony makes unequal arrangements look consensual (✅ owner §7).
- Community is fractured unless subaltern voices organise and speak (✅ owner §8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.467–469 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4119, PT-4098, PT-4105.
**Trap:** Subaltern justice = class only ❌ (owner: extends beyond class).
**Mains hook:** Hegemony explains why the disadvantaged may accept their position.
**Back:** *"Hegemony makes subordination look like consent."*

### Flashcard PT-4098 — Communitarians: MacIntyre, Sandel, Taylor, Walzer
**Front:** What do the communitarians argue against liberal justice?
**Definition (learn):** Communitarians stress virtue, embeddedness, social context and sphere-specific meanings of goods against abstract liberal universalism (✅ owner basic 20 §6, PDF pp.469–473).
**Breakdown:**
- Right over good versus embedded self versus simple equality (✅ owner advanced 20 §3).
- Criticise liberal neutrality and the unencumbered self (✅ owner §8).
- Strong moral diagnosis; weak political mechanism (✅ owner §8, PDF p.473).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.469–473 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4099, PT-4100, PT-4107, PT-4118.
**Trap:** Communitarians reject justice ❌.
**Mains hook:** Communitarian critique attacks the self-model of liberal justice, not justice itself.
**Back:** *"Communitarians: persons are embedded; goods have meanings."*

### Flashcard PT-4099 — Walzer: complex equality
**Front:** What is Walzer's complex equality?
**Definition (learn):** Each social good has its own meaning and should be distributed by criteria suited to its sphere. The aim is to stop one good, especially money, from dominating other spheres (✅ Wikipedia: *Spheres of Justice*, 1983, Basic Books; ✅ owner basic 20 §5, §8).
**Breakdown:**
- Walzer opposes Rawls's single category of primary goods (✅ Wikipedia).
- Money should not buy superiority in all spheres (✅ owner §8).
- Walzer's example: keeping money from corrupting politics, health and education (✅ owner §7, PDF p.473).
- Gauba's criticism: strong moral reasoning, weak political machinery (✅ owner advanced 20 §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1983 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-4098, PT-4085, PT-4118.
**Trap:** Complex equality = unequal shares everywhere ❌ (it is about blocking domination of one sphere by another).
**Mains hook:** Use spheres to argue against money-buys-everything.
**Back:** *"Walzer: each good in its sphere; money stays in its own."*

### Flashcard PT-4100 — Sandel: the unencumbered self
**Front:** What is Sandel's critique of Rawls's self?
**Definition (learn):** Rawls pictures persons as unencumbered, apart from social or communal ties. Sandel argues identity is constitutively formed by communities, traditions and histories, so commitments are part of who we are (✅ Wikipedia: *Liberalism and the Limits of Justice*, Cambridge UP, 1982; revised 1998).
**Breakdown:**
- Targets the original position and veil as misdescribing selfhood (✅ Wikipedia).
- Neutrality about the good life is itself a substantive stance favouring autonomy (✅ Wikipedia).
- Rawls's reply: the veil is a heuristic for principles, not a metaphysical claim (✅ owner basic 20 §14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1982, Cambridge UP (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-4086, PT-4098, PT-4108.
**Trap:** Treating Sandel as rejecting justice ❌.
**Mains hook:** Use Sandel's objection and Rawls's reply to test the veil.
**Back:** *"Sandel: no self stands outside its attachments."*

### Flashcard PT-4101 — Trap: Rawls wants identical outcomes
**Front:** Does Rawls want identical outcomes for everyone?
**Definition (learn):** No. Rawls permits inequality after liberty and fair opportunity, provided it benefits the least advantaged (✅ owner basic 20 §9, PDF p.448).
**Breakdown:**
- Flat equality is not the target (✅ owner advanced 20 §4 "not flat equality").
- Two principles and their order (PT-4087).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.448 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4087, PT-4088.
**Trap:** The trap itself ❌.
**Mains hook:** Show the permitted inequality and its conditions.
**Back:** *"Rawls permits differences that lift the floor."*

### Flashcard PT-4102 — Trap: Nozick is anti-justice
**Front:** Is Nozick hostile to justice?
**Definition (learn):** No. Nozick offers a full theory of justice centred on entitlement, not redistribution (✅ owner basic 20 §9, PDF pp.451–452).
**Breakdown:**
- He denies that welfare-state patterning is justified for just holdings (✅ owner advanced 20 §4).
- He accepts rectification of unjust history (PT-4029).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.451–452 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4090, PT-4029.
**Trap:** The trap itself ❌.
**Mains hook:** Engage the theory, then assess it.
**Back:** *"Nozick builds a theory; he just rejects the pattern."*

### Flashcard PT-4103 — Trap: any voluntary market transfer is just
**Front:** Does a voluntary transfer make a holding just for Nozick?
**Definition (learn):** No. The transfer must rest on just title; acquisition, transfer and rectification form a connected historical test (✅ owner basic 20 §9, PDF p.452).
**Breakdown:**
- Current voluntariness is insufficient if acquisition was unjust (✅ owner advanced 20 §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.452 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4090, PT-4029, PT-4028.
**Trap:** The trap itself ❌.
**Mains hook:** The past matters.
**Back:** *"Voluntary now, but the title must be clean."*

### Flashcard PT-4104 — Trap: Marxism has nothing to do with justice
**Front:** Is Marxism indifferent to justice?
**Definition (learn):** No. Marx condemns exploitation and endorses superior distributive principles in a classless order (✅ owner basic 20 §9, PDF p.453).
**Breakdown:**
- Justice within capitalism is structurally blocked by exploitation (✅ owner §8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.453 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4093, PT-4025.
**Trap:** The trap itself ❌.
**Mains hook:** Marx's justice is structural and class-based.
**Back:** *"Marx condemns exploitation; he does not ignore justice."*

### Flashcard PT-4105 — Trap: democratic socialism is Marxism without elections
**Front:** Is democratic socialism simply Marxism without elections?
**Definition (learn):** No. It insists on freedom of thought, multi-party competition and opposition rights (✅ owner basic 20 §9, PDF pp.460–461).
**Breakdown:**
- Distinct from Marxist revolution and dictatorship (✅ owner advanced 20 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.460–461 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4094, PT-4093.
**Trap:** The trap itself ❌.
**Mains hook:** Use civil liberties as the dividing line.
**Back:** *"Democratic socialism keeps the ballot; Marxism of the Lenin type drops it."*

### Flashcard PT-4106 — Trap: feminism is identical treatment
**Front:** Does feminism demand that women be treated exactly like men in every situation?
**Definition (learn):** No. Gauba also notes claims for maternity benefits, exemptions from certain hazardous work and reservations to correct underrepresentation (✅ owner basic 20 §5, §9, PDF pp.466–468).
**Breakdown:**
- Corrective support, not only formal sameness.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.466–468 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4096, PT-4013.
**Trap:** The trap itself ❌.
**Mains hook:** Differential protection can serve equality.
**Back:** *"Feminism: same rights, and support where disadvantage bites."*

### Flashcard PT-4107 — Trap: communitarian critique means rejecting rights
**Front:** Does communitarian criticism reject rights or justice?
**Definition (learn):** No. The criticism targets the abstract self and ethical neutrality, not the very idea of justice (✅ owner basic 20 §9, advanced 20 §12, PDF pp.469–473).
**Breakdown:**
- Communitarians want shared-good justice, not authoritarian absolutism (✅ owner advanced 20 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.469–473 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4098, PT-4100.
**Trap:** The trap itself ❌.
**Mains hook:** Separate the critique of liberal self-models from any attack on rights.
**Back:** *"Communitarians question the self, not the rights."*

### Flashcard PT-4108 — Six hidden questions in every justice theory
**Front:** What are the six questions every justice theory answers, and how do you compare theories?
**Definition (learn):** Who gets what, by what rule, through which state, with what property regime, in what community, and against which injustice (⚠️ owner basic 20 §1 UPSC key).
**Breakdown:**
- Compare on distribution rule, role of state, property, equality, community and critique (✅ owner basic 20 §8 comparison table).
- Rawls vs Nozick: distributive object, method, liberty, property, state, inequality treatment (⚠️ owner advanced 20 §10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Synthesis (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4087, PT-4090, PT-4098, PT-4121.
**Trap:** Comparing theories on one axis only ❌.
**Mains hook:** A comparison grid makes a rival-theory answer disciplined.
**Back:** *"Who, by what rule, through which state, owning what, in which community, against which wrong?"*

### Flashcard PT-4109 — PYQ route: justice theories
**Front:** Which verified PYQs anchor the diverse-perspectives chapter?
**Definition (learn):** Verified cross-applied Philosophy Optional PYQs: 2019 (10 marks, Rawls and Plato); 2021 (10 marks, Nozick's distributive theory); 2021 (20 marks, Sen vs Rawls); 2022 (15 marks, equity and equality in Marx); 2023 (10 marks, justice as fairness); 2025 (20 marks, liberty and equality without justice) (✅ owner basic 20 §10).
**Breakdown:**
- Nozick answer: state the theory, run an objection-reply, then judge (✅ owner advanced 20 §12).
- Sen vs Rawls answer: compare capability with primary goods (⚠️ owner route).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PYQ years as listed (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4089, PT-4090, PT-4071, PT-4076.
**Trap:** Answering the Sen–Rawls PYQ without naming both theories ❌.
**Mains hook:** Map each PYQ onto a named theory and a qualified verdict.
**Back:** *"Map each PYQ to a theorist, then to a verdict."*

### GROUP — Diverse Perspectives on Justice (advanced/20)

### Flashcard PT-4110 — Criticisms of Rawls
**Front:** Which criticisms of Rawls does Gauba record?
**Definition (learn):** Collectivist: Rawls may legitimate capitalism. Marxist: the veil detaches justice from class and property. Libertarian: liberty and incentives are sacrificed. Communitarian: neutrality evades the common good (✅ owner advanced 20 §7, PDF pp.448–449).
**Breakdown:**
- Gauba partly answers these by reading Rawls as converging libertarian, egalitarian and communitarian elements (✅ owner, PDF pp.449–450).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.448–450 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4087, PT-4111, PT-4098.
**Trap:** Treating these criticisms as decisive ❌.
**Mains hook:** Four critics, four different objections; reply through the veil's purpose.
**Back:** *"Four critics: capitalism, class, liberty, community."*

### Flashcard PT-4111 — Rawls as convergence of traditions
**Front:** How does Gauba partly reconcile Rawls with his critics?
**Definition (learn):** He reads Rawls as a convergence of libertarian, egalitarian and communitarian elements (✅ owner advanced 20 §7, PDF pp.449–450).
**Breakdown:**
- Liberty first, as libertarians want.
- Opportunity and difference, as egalitarians want.
- Social interdependence, as communitarians want (⚠️ owner interpretive gloss).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.449–450 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4110, PT-4087.
**Trap:** Assuming Rawls is a pure egalitarian ❌.
**Mains hook:** Use the convergence reading to answer the "is Rawls a socialist?" question.
**Back:** *"Rawls as a convergence of three traditions."*

### Flashcard PT-4112 — Rawls: benefit to the least advantaged is not sufficient
**Front:** Can an inequality be justified merely because it helps the worst-off?
**Definition (learn):** No. It must also respect equal basic liberties and fair equality of opportunity (⚠️ owner advanced 20 §7).
**Breakdown:**
- Necessary-but-not-sufficient correction (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Synthesis (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-4088, PT-4087.
**Trap:** Difference principle as sole test ❌.
**Mains hook:** Lexical priority blocks trade-offs.
**Back:** *"Helping the bottom is necessary, not enough."*

### Flashcard PT-4113 — Nozick: welfare taxation suspect, market injustice untouched
**Front:** What is the internal criticism of Nozick's treatment of taxation?
**Definition (learn):** Nozick treats welfare taxation as morally suspect while leaving structurally generated market injustice untouched (✅ owner advanced 20 §7, PDF p.452).
**Breakdown:**
- Libertarian historical justice can be inattentive to present structural power.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.452 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4090, PT-4029.
**Trap:** Assuming Nozick is blind to every market injustice ❌ (rectification applies).
**Mains hook:** Use the rectification principle and the structural critique together.
**Back:** *"Nozick taxes the redistributor, not the structure."*

### Flashcard PT-4114 — Lenin and Mao: the transitional socialist state
**Front:** Why does Gauba mention Lenin and Mao?
**Definition (learn):** To explain book-period socialist-state functions: socialisation, planning and ideological-cultural transformation during transition (✅ owner advanced 20 §5, PDF pp.456–458).
**Breakdown:**
- Book-period illustrations, not current evidence (✅ owner advanced 20 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.456–458 (✅ owner).
**Links:** PT-4093, PT-4115.
