**Trap:** Do not cite the concurrent majority as neutral; its origin is sectional defence (✅ owner 22 §14 and §14 chain 3).
**Mains hook:** It is an early conceptual precursor to mutual veto devices, but must be flagged as not value-free (⚠️ owner 22 §14).
**Back:** *"Every major section holds a veto; born to defend slavery."*

### Flashcard PT-5038 — Consociational democracy (Lijphart, 1977)
**Front:** Define consociational democracy and list its four features.
**Definition (learn):** Stable democracy in deeply divided societies through power-sharing (⚠️ owner 22 §14; ✅ owner 22 adv §4, PDF pp.508–509).
**Breakdown:**
- Grand coalition of segment leaders (✅ Wikipedia: Consociational democracy).
- Mutual veto for vital minority interests (✅ Wikipedia: Consociational democracy).
- Proportionality in representation and public appointments (✅ Wikipedia: Consociational democracy).
- Segmental autonomy over internal affairs (✅ Wikipedia: Consociational democracy).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Arend Lijphart, *Democracy in Plural Societies: A Comparative Exploration* (1977, Yale) (✅ Wikipedia: Consociational democracy). Owner cites Netherlands and Belgium; Wikipedia adds Cyprus, Lebanon, Switzerland, Northern Ireland, others.
**Links:** PT-5037, PT-5035, PT-5040, PT-5031.
**Trap:** Owner says Lebanon and Northern Ireland are "more contestable" comparators (⚠️ owner 22 §14); Wikipedia lists many more cases. Do not present historical cases as current (✅ owner 22 adv §12).
**Mains hook:** Consociation makes divided societies governable without forced assimilation, but may freeze segmental identities (⚠️ owner 22 adv §8B).
**Back:** *"Share the cabinet, share the veto, split the autonomy."*

### Flashcard PT-5039 — Objection-reply chain: FPTP vs List PR
**Front:** Give the objection and reply on FPTP and on List PR.
**Definition (learn):** FPTP objection: minority of votes becomes majority of seats; wasted votes. Reply: clear accountability and decisive government (⚠️ owner 22 §14). List PR objection: fragmentation and weak constituency link. Reply: faithful translation of preferences; minority voice (⚠️ owner 22 §14).
**Breakdown:**
- Chain 1: FPTP harms smaller parties and minority interests → accountability and decisiveness defence (⚠️).
- Chain 2: PR fragments legislatures → proportionality and inclusion defence (⚠️).
- Use both to show the trade-off, not a one-sided preference (⚠️ owner 22 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 22 §14 chains 1–2.
**Links:** PT-5033, PT-5035, PT-5034.
**Trap:** Directive "Discuss merits and demerits of FPTP and PR" requires both sides (⚠️ owner 22 §16).
**Mains hook:** Choose the system by the criterion you prioritise: decisiveness, inclusion or accountability (⚠️).
**Back:** *"FPTP: clear winner. PR: faithful voice. Both lose something."*

### Flashcard PT-5040 — Objection-reply chain: concurrent majority and consociation
**Front:** Give the objections and replies for Calhoun's concurrent majority and Lijphart's consociationalism.
**Definition (learn):** Calhoun: a universal veto risks deadlock and entrenched elite interests; reply: an early precursor to defensible mutual-veto devices, if not treated as value-free (⚠️ owner 22 §14 chain 3). Lijphart: elite grand coalitions reduce accountability and entrench identities; reply: consociation is preferable where a segment would otherwise be permanently excluded (⚠️ owner 22 §14 chain 4).
**Breakdown:**
- Deadlock vs inclusion (⚠️).
- Elite accountability vs stability (⚠️).
- Both replies rely on the deep-division premise (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Calhoun 1851; Lijphart 1977.
**Links:** PT-5037, PT-5038, PT-5018.
**Trap:** Do not present consociation as a universal default; it fits deeply divided societies (✅ owner 22 adv §8B).
**Mains hook:** Use these chains for a 15- or 20-mark answer on minority protection (⚠️ owner 22 §16).
**Back:** *"Veto can freeze; sharing can entrench; choose by the division."*

### Flashcard PT-5041 — Liberalism–democracy fusion and the tyranny-of-majority tension (Sartori)
**Front:** How does Sartori frame liberal democracy, and what tension does it name?
**Definition (learn):** Liberal democracy is historically fused but analytically separable: liberalism supplies limits, rights and constitutional restraint; democracy supplies popular rule and political equality (⚠️ owner 22 §15, Giovanni Sartori, *The Theory of Democracy Revisited* (1987)).
**Breakdown:**
- Liberalism: limits on power, individual rights (⚠️ owner 22 §15).
- Democracy: popular rule, political equality (⚠️ owner 22 §15).
- Tension: unconstrained majority vs excessive insulation (⚠️ owner 22 §15).
- Standard response: independent judiciary, entrenched rights, federalism, checks and balances (⚠️ owner 22 §15).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sartori 1987 (⚠️ owner 22; Wikipedia page not verified — 404 on fetch).
**Links:** PT-5004, PT-5018, PT-5020, PT-5022.
**Trap:** Sartori is a fusion theorist, not a simple majoritarian; the owner's presentation of the 1987 book is unverified (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Name the tension explicitly in a 20-mark answer; liberal constitutionalism holds both commitments (⚠️ owner 22 §15).
**Back:** *"Liberty limits, democracy empowers; keep both in tension."*

### Flashcard PT-5042 — Radical/direct vs reactionary vs conservative readings of representation
**Front:** How does the owner classify theories of representation on the radical–reactionary axis?
**Definition (learn):** Gauba surveys reactionary, conservative, liberal and radical theories, from elite discretion to direct popular control (✅ owner 22 adv §3, PDF pp.502–503). Radical/direct: representation dilutes sovereignty (✅ owner 22 adv §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Reactionary: order entrusted to rulers; narrow signalling by representatives (✅ owner 22 adv §8A).
- Conservative/trustee: judgment, not instruction (✅ owner 22 adv §6).
- Liberal/delegate: agent of equal citizens (✅ owner 22 adv §6).
- Radical/direct: direct democracy as truest form (✅ owner 22 adv §6).
- Hobbes and Hamilton fit reactionary; Burke and Madison fit conservative (✅ owner 22 adv §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.502–503.
**Links:** PT-5028, PT-5029, PT-5002, PT-5015.
**Trap:** The radical–conservative labelling of delegate and trustee is a secondary analytical classification, not Gauba's claim (⚠️ owner 22 §12).
**Mains hook:** Map the spectrum before assessing which theory best serves minority and popular control (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Reactionary rules, conservative judges, liberal agents, radical voices."*

### Flashcard PT-5043 — Indian liberal-democratic capsule (conceptual, not Polity detail)
**Front:** Summarise the Indian liberal-democratic capsule in the owner file.
**Definition (learn):** Universal adult franchise gives equal standing; competitive elections create authorisation; civil liberties enable opinion and opposition; judicial review restrains unconstitutional power; minority-rights protections limit majoritarian domination; local self-government widens participation (⚠️ owner 22 §17).
**Breakdown:**
- Franchise → equal standing (⚠️ owner 22 §17).
- Elections → authorisation (⚠️).
- Liberties and review → restraint (⚠️).
- Local government → participation below national level (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Keep article numbers and cases in Polity owners (⚠️ owner 22 §17, `Polity/advanced/27_Election-Commission.md`).
**Links:** PT-5004, PT-5018, PT-5022, PT-5041.
**Trap:** Do not claim India is constitutionally consociational (⚠️ owner 22 §17).
**Mains hook:** Use the capsule as a conceptual lens; cite Articles only from Polity owners (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Franchise, elections, liberties, review, minority guard, local voice."*

### Flashcard PT-5044 — Answer architecture for representation and liberal democracy
**Front:** How should a 10-, 15- or 20-mark answer on representation or liberal democracy be built?
**Definition (learn):** 10 marks (~150 words): thesis → named evidence → one trade-off → verdict. 15 marks (~200–250 words): thesis → two-model comparison → two evidence units → one objection-reply chain → Indian illustration → verdict. 20 marks (~250–300 words): tension upfront → multi-dimensional comparison → three evidence units → two chains → Indian/comparative application → graded verdict (⚠️ owner 22 §16).
**Breakdown:**
- Directive decoding: "distinguish delegate and trustee" is definitional and comparative (⚠️ owner 22 §16).
- "Can majority rule be reconciled with minority rights" is tension-testing (⚠️ owner 22 §16).
- Verdict must name the residual trade-off between stability, proportionality and minority protection (⚠️ owner 22 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 22 §16.
**Links:** PT-5028, PT-5033, PT-5039, PT-5040, PT-5018.
**Trap:** A one-sided preference for FPTP or PR without trade-off analysis (⚠️ owner 22 §16).
**Mains hook:** Use the directive words to choose the architecture; keep each evidence unit provenance-tagged (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Thesis, evidence, trade-off, verdict — scale to the marks."*

### GROUP — Contemporary Democracy, Social Change and Development (owner 23)

### Flashcard PT-5045 — Concept: models of contemporary democracy and the change–development map
**Front:** What are the five contemporary democracy models and the four social-change concepts in owner 23?
**Definition (learn):** Democracy models: elitist, pluralist, participatory, Marxist, deliberative (✅ owner 23 §1, PDF pp.513–539). Change concepts: revolution, evolution, development, progress (✅ owner 23 §1, PDF pp.540–541).
**Breakdown:**
- Elitist: leaders compete for votes (✅ owner 23 §1).
- Pluralist: groups bargain (✅ owner 23 §1).
- Participatory: citizens act beyond voting (✅ owner 23 §1).
- Marxist: formal democracy hides class power (✅ owner 23 §1).
- Deliberative: public reasoning shapes decisions (✅ owner 23 §1).
- Development paths: market, welfare, socialist, Gandhian, sustainable (✅ owner 23 §1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Gauba 5th ed. (2009), Ch.23–25, PDF pp.513–576 (✅ owner 23 header).
**Links:** PT-5046, PT-5057, PT-5060, PT-5065, PT-5068, PT-5074.
**Trap:** Mixing the democracy-model axis with the change-concept axis (⚠️ owner 23 §11).
**Mains hook:** Define the democracy model first, then distinguish change concepts before comparing thinkers (⚠️ owner 23 §11).
**Back:** *"Five models of democracy, four words for change, five paths of development."*

### Flashcard PT-5046 — Elitist democracy: Schumpeter (1942)
**Front:** What is Schumpeter's definition of democracy and where is it published?
**Definition (learn):** Democracy is an institutional arrangement in which leaders gain power through competitive struggle for votes; citizens choose among competing elites (✅ owner 23 §6, PDF p.515).
**Breakdown:**
- Method view: election of representatives in competitive elections (✅ Wikipedia: Joseph Schumpeter).
- Work: *Capitalism, Socialism and Democracy*, 1942; most popular of his works (✅ Wikipedia: Joseph Schumpeter).
- Owner: procedural redefinition, not rejection of democracy (✅ owner 23 adv §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1942 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5047, PT-5048, PT-5054 (Dahl), PT-5063 (Macpherson's objection), PT-5030.
**Trap:** Do not caricature Schumpeter as anti-democratic (✅ owner 23 adv §12).
**Mains hook:** Schumpeter's minimal democracy explains elite competition but is criticised for low participation; Macpherson's objection is the test (⚠️ owner 23 §10 prompt 1).
**Back:** *"Schumpeter: democracy is the vote-market for leaders."*

### Flashcard PT-5047 — Mannheim: elite rule reconciled with democracy
**Front:** How does Mannheim reconcile elite rule with democracy?
**Definition (learn):** Citizens can periodically remove leaders, which reduces the distance between elites and masses (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF p.514).
**Breakdown:**
- Periodic removal as democratic check (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
- Adapts elite analysis rather than rejecting democracy (✅ owner 23 adv §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.514.
**Links:** PT-5046, PT-5048, PT-5045.
**Trap:** Mannheim's reconciliation is a limited democracy; it does not require mass direct rule (⚠️ synthesis).
**Mains hook:** The removability test separates democratic elites from non-competitive ones (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Mannheim: elites may rule if the people can remove them."*

### Flashcard PT-5048 — Sartori: leadership as democracy's operational feature
**Front:** Why does Sartori argue that leadership is not a democratic defect?
**Definition (learn):** Leadership is the core operational feature of democracy, provided leaders compete electorally and remain accountable (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF pp.515–516; ⚠️ owner 23 §12 cites Sartori 1987).
**Breakdown:**
- Leadership is unavoidable in mass politics (⚠️ owner 23 §12).
- Competitive, accountable selection distinguishes democratic from non-competitive elite rule (⚠️ owner 23 §12).
- Elections, party competition and free press are the constraints (⚠️ owner 23 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** *The Theory of Democracy Revisited* (1987), owner-cited; Wikipedia fetch returned 404 (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-5041, PT-5046, PT-5052, PT-5053.
**Trap:** Sartori's democratic elitism is not the same as Mosca's non-competitive elitism (⚠️ owner 23 §12).
**Mains hook:** Use Sartori to answer Michels: competitive selection constrains elites without abolishing leadership (⚠️ owner 23 §17 chain 1).
**Back:** *"Leaders are democracy's engine if they can be fired."*

### Flashcard PT-5049 — Aron: plural elites in industrial society
**Front:** How does Aron describe the elite structure of liberal democracy?
**Definition (learn):** Liberal democracy is distinguished by a plurality of elites, checks and balances, and compromise rather than a unified party elite (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF p.515; ⚠️ owner 23 §12 cites *Democracy and Totalitarianism* (1965)).
**Breakdown:**
- Political, economic/managerial, military, intellectual/technocratic elites (⚠️ owner 23 §12).
- Interaction among elites shapes outcomes, not one monolithic class (⚠️ owner 23 §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1965 (⚠️ owner 23 §12; not verified).
**Links:** PT-5046, PT-5047, PT-5050.
**Trap:** Aron's plural elites are not a claim that power is evenly spread (⚠️ synthesis).
**Mains hook:** Plural elites plus checks answer the monolithic-elite worry (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Aron: many elites, checked by each other."*

### Flashcard PT-5050 — Pareto: circulation of elites
**Front:** What is Pareto's "circulation of elites" and his "lions and foxes"?
**Definition (learn):** Society is always ruled by an elite; governing elites decline and are replaced by counter-elites rising from below; history is "a graveyard of aristocracies" (✅ owner 23 §12; ⚠️ owner 23 adv §5, PDF pp.514, 532).
**Breakdown:**
- Lions: force; foxes: cunning and manufactured consent (⚠️ owner 23 §12).
- Work: *The Mind and Society* (Italian *Trattato di Sociologia Generale*, 1916) (⚠️ owner 23 §12; not verified by fetch).
- Circulation replaces mass self-rule with elite replacement (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1916 (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-5051, PT-5052, PT-5046.
**Trap:** Pareto is not a theorist of mass rule; his elites are superior minorities (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Circulation shows elites can be replaced, but not that the people rule (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Pareto: lions use force, foxes use consent; elites circulate."*

### Flashcard PT-5051 — Mosca: political class and political formula (1896)
**Front:** What are Mosca's "political class" and "political formula"?
**Definition (learn):** In every society an organised minority rules an unorganised majority; rule rests on superior organisation and is justified by a political formula (✅ owner 23 §12; ✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF p.513).
**Breakdown:**
- Ruling class monopolises wealth, power and prestige (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
- Political formula: justifying myth such as divine right or popular sovereignty (✅ owner 23 §12).
- Work: *Elementi di Scienza Politica* (*The Ruling Class*), 1896 (✅ Wikipedia: Gaetano Mosca).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1896 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5050, PT-5052, PT-5062 (Gramsci hegemony).
**Trap:** The formula is justificatory, not necessarily true; popular sovereignty is also a formula (✅ owner 23 §12).
**Mains hook:** Democracy's legitimating myth can be tested by whether competition is real (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Mosca: the few organise, the myth legitimises."*

### Flashcard PT-5052 — Michels: iron law of oligarchy (1911)
**Front:** State Michels' iron law of oligarchy and its causes.
**Definition (learn):** Any large organisation, even democratic, drifts toward oligarchic leadership (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF p.514; ✅ Wikipedia: Iron law of oligarchy).
**Breakdown:**
- Technical necessity and division of labour (✅ Wikipedia: Iron law of oligarchy).
- Leaders' superior control of information and organisation (✅ owner 23 §12).
- Psychological: masses feel need to be led; leaders seek power (✅ Wikipedia: Iron law of oligarchy).
- Work: *Political Parties* (1911); English 1915 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1911; "Who says organization, says oligarchy" (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5053, PT-5048, PT-5051, PT-5063 (Macpherson participation).
**Trap:** Michels describes drift, not inevitable total domination of every organisation; the owner's "inevitable" wording should be flagged (⚠️ owner 23 §12 "inevitably").
**Mains hook:** Michels is the strongest objection to participatory democracy; the reply is competitive selection plus multiplied participatory sites (⚠️ owner 23 §17).
**Back:** *"Organisation is oligarchy: who says organisation, says few leaders."*

### Flashcard PT-5053 — Objection-reply: elite theory vs participatory and competitive replies
**Front:** Give the objection and the two replies on elite theory.
**Definition (learn):** Objection: Michels's iron law makes mass participation illusory (✅ owner 23 §17 chain 1). Reply 1: Sartori—competitive, accountable selection constrains elites (⚠️ owner 23 §17). Reply 2: Macpherson—multiplied participatory sites counter oligarchic drift (⚠️ owner 23 §17).
**Breakdown:**
- Objection: organisational necessity concentrates power (✅ owner 23 §17).
- Reply: competition constrains; participation multiplies sites (⚠️).
- Residual: elites cannot be abolished outright (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 §17.
**Links:** PT-5052, PT-5048, PT-5063, PT-5057.
**Trap:** Do not treat the reply as eliminating oligarchy; it only limits it (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Use this chain for a 15-mark critical examination of elite democracy (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
**Back:** *"Michels says yes to oligarchy; reply: contest it, multiply voices."*

### Flashcard PT-5054 — Dahl: polyarchy and pluralism (1971)
**Front:** What is Dahl's polyarchy and what does it describe?
**Definition (learn):** Practical democracy resembles polyarchy: policy shaped by group competition rather than undivided popular rule or a single elite (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF pp.516–518).
**Breakdown:**
- Work: *Polyarchy: Participation and Opposition*, 1971 (✅ Wikipedia: Polyarchy).
- Eight conditions (1956 Preface) and criteria: elected officials control policy; frequent free elections; universal adult vote; right to run; freedom of expression; alternative information; associational autonomy (✅ Wikipedia: Polyarchy).
- Owner: pluralist equilibrium (✅ owner 23 §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1971; Dahl 1989 criteria list (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5055, PT-5056, PT-5046, PT-5063.
**Trap:** Dahl's polyarchy is a degree-of-democracy measure, not a claim that all adults govern (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Dahl's criteria give a checklist for testing whether a regime is polyarchal (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Polyarchy: many contenders, open contest, real opposition."*

### Flashcard PT-5055 — Pluralist democracy: group bargaining
**Front:** What does pluralist democracy claim about policy-making?
**Definition (learn):** Policy emerges from bargaining among relatively autonomous groups rather than from a single majority or a single elite (✅ owner 23 §3, PDF pp.516–517).
**Breakdown:**
- Groups: unions, farmers, traders, women's associations (⚠️ owner 23 §7).
- Dispersed influence highlighted (✅ owner 23 §6).
- Application: transport policy negotiated among unions, commuters, environmentalists and industry (⚠️ owner 23 adv §9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.516–517.
**Links:** PT-5054, PT-5056, PT-5057.
**Trap:** Pluralism is not equal influence; organised groups prevail (✅ owner 23 §6).
**Mains hook:** Pluralism captures policy complexity but may privilege organised groups (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Pluralism: groups bargain, not the masses."*

### Flashcard PT-5056 — Trap: pluralism privileges the organised
**Front:** Why is pluralism criticised for unequal voice?
**Definition (learn):** Pluralism may privilege organised, vocal and resourceful groups over weaker citizens (✅ owner 23 adv §7, PDF pp.516–518).
**Breakdown:**
- Unequal organisation → unequal voice (✅ owner 23 adv §6).
- Improves on elitism by replacing one minority with many (✅ owner 23 adv §7).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.516–518.
**Links:** PT-5055, PT-5054, PT-5057.
**Trap:** Do not claim pluralism means every citizen is represented (✅ owner 23 adv §12).
**Mains hook:** Use participatory correction to show inclusion of weaker citizens (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Many groups, but not many equal voices."*

### Flashcard PT-5057 — Participatory democracy: Rousseau's inheritance
**Front:** What is participatory democracy and who is its precursor?
**Definition (learn):** Democracy requires active citizen involvement in policy and public life, including voting, protest, local action, initiative, recall and public hearing (✅ owner 23 §3, adv §3, PDF pp.518–522).
**Breakdown:**
- Precursor: Rousseau, sovereignty not alienable (✅ owner 23 §6, PDF pp.518–519).
- Forms: local action, initiative, recall, hearings (✅ owner 23 adv §3).
- Gain: democratic education and accountability (✅ owner 23 adv §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.518–522.
**Links:** PT-5015, PT-5058, PT-5059, PT-5065.
**Trap:** Participation is not deliberation (✅ owner 23 adv §12; see PT-5066).
**Mains hook:** Participation builds accountability but needs channels and discipline (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Participation: citizens act, not just vote."*

### Flashcard PT-5058 — Pateman: Participation and Democratic Theory (1970)
**Front:** What is Pateman's book and why is it cited in the participatory tradition?
**Definition (learn):** Carole Pateman, *Participation and Democratic Theory*, 1970, Cambridge University Press (✅ Wikipedia: Carole Pateman).
**Breakdown:**
- Year and publisher verified (✅ Wikipedia: Carole Pateman).
- Core argument (educative effect, participatory society) not verified; not in owner 23 (⚠️).
- Later: "Participatory Democracy Revisited" (2012) (✅ Wikipedia: Carole Pateman).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1970 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5057, PT-5053, PT-5059.
**Trap:** Do not attribute specific Pateman claims beyond the fetched facts; the owner does not name her (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Use Pateman as a named participatory source only with the caution that claims are from memory (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Pateman 1970: participation as democratic theory."*

### Flashcard PT-5059 — Trap: participation can become disorderly mobilisation
**Front:** What warning does Gauba attach to participatory mobilisation?
**Definition (learn):** Excessive street mobilisation without discipline may lead to crowd pressure, disruption and unreasonable demands (✅ owner 23 adv §7, PDF pp.521–522).
**Breakdown:**
- Over-mobilisation risk (✅ owner 23 §8A).
- Requires disciplined channels (✅ owner 23 adv §6).
- Relevant to protest and movement questions (⚠️ owner 23 §24.3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.521–522.
**Links:** PT-5057, PT-5066, PT-5100.
**Trap:** Participation alone is not legitimacy; disorder can damage democracy (✅ owner 23 adv §7).
**Mains hook:** Qualify participatory claims with channels and reasoned contestation (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Mobilised is not the same as legitimate."*

### Flashcard PT-5060 — Marxist democracy: bourgeois democracy and class rule
**Front:** How do Marx and Engels criticise liberal democracy?
**Definition (learn):** Liberal democracy is bourgeois democracy: the political form of capitalist class domination; formal democracy masks exploitation (✅ owner 23 adv §4–5, PDF pp.523–525, 550).
**Breakdown:**
- Bourgeois democracy: political form of class rule (✅ owner 23 adv §4).
- Substantive democracy: judged by social interests and material equality (✅ owner 23 adv §4, PDF p.528).
- Socialist transition needed for substantive democracy (✅ owner 23 §3, PDF pp.523–535).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.523–535.
**Links:** PT-5061, PT-5062, PT-5071, PT-5066.
**Trap:** Marxist democracy is not a synonym for one-man dictatorship; it is a class theory of substantive democracy (✅ owner 23 §12, PDF pp.525–535).
**Mains hook:** The Marxist critique exposes class power; its practical cost is curtailed opposition (✅ owner 23 adv §7).
**Back:** *"Marx: liberal votes, bourgeois rule."*

### Flashcard PT-5061 — Lenin: dictatorship of the proletariat and democratic centralism
**Front:** What do Lenin's dictatorship-of-the-proletariat and democratic centralism mean in owner 23?
**Definition (learn):** Lenin sharpens the dictatorship-of-the-proletariat thesis and links democratic centralism to socialist organisation (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF pp.525–527, 532).
**Breakdown:**
- Dictatorship of the proletariat as transition (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
- Democratic centralism as party organisation (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
- Practical restriction of opposition and liberties in socialist practice (✅ owner 23 adv §7, PDF pp.533–535).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.525–535.
**Links:** PT-5060, PT-5062, PT-5052 (Michels's oligarchy).
**Trap:** Do not treat dictatorship of proletariat as liberal dictatorship; owner stresses class theory (✅ owner 23 §12).
**Mains hook:** Socialist democracy's promise faces the liberty test (⚠️ owner 23 adv §7).
**Back:** *"Lenin: class rule through the party, in theory for the many."*

### Flashcard PT-5062 — Gramsci: hegemony and consent
**Front:** What is Gramsci's hegemony and how does it revise revolutionary expectation?
**Definition (learn):** Ruling-class dominance is secured by cultural and ideological leadership exercised through civil-society institutions, so subordinate groups accept ruling interests as common sense (✅ owner 23 §13; ✅ Wikipedia: Antonio Gramsci).
**Breakdown:**
- Hegemony: consent, not force alone (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF pp.534–535).
- War of position: build counter-hegemony in civil society (⚠️ owner 23 §13).
- Political society: direct coercion; civil society: consent (✅ Wikipedia: Antonio Gramsci).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** *Prison Notebooks*: owner says written 1929–1935 (✅ owner 23 §13). Wikipedia says imprisonment 1926–1937; notebook writing dates not separately stated (⚠️ date conflict flagged; owner-vs-source).
**Links:** PT-5060, PT-5051, PT-5067, PT-5083 (dependency), PT-5100.
**Trap:** Hegemony is not only coercion; do not reduce it to force (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Gramsci exposes manufactured consent, but the war-of-position answer presupposes contestable civil society (⚠️ owner 23 §17 chain 2).
**Back:** *"Gramsci: rule by consent, won in civil society."*

### Flashcard PT-5063 — Macpherson: possessive individualism and developmental democracy
**Front:** What does Macpherson attack and what democracy does he seek?
**Definition (learn):** Macpherson attacks possessive-individualist and market-equilibrium democracy, distinguishes developmental from extractive power, and seeks a humane synthesis of liberty and equality (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF pp.535–537).
**Breakdown:**
- Possessive individualism: individuals as owners of their abilities, owing nothing to society (✅ Wikipedia: Possessive individualism).
- Work: *The Political Theory of Possessive Individualism*, 1962 (✅ Wikipedia: Possessive individualism).
- Work: *The Life and Times of Liberal Democracy* (1977) cited in owner and not verified (⚠️; Wikipedia 404).
- Objection to Schumpeter-Dahl equilibrium: democracy as human development, not market registration of wants (✅ owner 23 adv §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1962 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5046, PT-5054, PT-5057, PT-5053.
**Trap:** Macpherson's democracy is not anti-liberal; he seeks liberty plus equality (✅ owner 23 adv §5, §7).
**Mains hook:** Use Macpherson to attack low-participation equilibrium models (⚠️ owner 23 §10 prompt 1).
**Back:** *"Macpherson: democracy must develop people, not just sell votes."*

### Flashcard PT-5064 — Habermas: public sphere (1962/1989)
**Front:** What is the public sphere and how does Habermas define the bourgeois version?
**Definition (learn):** A space between private persons and state authority where citizens debate matters of common concern and check political power (✅ Wikipedia: The Structural Transformation of the Public Sphere; ⚠️ owner 23 adv §23.3).
**Breakdown:**
- Work: *The Structural Transformation of the Public Sphere*, German 1962, English 1989 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Places: coffee houses, salons, letters, press (✅ Wikipedia).
- Exclusion: property and education gates; the bourgeois sphere was not universal (✅ Wikipedia; ✅ owner 23 adv §23.3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1962 German; 1989 English (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5065, PT-5066, PT-5098, PT-5016.
**Trap:** A nostalgic golden-age contrast is not available; the historical sphere was exclusive (✅ owner 23 adv §23.3).
**Mains hook:** Ask which arrangement is more inclusive and more deliberative; they do not necessarily move together (⚠️ owner 23 adv §23.3).
**Back:** *"Habermas: citizens reason together between state and market."*

### Flashcard PT-5065 — Deliberative democracy
**Front:** Define deliberative democracy and its legitimacy claim.
**Definition (learn):** Legitimacy grows when citizens and representatives justify public decisions through reasoned discussion while respecting freedom (✅ owner 23 §3, PDF pp.537–539).
**Breakdown:**
- Requires reason-giving and mutual persuasion (✅ owner 23 §5).
- Corrects vote aggregation and unreasoned mobilisation (✅ owner 23 adv §7).
- Presumes argument culture, information and listening (✅ owner 23 adv §7).
- Habermasian bridge is a comparator, not Gauba's named claim (⚠️ owner 23 §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.537–539.
**Links:** PT-5066, PT-5064, PT-5067, PT-5099.
**Trap:** Deliberation is not mere participation (✅ owner 23 §9).
**Mains hook:** Deliberation suits ecological and distant-harm questions better than votes (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.7 reply 3, PT-5092).
**Back:** *"Deliberation: justify, do not just aggregate."*

### Flashcard PT-5066 — Participation vs deliberation (comparison pair)
**Front:** Why is participation alone insufficient for legitimacy?
**Definition (learn):** Participation means taking part in politics; deliberation adds reason-giving, mutual persuasion and justified decision (✅ owner 23 §5, PDF pp.518–522, 537–539).
**Breakdown:**
- Participation: mobilise, protest, vote (⚠️ owner 23 adv §4).
- Deliberation: reasoned justification (✅ owner 23 §5).
- Legitimacy: needs both plus accountability (⚠️ owner 23 adv §12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.518–539.
**Links:** PT-5057, PT-5065, PT-5059.
**Trap:** Participation automatically yielding deliberation is wrong (✅ owner 23 adv §12).
**Mains hook:** The 15-mark prompt "participatory vs deliberative" requires this distinction (⚠️ owner 23 adv §10 prompt 2).
**Back:** *"Participation acts; deliberation justifies the act."*

### Flashcard PT-5067 — Objection-reply: Gramsci hegemony vs formal democracy
**Front:** Give the Gramscian objection to formal democracy and the pluralist/deliberative reply.
**Definition (learn):** Objection: formal procedures can mask class domination if civil society is controlled, so consent is manufactured (✅ owner 23 §17 chain 2). Reply: the war of position itself presupposes contestable civil society; counter-hegemonic mobilisation shows formal space is not decorative (⚠️ owner 23 §17).
**Breakdown:**
- Objection: manufactured consent (✅ owner 23 §17).
- Reply: pluralist/deliberative evidence of contestability (⚠️).
- Residual: historical examples must be cited carefully (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 §17.
**Links:** PT-5062, PT-5065, PT-5098.
**Trap:** Do not conclude that formal democracy suffices; the reply is conditional (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Use with Gramsci for a 20-mark answer on consent and hegemony (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
**Back:** *"Manufactured consent? Only if civil society is closed."*

### Flashcard PT-5068 — Revolution vs evolution
**Front:** Distinguish revolution from evolution.
**Definition (learn):** Revolution is rapid, abrupt transformation of the social system; evolution is cumulative, gradual change from simple to more complex forms (✅ owner 23 §3, PDF p.540).
**Breakdown:**
- Pace: sudden vs gradual (✅ owner 23 adv §8B).
- Scope: revolution can change multiple dimensions (✅ owner 23 adv §4).
- Normative load: revolution not automatically superior; evolution largely descriptive (⚠️ owner 23 adv §8B).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.540.
**Links:** PT-5069, PT-5070, PT-5071, PT-5072.
**Trap:** "Revolution" is not a synonym for violence in owner definition; it is defined by abruptness (✅ owner 23 §3).
**Mains hook:** Separate pace from morality before comparing Marx and Popper (⚠️ owner 23 §11).
**Back:** *"Revolution breaks; evolution accumulates."*

### Flashcard PT-5069 — Trap: development is not growth
**Front:** Why is development not identical to GDP or growth?
**Definition (learn):** Development is movement toward stronger, more organised, efficient and satisfying institutions; Gauba's concept includes social structure, rights, welfare, ecology and political capability (✅ owner 23 §5, PDF pp.555–576).
**Breakdown:**
- Growth: output expansion (⚠️ owner 23 adv §4).
- Development: social, political, ecological dimensions (✅ owner 23 §5).
- Growth can coexist with inequality and ecological depletion (⚠️ owner 23 adv §4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.555–576.
**Links:** PT-5070, PT-5074–PT-5078, PT-5079.
**Trap:** "Development is just GDP" is wrong (✅ owner 23 §9).
**Mains hook:** Growth is one dimension; development concerns the quality and direction of collective outcomes (⚠️ owner 23 §11).
**Back:** *"Growth counts output; development counts people."*

### Flashcard PT-5070 — Trap: progress is normative, not neutral
**Front:** How does "progress" differ from "development" and "change"?
**Definition (learn):** Progress is change judged morally superior, not merely empirically different (✅ owner 23 §3, PDF p.541).
**Breakdown:**
- Development: empirical effectiveness or capacity (✅ owner 23 §5).
- Progress: moral judgment that the new condition is better (✅ owner 23 §5).
- Modernisation language: qualify because Gauba treats it as one strand (⚠️ owner 23 §11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.541.
**Links:** PT-5068, PT-5069, PT-5073.
**Trap:** "Progress is neutral" is wrong (✅ owner 23 §9).
**Mains hook:** Name the normative standard when using progress (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Progress says better; development says stronger."*

### Flashcard PT-5071 — Marxist revolutionary change: dialectical and historical materialism
**Front:** How does Marxism explain major social transformation?
**Definition (learn):** Contradictions within modes of production produce class struggle and epochal transformation; change is grounded in dialectical and historical materialism (✅ owner 23 adv §3, PDF pp.542–550).
**Breakdown:**
- Motor: class contradiction in the mode of production (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
- Revolution as structural break (✅ owner 23 §4).
- Lenin's sharpening and state transition (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.542–550.
**Links:** PT-5060, PT-5061, PT-5072, PT-5073.
**Trap:** Marx's historical materialism is not the same as Popper's rejected historicism; the contrast is central (✅ owner 23 adv §3).
**Mains hook:** Compare revolution's depth with its costs (⚠️ owner 23 adv §6).
**Back:** *"Marx: contradiction in production drives epochal change."*

### Flashcard PT-5072 — Popper: piecemeal social engineering (1957 book)
**Front:** What is Popper's piecemeal social engineering and where does it appear?
**Definition (learn):** Modest, reversible adjustments that can be tested and corrected, instead of total redesign (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF pp.551–554; ✅ Wikipedia: The Poverty of Historicism).
**Breakdown:**
- Rejects historicism and holistic social engineering (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
- Methodological individualism (✅ owner 23 adv §3).
- Publication: read as paper 1936; Economica articles 1944–1945; book 1957 (✅ Wikipedia: The Poverty of Historicism).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1957 book (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5071, PT-5073, PT-5068.
**Trap:** Popper is not against social change; he rejects total redesign (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
**Mains hook:** Piecemeal reform is reversible but may leave structural injustice intact (⚠️ owner 23 adv §6).
**Back:** *"Popper: fix one evil, test, correct."*

### Flashcard PT-5073 — Objection-reply: revolution vs Popperian reform
**Front:** Give the objection to revolutionary historicism and the reply.
**Definition (learn):** Objection: comprehensive historical scripts can become authoritarian pseudo-science insulated from falsification (✅ owner 23 adv §7, PDF pp.551–553). Reply: revolutionary change promises structural emancipation, but its costs of violence and new domination must be weighed (✅ owner 23 adv §6).
**Breakdown:**
- Popper: falsifiability and reversibility (✅ owner 23 adv §5).
- Marx: structural depth (✅ owner 23 adv §6).
- Residual: reform may leave injustice untouched (✅ owner 23 adv §6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.551–553.
**Links:** PT-5071, PT-5072, PT-5070.
**Trap:** Popper is not an anti-change conservative (⚠️ synthesis).
**Mains hook:** Compare Marxist revolutionary change with Popperian reform; answer with the trade-off between depth and reversibility (⚠️ owner 23 §10 prompt 3).
**Back:** *"Depth versus reversibility: revolution bites, reform checks."*

### Flashcard PT-5074 — Development path: market
**Front:** What does the market path of development promise and risk?
**Definition (learn):** Modernisation through growth, industrialisation, competition and open economy (✅ owner 23 adv §8C, PDF pp.562–563).
**Breakdown:**
- Promise: productivity, mobility, merit (✅ owner 23 §8B).
- Civil-political liberty plus opportunity (✅ owner 23 adv §8C).
- Risk: profit logic corrodes solidarity and public purpose (✅ owner 23 adv §7, PDF p.563).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.562–563.
**Links:** PT-5021, PT-5075, PT-5069.
**Trap:** Market development is not market democracy (✅ owner 23 adv §12).
**Mains hook:** Growth-led development can deepen inequality (⚠️ owner 23 adv §9).
**Back:** *"Market: growth and mobility, risk of corroded solidarity."*

### Flashcard PT-5075 — Development path: welfare
**Front:** What does the welfare path promise and what is its warning?
**Definition (learn):** Social security and redistribution within a market society (✅ owner 23 adv §8C, PDF pp.563–565).
**Breakdown:**
- Promise: softens exclusion and provides services (✅ owner 23 §8B).
- Warning: fiscal strain, inefficiency and corruption in weak states (✅ owner 23 adv §8C, PDF p.565).
- Rights plus social-security floor (✅ owner 23 adv §8C).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.563–565.
**Links:** PT-5074, PT-5076, PT-5069.
**Trap:** Welfare is not socialism; it works within market economy (✅ owner 23 adv §8C).
**Mains hook:** Welfare reaches redistribution, but outcomes depend on state capacity (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Welfare: a floor under the market."*

### Flashcard PT-5076 — Development path: socialist
**Front:** What does the socialist development path prioritise and what does it cost?
**Definition (learn):** Social ownership, worker welfare and planned development (✅ owner 23 adv §8C, PDF pp.565–566).
**Breakdown:**
- Priority: social-economic rights and employment (✅ owner 23 §8B).
- Cost: civil-political liberties may be compressed (✅ owner 23 adv §8C, PDF p.565).
- Owner's Indian anchor: mixed economy blends welfare and socialist paths (⚠️ owner 23 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.565–566.
**Links:** PT-5057, PT-5060, PT-5075, PT-5061.
**Trap:** Socialist development is not automatically democratic in liberties (✅ owner 23 adv §7).
**Mains hook:** Economic rights and political liberties must be jointly tested (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Socialist: planned welfare, liberties at risk."*

### Flashcard PT-5077 — Development path: Gandhian
**Front:** What does the Gandhian development path stand for?
**Definition (learn):** Moral self-restraint, dignity of labour, need-based living and decentralist ethics; resists consumerism (✅ owner 23 adv §8C, PDF pp.566–569).
**Breakdown:**
- Owner: Gandhi refuses consumerist development; values bread labour, anti-concentration and moral regeneration (✅ owner 23 adv §12).
- Trusteeship and anti-greed (✅ owner 23 §8B; adv §8C).
- Hard to map mechanically onto large industrial society (⚠️ owner 23 adv §8C).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF pp.566–569.
**Links:** PT-5094 (stewardship), PT-5078 (sustainable), PT-5076, PT-5069.
**Trap:** Gandhi is not simply anti-development; he offers a morally qualified model (✅ owner 23 adv §12).
**Mains hook:** Gandhian restraint is a corrective to growth fetishism; scale is the open question (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Gandhi: need, labour, restraint — development with conscience."*

### Flashcard PT-5078 — Sustainable development: present needs without harming the future
**Front:** Define sustainable development and attribute the Brundtland formulation.
**Definition (learn):** Meet present needs without compromising future generations' ability to meet theirs (✅ Wikipedia: Our Common Future, paraphrased; ✅ owner 23 adv §22.3, paraphrase rule).
**Breakdown:**
- Report: *Our Common Future*, 1987; commission inaugural 1984 (✅ Wikipedia: Our Common Future).
- Owner: sustainable model in development (✅ owner 23 §8B, PDF pp.568–572).
- Caution: development cannot mean limitless extraction (✅ owner 23 adv §8C).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1987; 1984 commission (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5079, PT-5094, PT-5093, PT-5077.
**Trap:** Do not quote the Brundtland definition verbatim; paraphrase (✅ owner 23 adv §19).
**Mains hook:** Sustainable development is a development model; ecological political theory is a different axis (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22 scope note).
**Back:** *"Brundtland 1987: today's needs, tomorrow's capacity."*

### Flashcard PT-5079 — Limits to Growth (1972): a contested debate
**Front:** What is *The Limits to Growth* and how should it be cited?
**Definition (learn):** A 1972 study commissioned by the Club of Rome, by Donella Meadows, Dennis Meadows, Jørgen Randers and William Behrens, modelling resource, population and pollution trends (✅ Wikipedia: Limits to Growth).
**Breakdown:**
- Year and commissioner verified (✅ Wikipedia: Limits to Growth).
- Received severe criticism; later comparison found tracking of its business-as-usual scenario (✅ Wikipedia: Limits to Growth).
- Owner: cite as opening of debate, not verified prediction (✅ owner 23 adv §22.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1972 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5080, PT-5092, PT-5078.
**Trap:** Do not treat projections as settled prediction (✅ owner 23 adv §22.2).
**Mains hook:** The decoupling debate remains empirically open (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.2).
**Back:** *"1972: the debate that opened the growth limit."*

### Flashcard PT-5080 — Coleman and Almond: political development as modernisation
**Front:** How do Coleman and Almond conceptualise political development?
**Definition (learn):** Political development involves differentiation of specialised structures, secularisation of political culture and expanded system capacity to manage change (✅ owner 23 §14; ✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF pp.572–575).
**Breakdown:**
- Work: *The Politics of the Developing Areas* (1960) (⚠️ owner 23 §14; not verified).
- Differentiation, secularisation, capacity (✅ owner 23 §14).
- Modernisation-based (⚠️ owner 23 adv §5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1960 (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-5081, PT-5082, PT-5083.
**Trap:** The Western-model assumption is criticised (⚠️ owner 23 §14).
**Mains hook:** Use with the teleology critique to show range (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Coleman: differentiate, secularise, enlarge capacity."*

### Flashcard PT-5081 — Pye: crises of political development
**Front:** List Pye's six crises of political development.
**Definition (learn):** New states must resolve identity, legitimacy, penetration, participation, distribution and integration crises; sequence and manner shape the path (✅ owner 23 §14; work attributed to *Crises and Sequences in Political Development* (1971) ⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Identity; legitimacy; penetration; participation; distribution; integration (✅ owner 23 §14).
- Sequence matters (✅ owner 23 §14).
- Association with Princeton/SSRC Committee volume (⚠️ owner 23 §14).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Six crises (✅ owner 23 §14).
**Links:** PT-5080, PT-5082, PT-5083, PT-5085.
**Trap:** Crises are not stages to be completed; the owner says implicit linear-stage assumption is a weakness (✅ owner 23 §17 chain 3).
**Mains hook:** Use Pye's list for nation-building; the Indian linguistic reorganisation can be used cautiously as illustration (⚠️ owner 23 §16).
**Back:** *"Identity, legitimacy, penetration, participation, distribution, integration."*

### Flashcard PT-5082 — Almond and Powell: structural-functional developmental approach (1966)
**Front:** What are the system, process and output functions in Almond and Powell's approach?
**Definition (learn):** Every political system performs system functions (socialisation, recruitment, communication), process functions (interest articulation and aggregation, policy-making, rule application, adjudication) and output functions (extraction, regulation, distribution, symbolic response); development means differentiation, autonomy and secularisation (✅ owner 23 §14).
**Breakdown:**
- Work: *Comparative Politics: A Developmental Approach* (1966) (⚠️ owner 23 §14).
- Differentiation and subsystem autonomy (✅ owner 23 §14).
- Descriptive, not prescriptive (✅ owner 23 §17 chain 3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1966 (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-5080, PT-5081, PT-5083.
**Trap:** Functional list is not a claim that one liberal endpoint is mandatory (✅ owner 23 §17).
**Mains hook:** Use the functional list to structure any "political development" answer (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
**Back:** *"Socialise, aggregate, extract: the functions every system performs."*

### Flashcard PT-5083 — Modernisation-teleology critique and dependency alternatives
**Front:** What is the teleology critique and what alternative do dependency theorists offer?
**Definition (learn):** Critics say developmental approaches treat Western liberal-democratic society as the natural endpoint and read traditional societies as lagging (⚠️ owner 23 §14). Dependency: underdevelopment is partly produced by a core–periphery relation (⚠️ owner 23 §16; Frank and Wallerstein, unverified).
**Breakdown:**
- Teleology: single linear path (⚠️ owner 23 §14).
- Non-converging modernities: single-party or hybrid regimes (⚠️ owner 23 §14).
- Dependency: structural, historically colonial relations (⚠️ owner 23 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Frank "development of underdevelopment"; Wallerstein world-systems (⚠️ owner 23 §16, unverified by fetch).
**Links:** PT-5082, PT-5080, PT-5062, PT-5094.
**Trap:** Dependency is not identical to Marxism; do not conflate the two (⚠️ synthesis).
**Mains hook:** Use teleology critique to challenge Pye's sequence; flag residual weakness (⚠️ owner 23 §17 chain 3).
**Back:** *"Not a waiting room for the West: a structure of dependence."*

### Flashcard PT-5084 — Kothari: Congress System and Politics in India (1970)
**Front:** What is Kothari's Congress-system thesis and his later critical turn?
**Definition (learn):** The Indian National Congress functioned as a consensual umbrella absorbing factions; opposition acted mainly as parties of pressure (⚠️ owner 23 §15, "The Congress System in India," *Asian Survey*, 1964 — unverified). Later critique of the centralised state and post-1967 erosion (⚠️ owner 23 §15).
**Breakdown:**
- Congress System: competition within Congress (⚠️ owner 23 §15).
- Politics in India, 1970 (✅ Wikipedia: Rajni Kothari).
- Later turn: critical of centralised developmental state (⚠️ owner 23 §15).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1964 journal title not verified (Wikipedia does not list it); 1970 verified (✅ Wikipedia: Rajni Kothari).
**Links:** PT-5085, PT-5081, PT-5086.
**Trap:** Keep the 1964 thesis and the later critical turn separate (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
**Mains hook:** Assess the Congress-system thesis by stating it, its nation-building reading and its later critique (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
**Back:** *"Kothari: Congress absorbed the contest; later he doubted the state."*

### Flashcard PT-5085 — Nation-building vs state-building (Kothari and owner 23)
**Front:** Why do nation-building and state-building need priority in development theory?
**Definition (learn):** They are not automatic by-products of economic modernisation; they require creating allegiance and administrative reach (✅ owner 23 adv §5, PDF p.575; ⚠️ owner 23 §9 imagined application).
**Breakdown:**
- Nation-building: emotional allegiance across diversity (⚠️ owner 23 adv §9).
- State-building: effective administrative capability (⚠️ owner 23 adv §9).
- Political development ties them together (✅ owner 23 adv §3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PDF p.575.
**Links:** PT-5080, PT-5081, PT-5084, PT-5086.
**Trap:** Do not treat nation-building as cultural uniformity; the owner frames it as accommodation of diversity (⚠️ owner 23 §15).
**Mains hook:** End with political development and state-building in diverse polities (⚠️ owner 23 §11).
**Back:** *"Nation for allegiance, state for capacity."*

### Flashcard PT-5086 — NITI Aayog (2015): dated institutional anchor
**Front:** When did NITI Aayog replace the Planning Commission and how should it be used?
**Definition (learn):** The Planning Commission was replaced by NITI Aayog with effect from 1 January 2015 (📰 owner 23 §16, dated institutional shift).
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 1 January 2015 (📰 owner 23 §16).
- Owner reading: move toward indicative planning (⚠️ owner 23 §16).
- Use only as general illustrative anchor (⚠️ owner 23 §16).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2015 (📰; not fetched).
**Links:** PT-5076, PT-5085, PT-5084.
**Trap:** Do not present NITI Aayog's current policy as an owner fact; detail belongs to Economy/Governance (⚠️ owner 23 §16).
**Mains hook:** A planning shift is evidence of development paradigm change, qualified by date (⚠️).
**Back:** *"2015: planning gives way to indicative guidance (as owner notes)."*

### Flashcard PT-5087 — Populism: thin-centred ideology (Mudde, 2004)
**Front:** What is populism's thin-centred definition and who formulated it?
**Definition (learn):** A thin ideology whose core is the people–elite antagonism and primacy of popular will, attaching to a fuller host ideology (⚠️ owner 23 adv §21.1; Cas Mudde, "The Populist Zeitgeist," *Government and Opposition* (2004)).
**Breakdown:**
- Moral binary: pure people vs corrupt elite (⚠️ owner 23 §21.1).
- Homogenisation and anti-institutionalism (⚠️ owner 23 §21.1).
- Left and right variants (⚠️ owner 23 §21.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2004 (⚠️ owner 23 §19; not fetched).
**Links:** PT-5088, PT-5089, PT-5090, PT-5098.
**Trap:** Populism is not a synonym for the right or authoritarianism (⚠️ owner 23 §21.2 and §21.5 caution).
**Mains hook:** Thin core explains the left/right variance (⚠️ owner 23 §21.1).
**Back:** *"Thin core, host ideas vary."*

### Flashcard PT-5088 — Laclau: populism as a logic of articulation (2005)
**Front:** How does Laclau's account differ from the thin-centred ideology view?
**Definition (learn):** Populism is a logic of political articulation: "the people" is constructed by linking unmet demands against a common antagonist (⚠️ owner 23 adv §21.1; *On Populist Reason* (2005)).
**Breakdown:**
- Ideology view (Mudde) vs logic view (Laclau) (⚠️ owner 23 §21.1).
- Shows the concept's contested status (⚠️ owner 23 §21.1).
- Not a pre-existing group (⚠️ owner 23 §21.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2005 (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-5087, PT-5089, PT-5090.
**Trap:** Do not present Laclau as a pre-given ideology; paraphrase only (⚠️ owner 23 §19).
**Mains hook:** Naming both accounts shows the concept is contested (⚠️ owner 23 §21.1).
**Back:** *"Laclau: the people is built, not found."*

### Flashcard PT-5089 — Müller: exclusive representation and anti-pluralism (2016)
**Front:** What is Müller's distinguishing claim about populism?
**Definition (learn):** Populism's feature is a moral claim to exclusive representation: the movement alone speaks for the real people, so opponents are illegitimate (⚠️ owner 23 adv §21.5; *What Is Populism?* (2016)).
**Breakdown:**
- Anti-elitism alone is not the distinction (⚠️ owner 23 §21.5).
- Four steps: opposition delegitimised, minorities excluded, counter-majoritarian institutions attacked, institutional hollowing (⚠️ owner 23 §21.5).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2016 (⚠️).
**Links:** PT-5087, PT-5090, PT-5018, PT-5041.
**Trap:** Do not quote Müller (⚠️ owner 23 §19).
**Mains hook:** Test whether opposition is treated as legitimate alternative (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Müller: only we are the people — so opposition is illegitimate."*

### Flashcard PT-5090 — Populism vs popular democracy (discriminator)
**Front:** What separates populism from popular or participatory democracy?
**Definition (learn):** Popular democracy multiplies channels through which a plural people governs; populism collapses them into one channel because the people is treated as singular (⚠️ owner 23 adv §21.6).
**Breakdown:**
- Plural vs singular people (⚠️ owner 23 §21.6).
- Disagreement: legitimate vs evidence of manipulation (⚠️).
- Opposition: alternative government vs illegitimate (⚠️).
- Deliberation is difficult under moral binary (⚠️ owner 23 adv §21.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner table §21.6.
**Links:** PT-5087, PT-5089, PT-5066, PT-5057.
**Trap:** "Populism = criticism of elites": the premise of a singular people is the real separator (⚠️ owner 23 adv §21.6).
**Mains hook:** Distinguish with the discriminator, closing on the plurality test (⚠️ owner 23 §21.9).
**Back:** *"Plural channels vs one channel for a singular people."*

### Flashcard PT-5091 — Ecological political theory: three orientations and Naess (1973)
**Front:** Name the three orientations toward the non-human world and the Naess distinction.
**Definition (learn):** Anthropocentric: nature matters for humans; biocentric: living things have value in themselves; ecocentric: ecological wholes are the primary unit of value (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.1). Naess's shallow vs deep ecology (1973) (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.1).
**Breakdown:**
- Anthropocentric: instrumental value; intergenerational conservation (⚠️).
- Biocentric: constraints beyond aggregate benefit (⚠️).
- Ecocentric: systemic integrity; eco-authoritarian risk enters here (⚠️).
- Shallow: pollution for human health; deep: intrinsic value (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Naess 1973 (⚠️; not fetched).
**Links:** PT-5092, PT-5093, PT-5096.
**Trap:** Ecological political theory is not the sustainable-development model (⚠️ owner 23 §10 and adv §22 scope).
**Mains hook:** Place the question on the grid first (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.1).
**Back:** *"Human, living, whole: the three moral circles."*

### Flashcard PT-5092 — Green critique of productivism
**Front:** What is productivism and the green argument against it?
**Definition (learn):** Productivism is the assumption that expanding material production is the master goal of political economy; greens argue it is shared across market, welfare and socialist models (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.2).
**Breakdown:**
- Premise 1: throughput cannot expand indefinitely in a finite system (⚠️).
- Premise 2: growth delivers employment, revenue and peace (⚠️).
- Conclusion: the ecological question is political (⚠️).
- Reply: relative vs absolute decoupling dispute (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Limits to Growth as opening of debate (✅ Wikipedia: Limits to Growth).
**Links:** PT-5079, PT-5091, PT-5093.
**Trap:** Do not present Limits to Growth as verified prediction (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.2).
**Mains hook:** Absolute decoupling would answer the critique; whether it occurs is contested (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Growth must decouple absolutely, or the limit bites."*

### Flashcard PT-5093 — Intergenerational justice and the three problems
**Front:** What are the three problems in intergenerational justice?
**Definition (learn):** Present decisions that foreclose future options require justification to those who cannot participate (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.3).
**Breakdown:**
- Representation problem: no standing for future generations (⚠️).
- Discounting problem: standard appraisal favours present consumption (⚠️).
- Non-identity problem: future people's identity depends on present choices (⚠️).
- Brundtland paraphrase: meet present needs without compromising future ability (✅ Wikipedia: Our Common Future).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Brundtland 1987 (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5078, PT-5094, PT-5092.
**Trap:** Do not present the non-identity problem as a decisive refutation (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.3).
**Mains hook:** Rawls's veil of ignorance can cover generation (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.3).
**Back:** *"Who speaks for the unborn, at what discount, and who exists?"*

### Flashcard PT-5094 — Stewardship and Gandhian trusteeship parallel
**Front:** What is ecological stewardship and why is it India-usable?
**Definition (learn):** Present holders of land and resources are trustees: entitled to use, obliged to conserve, answerable for what they pass on (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.4).
**Breakdown:**
- Parallel: Gandhian trusteeship across society (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.4, citing owner 05 §12.4 and PDF p.81).
- Extension: across time (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.4).
- Common-good cross-link (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner citation PDF p.81 (✅ owner 23 adv §22.4 as internal cross-reference).
**Links:** PT-5077, PT-5078, PT-5093.
**Trap:** Present as a conceptual parallel, not a claim that Gandhi advanced ecological doctrine (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.4).
**Mains hook:** Stewardship gives the common good determinate content (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Trustee today, steward for tomorrow."*

### Flashcard PT-5095 — Environmental justice: environmentalism of the poor
**Front:** What are the three mechanisms of environmental injustice and the Guha–Martinez-Alier thesis?
**Definition (learn):** Environmental harms and benefits are distributed along power lines; mechanisms are siting and exposure, displacement, and voice (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.5). Thesis: environmentalism of the poor, by Ramachandra Guha and Joan Martinez-Alier, *Varieties of Environmentalism* (1997) (⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Siting and exposure (⚠️).
- Displacement by extraction (⚠️).
- Voice: exposed people least represented (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1997 (⚠️; not fetched).
**Links:** PT-5091, PT-5092, PT-5098.
**Trap:** Do not attach Indian cases or figures to the thesis from this file (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.5).
**Mains hook:** Ecology is a justice and recognition question, not only scarcity (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Pollution follows power; voice follows wealth."*

### Flashcard PT-5096 — Eco-authoritarian risk: Hardin (1968) and survivalism
**Front:** What is the eco-authoritarian argument and why must it be stated fairly?
**Definition (learn):** If ecological limits are hard and democracies discount distant costs, democracy may fail to act in time, so coercive centralised direction may seem necessary (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.6). Hardin, "The Tragedy of the Commons" (1968) proposed mutually agreed coercion; survivalist writers (Ophuls, Heilbroner) drew authoritarian conclusions (⚠️).
**Breakdown:**
- Premise: harms severe and irreversible (⚠️).
- Emergency reasoning generalises (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.6).
- Most green theory rejects the conclusion (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.6).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Hardin 1968 (⚠️; not fetched).
**Links:** PT-5097, PT-5091, PT-5014 (liberties).
**Trap:** Do not present eco-authoritarianism as the green position (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.6).
**Mains hook:** Reply with Ostrom's empirical rebuttal and legitimacy (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.7).
**Back:** *"Emergency can become an excuse for coercion."*

### Flashcard PT-5097 — Ostrom: Governing the Commons (1990) and eight principles
**Front:** What did Ostrom show against Hardin, and how many design principles did she identify?
**Definition (learn):** User communities can build monitoring, graduated sanctions and dispute resolution to govern common-pool resources without privatisation or central coercion (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.7).
**Breakdown:**
- Book: *Governing the Commons: The Evolution of Institutions for Collective Action*, 1990 (✅ Wikipedia: Elinor Ostrom).
- Eight design principles for long-enduring CPR institutions (✅ Wikipedia: Elinor Ostrom).
- Limit: conditions do not obviously scale to global commons such as the atmosphere (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.8 E3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1990; eight principles (✅ Wikipedia).
**Links:** PT-5096, PT-5091, PT-5095.
**Trap:** Ostrom's principles are not a general proof for all commons (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.8).
**Mains hook:** Ostrom defeats the empirical premise of eco-authoritarianism, then adds legitimacy (⚠️ owner 23 adv §22.7).
**Back:** *"Ostrom 1990: communities can govern commons without the Leviathan."*

### Flashcard PT-5098 — Digital democracy: consent, representation and the public sphere
**Front:** What does digital mediation do to consent, representation and the public sphere (democratic-theory layer)?
**Definition (learn):** Platform politics can alter the informational basis of consent without changing the vote; disintermediation weakens parties and journalism; the public sphere faces ownership, attention, fragmentation and loss of common agenda pressures (⚠️ owner 23 adv §23.1–23.3).
**Breakdown:**
- Consent: differential information, rapid false claims, thin consent to terms (⚠️ owner 23 adv §23.1).
- Representation: disintermediation and fragmented address (⚠️ owner 23 adv §23.2).
- Public sphere: private ownership, attention economy, filter bubble as contested hypothesis, loss of common agenda (⚠️ owner 23 adv §23.3).
- Countervailing: lower cost of organising (⚠️ owner 23 adv §23.2).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Habermas 1962/1989 (✅ Wikipedia). Power-theory detail belongs to `Political-Theory/basic/15_Power-Authority-and-Legitimacy.md` §14 (✅ owner 23 adv §23).
**Links:** PT-5064, PT-5087, PT-5095, PT-5099, PT-5100.
**Trap:** Filter-bubble effects are a contested hypothesis, not established (⚠️ owner 23 adv §23.3).
**Mains hook:** Graded verdict: strengthened participation vs weakened deliberation (⚠️ owner 23 adv §23.5).
**Back:** *"Digital: wider voice, thinner consent, shakier common ground."*

### Flashcard PT-5099 — Democratisation and democratic backsliding
**Front:** Distinguish democratisation from democratic backsliding.
**Definition (learn):** Democratisation is the transition toward competitive, inclusive, accountable rule; backsliding is incremental weakening of competition, accountability, liberties or checks even while elections continue (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.1–24.2).
**Breakdown:**
- Democratisation map: liberalisation → participation and contestation → institutionalisation → consolidation (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.1).
- Backsliding: cumulative, formally enacted tilts (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.2).
- Elections necessary but insufficient (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.1).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No country labels (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.2).
**Links:** PT-5007, PT-5041, PT-5065, PT-5100.
**Trap:** Looking only for abrupt coups; backsliding is gradual (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.2).
**Mains hook:** Diagnose the function affected rather than attach a current label (⚠️).
**Back:** *"Backsliding: elections continue, checks quietly thin."*

### Flashcard PT-5100 — Social movements and democracy
**Front:** When do social movements deepen or damage democracy?
**Definition (learn):** Sustained collective claims outside routine electoral channels; they deepen democracy when they expand voice, reason-giving and non-violent accountability, and damage it when coercion, disinformation or exclusive claims replace justification (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.3).
**Breakdown:**
- Movements convert dispersed grievance into organised demand (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.3).
- Not automatically parties; electoral success is not the only measure (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.3).
- Counter-publics for excluded groups (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.3).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 adv §24.4 comparative table.
**Links:** PT-5059, PT-5062, PT-5099, PT-5098.
**Trap:** Movements are not automatically democratic; test the means (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.3).
**Mains hook:** Test whether mobilisation enlarges equal public agency and reasoned contestation (⚠️ owner 23 adv §24.4).
**Back:** *"Movements widen voice; test their means."*

### Flashcard PT-5101 — Answer architecture for contemporary democracy, change and development
**Front:** How do you structure a 20-mark answer on contemporary democracy, social change or development?
**Definition (learn):** Name the democracy model first; separate change concepts; state development beyond growth; compare rival paths on a spectrum; end with political development and state-building (⚠️ owner 23 §11). 20 marks: thesis with caveat → multi-dimensional comparison (owner 23 §8) → three evidence units → two objection-reply chains → Indian application used sparingly → graded verdict (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
**Breakdown:**
- Directive decoding: "critically examine elite theory" requires Mosca/Michels/Pareto plus Sartori and participatory replies (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
- "Discuss political development" requires Almond/Powell plus teleology critique (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
- "Assess Kothari's Congress System thesis" requires separation of the 1964 thesis and later turn (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
- Ecology and populism answers use the module's own architecture (⚠️ owner 23 adv §21.9, §22.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 23 §11 and §18.
**Links:** PT-5045, PT-5053, PT-5066, PT-5073, PT-5081, PT-5084, PT-5090, PT-5095.
**Trap:** Answering a development question with GDP only; answering elite theory without a democratic reply (⚠️ owner 23 §12).
**Mains hook:** Graded verdict: residual tension between elite necessity and accountability, or between growth and development (⚠️ owner 23 §18).
**Back:** *"Model, change, development, reply, verdict."*
